- **feat: log simulator rewritten — sample logs for every feature and format, generated directly or live, headless CLI for Claude sessions (2026-09-27, person-requested: *"Der Log Simulator ist im Vergleich zum Feature Set der eigentlichen Anwendung stark veraltet. Bringe ihn auf einen Stand, der Logvarianten für alle Features erstellen kann. Er soll auch Logs direkt, also ohne Trailing erzeugen können (Anzahl Einträge oder ungefähre Größe der Log Datei einstellbar). Auch soll das Tool ohne UI ansteuerbar sein und in Claude Sessions von Claude verwendet werden können …"*)**. See `tools/log-sim/README.md` and `docs/testing-and-limitations.md` → "Sample data: the log simulator".
  - **Engine** `tools/log-sim/core.js` (new, UMD): seeded and time-zone independent — same seed + options = same bytes in CLI and page. 14 scenarios, one per feature area (stack traces, 2D/3D position, move/reached Link pairs with same-thread keys and slow/aborted cases, durations/clock times, sensor values with spikes, JSON-array spectra, embedded XML/JSON/.NET dumps, paths, GUID/IP/hex/URL, error bursts, idle gaps, extra levels, Unicode/long lines); 7 formats (builtin default, custom columns, bracket, JSON Lines, RFC 5424 syslog with numeric levels, mixed default+syslog for Meta formats, plain text). Every non-builtin format ships its PhiLogg format-definition export, written next to the output.
  - **Direct generation**: entries or approximate size per file, several files (`rotate` = one timeline, `parallel` = a service per file with optional clock skew), gzip, ZIP (own stored-method writer), CRLF.
  - **CLI** `tools/log-sim/cli.js` (new, no dependencies): `--list` prints formats and scenarios with the features they feed; batch output to stdout/file/folder/zip; `--follow` live mode with rotation. ~6 s for 100 MB.
  - **Screenshots** `tools/log-sim/screenshot.js` (new): headless Chromium with `philogg.html`, generated files and their formats loaded, optional page JS and theme.
  - **Page** `tools/log-simulator.html`: now loads the engine; format/scenario/seed/rate/start controls, "Generate" (download or chosen folder, progress), "Format definition", and the previous live writing (per-write open/close + write queue kept), whose content now follows the chosen format and scenarios. Generating also works without the File System Access API.
  - **CLAUDE.md**: sample data comes from the simulator.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 300**.
  - **Full suite**: **6519 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: JSON Lines log formats with path-addressable keys, array columns in the extraction table, Heatmap + Profile plots (2026-09-27, person-requested, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #82)**. Research first (Loki, Splunk `spath`, Elasticsearch/Kibana, Seq, Datadog, jq/JMESPath): dot paths are the norm, `["a.b"]` escapes a dotted key, arrays come down to index / any-match / explode. See `PROJECT.md` → "JSON Lines formats", `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "JSON Lines kind", `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Pattern restricted to columns" / "Array columns" / "Heatmap and Profile".
  - **Format mode `json`**: each `{`-line is one entry; time/level/message from configurable keys (epoch s/ms/µs/ns or ISO 8601 or a tsFormat); custom columns by path — `ctx.req.id`, `tags[0]`, `["http.status"]`, a literal dotted key winning first (OpenTelemetry). Objects/arrays are kept as JSON text. Workers parse it too; the native desktop parser doesn't (falls back to JS).
  - **Format dialog**: a **JSON Lines** kind — detected keys with types and counts, Time/Level/Message selects, checkboxes for columns, a path field; JSON examples switch a new format to it automatically. Export/import carry the keys.
  - **Extraction**: a pattern restricted to columns now tabulates from those columns. **Array columns** (every value a JSON array): Joined / Per index / Aggregate / Explode, via a header toggle or the context menu, stored as `node.arrayViews` through every persistence carrier.
  - **Plot**: **Heatmap** (X × element index → color, bounded by pixel-sized bands) and **Profile** (one row's array via a slider, table-selected rows overlaid).
  - **Fix**: `#extractContextMenu`'s hidden items (Rename column outside a header) were never actually hidden — no CSS rule matched them.
  - **Backlog**: #82 reduced to its logfmt half.
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 297–299**.
  - **Full suite**: **6454 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: unified import/export — one "Import…", every element opens in its own editor; new theme editor (2026-09-26, person-requested)**. See `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "Central import", `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Theme editor" / "Log format export/import".
  - **One import**: Open → **"Import…"** (was "Import session…", now multi-file) and a file dropped/opened anywhere both go through `importPhiloggJsonText`, which dispatches on the JSON `format` marker: session → the existing matching flow; filter branch → attached under the **active node** with a toast naming it; filter library preset → the "Save to filter library" dialog prefilled; log format → the **format wizard** prefilled (plus a checkbox per filename pattern, already-mapped ones unchecked); theme / syntax scheme → the new theme editor prefilled. Saving always creates a new entry. Several files queue their editors (`queueImportEditor`). Other JSON keeps opening in the inline viewer.
  - **Removed** (no migration, < 1.0.0): Settings → Log Formats "Import…" + `#logFormatImportDialog`, "+ Import theme…", "+ Import scheme…", both "Download template…" buttons, the tree context menu's "Load filter…", `#filterFileInput`/`#sessionFileInput`/`#themeFileInput`/`#syntaxSchemeFileInput`/`#logFormatFileInput` (one `#importFileInput` now).
  - **Exports**: a per-entry **Export** button on custom themes, custom syntax schemes and filter library presets (new `philogg-filter-library` file: name, icon, roots, activeRef), next to the existing log-format one. All JSON saves share `saveJsonExportFile`.
  - **Theme editor** (`#themeEditorDialog`): "+ New theme…" (starts from the active colors) / **Edit** (incl. rename) on custom themes, and the same for syntax schemes (syntax colors only). Color groups on the left, each color with a swatch opening the highlight-colour picker's new **plain mode** (no Free/Theme toggle, no Clear) and a typed CSS value; a live preview on the right. The "-soft" variants derive from their base color ("Auto"), with a per-key override. A theme's own syntax colors stay optional ("Part of this theme").
  - **Filter library**: the Save dialog gained the icon pick.
  - **Tests**: GROUP 293 rewritten (import via the wizard); new **Groups 294–296**; GROUP 21/48a/60b/85b/169f/210c updated for the removed entry points.
  - **Full suite**: **6369 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: log format JSON export/import (2026-09-26, person-requested)**. Each ordinary format in Settings → Log Formats gets an **Export** button (JSON with the definition and the globs of its filename rules). Import via the new **Import…** button there, or by dropping/opening the file anywhere in the app — `loadFileDescriptors` recognises the `philogg-log-format` marker in small `.json` files and opens `#logFormatImportDialog` instead of loading them. The dialog suggests the exported name and lets the person pick which filename patterns become rules (globs another rule already owns start unchecked). An import always creates a new format. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Log format export/import (JSON)". Tests: new **GROUP 293**. Full suite: **6261 passed, 0 failed**. (The import side was reworked the same day by the unified import above — the Import… button and `#logFormatImportDialog` are gone, an import opens the format wizard.)

- **feat: sortable Δt column — "where is the most time lost" without a threshold (2026-09-26, follow-up to the Gap filter below)**. The Filtered view's Δt header is now sortable (first click largest first). Each row keeps its own chronological Δt under any column sort: `buildViewDeltaMap` captures it from the view list before sorting (only while a sort is active), and the row renderer reads it instead of the row above. Rows without a Δt sort last in both directions. On a gap node the map is the node's measured per-group gap, so `Gap ≥ 0 per Thread` + Δt sort ranks the longest per-thread pauses. `sortColumn: "delta"` is accepted by the session-cache/-import restore. Behavior change: Δt used to be dashed out under a column sort (GROUP 13 updated). Tests: new **GROUP 292**. Full suite: **6230 passed, 0 failed**. Docs: `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Column sort", `docs/filters.md` → "Gap filter", README.

- **feat: timing analysis for multi-process logs — Gap filter, link Δt condition, link correlation key (2026-09-26, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #24 + two link options)**. Motivation: in a log written by several processes/threads, the plain gap to the previous line is nearly meaningless (somebody always logs); timing only means something per thread, or between two specific messages that belong together. Current behavior: `docs/filters.md` → "Gap filter", "Correlation key", "Δt condition".
  - **Gap filter** (`filterType: "gap"`, `value: { ms, per }`): keeps entries of the parent's result whose distance to the previous entry is `>= ms`, optionally **measured per** a column (Thread, Location, Method, custom, or Source on a merge): one pass, last ts per group. New `#gapDialog` (value + ms/s/min + "measured per", the filter popup's live summary with each sample's gap), opened via **"Gap filter…"** in the tree context menu (a child of the right-clicked node), edited via Edit filter… (context menu, sidebar toolbar, Ctrl+E). The Filtered view's Δt column shows each row's **measured** gap on a gap node. No new node field: `value` carries everything. The optional Context-view "⏸ N s without entries" separator label was not done.
  - **Link Δt condition** (`linkDt: { op, ms }`): only tuples whose span (first → last real entry, new `pair.dtMs`/`tsMin`/`tsMax` from `buildPairEntry`) is `>`/`<` the limit. It's checked before a target is claimed under exclusive matches. The multi-hop dialog puts it on the last hop (total span). An extraction on link pairs gets a synthetic **Δt (ms)** column (`DT_COL = -3`) for sorting, stats, and plotting duration over time.
  - **Link correlation key** (`linkKey: { column } | { pattern }`): pair only entries with the same column value, or the same wildcard capture(s) such as `axis [*:int]`. Targets are indexed by key once per recompute, and each reference searches only its key's list with the unchanged occurrence search. So direction, N, the tie-break, exclusivity and order enforcement are all kept. The key applies to every hop, read off the entry each hop searches from.
  - **Link dialog**: the two new option rows (pill switch + inline controls, greyed out while off) and a **live preview** ("3.418 pairs" / "12 of 3.418 pairs", histogram, sample pairs with their Δt), evaluated as the would-be nested baked chain.
  - **Refactor**: the duplicated link pairing loops of `getEntriesUncached` and `getEntriesFromBaked` became one `computeLinkPairs`. The live result summary (`renderLiveResults`/`renderLiveMatchMinimap`) now takes a target container, and its CSS moved from `#filterResults…` ids to `.live-results`/`.live-match`/`.live-minimap`/`.live-samples` classes, so the new dialogs reuse it. `linkDt`/`linkKey` are threaded through every carrier via one `copyLinkOptions` (sanitizing hand-edited values).
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 289–291**. GROUP 122/263 check `#filterLiveMatch`'s state with `classList.contains` (the element now carries its base class).
  - **Full suite**: **6222 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: resizable, level-coloured Facet panel; Patterns moved to the first view tab (2026-09-26, person-requested)**. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Facet panel" / "View Selector".
  - **Facet panel width**: new `#facetResizer` grip on the panel's left edge drags its width (180px … view area − 240px), session-only like the other resizers. It sits inside `#facetPanel`, so it hides with the panel.
  - **Facet level colour**: each value's name is shown in the colour of the most severe level among its entries (follows the level quick-filter).
  - **Tab order**: `Patterns | Context | Filtered | Table | Plot` (Stacked: `Patterns | Stacked | Table | Plot`); `Ctrl+1` opens Patterns, `Ctrl+2`…`Ctrl+5` follow left to right.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 288** (288c: level colour); GROUP 72, 194d and 286b updated to the new positions.
  - **Full suite**: **6150 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: Patterns tab (message-pattern clustering) and Facet panel (value distribution per column) (2026-09-26, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #23 + #84)**. Two analysis surfaces over the active node's result as the Filtered view sees it (level quick-filter included). Neither filters anything itself: every action adds an ordinary filter node under the active node, one undo step each. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Patterns tab" / "Facet panel".
  - **Patterns tab** (`#fhTabs`, last: `… | Plot | Patterns`, `Ctrl+5`): the first line of each message is normalized by one regex pass (GUID, IPv4[:port], quoted string, Windows/UNC/Unix path, hex, number → placeholders) and identical shapes are grouped: Count, %, most severe Level, Pattern, First/Last, sortable (count ascending surfaces rare messages). Click → text filter on the message with every placeholder as `[*]`; Alt+click or ⊘ → the same as NOT, staying on Patterns; Extract → `[*:int]`/`[*:float]` per placeholder, opening the node's Table; → jumps to the group's first entry in Filtered. Virtualized like the extraction table.
  - **Facet panel** (`#facetPanel`, toggled by `#btnFacets` at the right end of `#viewBar` or `Ctrl+Shift+F`, remembered): one collapsible section per middle column (Thread/Location/Method/custom), Level, and Source for a merged file; top 8 values with count/%/bar, "+k more" in steps of 25. Click → exact whole-value filter (case-sensitive `^…$` regex restricted to that column; a `level` node for Level; an entry-set for Source); Alt+click or right-click → NOT. Panel and pattern-row clicks never take keyboard focus.
  - **Layout**: `#fhSplit`, `#extractWrap`, the new `#patternsWrap`, the empty state, inline viewer and folder minimap now sit in one row container, `#viewArea`, with the facet panel on its right. `isOffLogTab()` replaces the scattered `table || plot` checks where they meant "a tab that replaces `#fhSplit`".
  - **Scale**: both are one pass per result (`startEntryScan`: synchronous up to 50,000 entries, otherwise 12ms slices via `queueTask` with a progress line), cached per node result (`makeEntryAnalysis`: `WeakMap` keyed by the result array + its length + the level quick-filter); a tailed file's in-place append folds in only the new entries. A component benchmark (Node/V8, 1M synthetic messages) put normalization at ~1.5s total CPU, which is why it is sliced; not yet measured in the real app (`docs/performance-testing.md`).
  - **Undo**: new `"create"` undo kind (`pushCreateUndo`) for these one-click creations.
  - **Shortcuts**: new rebindable `viewTab5` (`Ctrl+5`) and `toggleFacets` (`Ctrl+Shift+F`).
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 286–287**; GROUP 72 (Stacked layout: `Ctrl+4` now reaches Patterns), 194 (`#btnFacets` after New in `#viewBar`) and 215 (18 `.icon-toggle` instances) updated.
  - **Full suite**: **6131 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: open gzip-compressed logs (`.gz`) (2026-09-25, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #83)**. A rotated log like `app.log.1.gz` now opens transparently on every route: drag-drop, the file picker, the desktop wrapper's native pick/drop and file-association/"Open in PhiLogg" launch, folder watch, ZIP entries, `?url=`, and the format dialog's example loader. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "gzip-compressed logs (`.gz`)" has the full mechanics.
  - **Detection and inflate**: gzip is detected by its magic bytes (`1f 8b`), not by extension. It's inflated with the native `DecompressionStream("gzip")`, the same Web API the ZIP path uses, so there's no dependency. The compressed input is streamed chunk by chunk, and the inflate drives the row's read-phase progress.
  - **Where it plugs in**: `loadOneFileIntoTree` (every file route) and `loadUrlIntoTree` inflate right before the text read, and so does the desktop session-cache "path" restore (`reopenLocalPathForRestore`).
  - **Native parsing (desktop)**: the Rust parser never sees gzip bytes as text. `parse_log_file` rejects a gzip file up front (`philogg_logparse::is_gzip`), before streaming anything, and every native caller already falls back to the JS read + parse, which inflates. So there is no Rust-side decompression and no new crate.
  - **Range reads**: the tail poll's one-time gzip check is a 2-byte read through `getPartialReadFile`. The minimap probe returns "no range" for gzip, and the windowed load falls back to a full load because it has no range.
  - **Names**: the node keeps its real name. `.gz`, plus a logrotate counter behind it, is looked through for format rules and folder-watch patterns (`fileNameGlobTest`) and for "is this a log?" (`isCompatibleFolderFile`, which also drives ZIP entry classification). `app.log.1.gz` counts as a log; `data.gz`/`.tar.gz` don't.
  - **Static only**: a gzip file is never tailed. No load or "path" restore attaches a tail, and `tailTick` drops one that a session restore or folder rescan reattaches.
  - **Picker filters**: `.gz` added to the `<input>` accept lists and the desktop `pick_files` filter.
  - **Desktop**: `.gz` launch arguments route like `.log`, the native folder listing is asked for `.gz`, and an opt-in `.gz` Explorer verb was added. `.gz` is deliberately not a `fileAssociations` entry. `docs/desktop.md` is updated.
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 284–285** (285d/e: the desktop native route with a stub that rejects gzip the way `parse_log_file` does, and the desktop "path" restore); crate unit test `gzip_magic_is_detected`. GROUP 272's tail-poll request count now includes the one-time 2-byte gzip check on a hand-attached (reattach-style) tail; load paths mark their own fresh tails as already checked, so a normal poll stays at two requests.
  - **Full suite**: **6056 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: desktop splash screen removed (this session, 2026-09-25)**. Startup is fast enough that the splash (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #51's "no feedback during startup" half) no longer earns its place. Gone: `windows::create_splash`/`dismiss_splash`, the `SPLASH` window label, `protocol.rs`'s generated `app/splash.html` page and `splash_url()`, and the `"splash"` entry in `capabilities/default.json`'s window list. The main window used to be built `visible(false)` and revealed by the splash dismissal; it is now created visible right away, on its existing dark native background colour so there is no white flash. `app_ready` (the injected script's first-paint signal) stays, since it still flushes a cold-launch `.zip`/folder open waiting in `pending_local_load`. `philogg.html` is unchanged (it never had a splash hook). This also retires the headless-Xvfb artifact where the hidden main window never appeared. Docs: `docs/desktop.md`, `desktop/README.md`, `PROJECT.md`.

- **feat: Export / Share — a ticket-oriented export: "Copy for ticket" snippet + full-view attachments (.log/.csv/.tsv/standalone HTML report)** (this session, 2026-09-25, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #31 + #32 combined into one concept, requested as "an export/report concept especially useful for communicating findings via ticket systems like Jira"). Concept and current behavior: `docs/export.md`.
  - **Entry point**: one toolbar button, `#btnExport` (left of Settings), plus the rebindable shortcut `Ctrl+Shift+E` (`exportView`), opening `#exportDialog`. Nothing loaded → a "Nothing to export" toast.
  - **Copy for ticket** (the dialog's primary action): source file(s), time range, "x of y entries matched", the filter chain as numbered human-readable steps with per-step counts (`describeExportStep`; the level quick-filter becomes its own last step), the root file's bookmarks and notes, and an excerpt of the first N matching raw lines. Bounded for ticket fields: N adjustable (default 20, 0–500), each entry ≤ 1,000 chars, ≤ 25 bookmark/note lines, total ≤ 30,000 chars (under Jira's 32,767 limit); every cut is announced in the text.
  - **Flavors**: Markdown (Jira Cloud, GitHub, GitLab, Azure DevOps), Jira wiki (Server/Data Center: `h3.`, `{{…}}`, `{noformat}`), plain text — one segmented switch with a live preview, remembered in `philogg-export-format` (excerpt lines in `philogg-export-excerpt-lines`). Free text is escaped per flavor; the Markdown fence grows past any backtick run in the quoted lines.
  - **Attachments** (the full current view = the active node's result + level quick-filter, log order): `.log` raw lines, `.csv` (RFC 4180, the visible log columns incl. custom ones), `.tsv` (tab → space, line break → `\n`), and a standalone HTML report (chain, all bookmarks/notes, every matching entry with level colors and ★/notes inline; inline CSS only, no script, everything escaped). Built as chunked string parts (5,000 entries each) for one `Blob` — no DOM collection. Saved via `showSaveFilePicker` where available (Chromium, the Windows desktop build), else a download, the same path as the other file saves; a cancelled picker saves nothing.
  - **Privacy**: nothing leaves the machine except the explicit clipboard write or file save; only file names, never local paths.
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 281–283**. GROUP 114's rebindable-shortcut row count now reads `SHORTCUT_ACTIONS.length` instead of a hardcoded 20.

- **feat: incremental find inside the current view — a find bar (`Ctrl+G`/`F3`) with "Add as filter" (2026-09-25, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #3)**. `Ctrl+F` always created a filter node, so "just look for this string once" cost a tree node you then deleted. The new `#findBar` searches first and promotes to a filter only on demand. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Find bar".
  - **Shortcuts**: `Ctrl+F` stays "New filter" (least disruptive: core idiom, empty-state hint). The find bar takes the browser's own find-next keys the app didn't use: `Ctrl+G` opens/focuses it (steps when already focused), `F3`/`Shift+F3` next/previous from anywhere, `Enter`/`Shift+Enter` in its input, `Ctrl+Enter` = Add as filter, `Esc` closes. The three chords are rebindable in Settings → Shortcuts.
  - **Search**: only the current view's rows (Context or Filtered; "Not in this view" on Table/Plot/Link), over the entry array rather than the DOM, with the text filter's own query language (literal, wildcard tokens, regex; Aa and .* toggles). Debounced typing jumps to the first hit; `n / m` counter; hits marked in every column (`mark.find-match-mark`) in both views and the Entry Detail.
  - **Scale**: time-sliced scan (12ms slices of 2048-entry batches), hit indices + binary-search stepping, rescan only when the view's list changes (array identity).
  - **Add as filter**: the same `createFilterNode` call the Ctrl+F popup makes, with the find bar's case/regex — no new node field.
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 279** (279a/b) and **280**.

- **fix: no console window from the release desktop app; "Select" actions use the selection checkmark (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested: *"change the icon of the "Selection" button to match the selection symbol in the filter tree"* and *"make sure, that when using "release please" the build is not a dev version that spawns a console"*)**.
  - Root cause of the console: the release exe already is a GUI-subsystem build (`windows_subsystem = "windows"` for every non-debug build), but `fonts.rs` spawned `powershell` for the system font list without `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, so Windows opened a console window for that child on every start. It now passes the flag, like `vs_integration.rs`. `release-please.yml`'s `build_desktop` also checks the built exe's PE subsystem field and fails the release if it is not 2 (Windows GUI).
  - The toolbar "Select" buttons and the context menu's "Add to selection" item draw the selection filter's tree checkmark (`ICON_CHECK`'s path) instead of the filter funnel.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 277**. Full suite: **5845 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat + fix: Context view — surroundings open on the tab switch, new "Expand around matches" button (this session, 2026-09-25, person-reported/requested: *"Beim Wechsel Filter -> Context sollte die Umgebung des Eintrag ausgeklappt sein … Es klappt erst aus wenn ich wieder einen Eintrag klicke"* and *"einen [Button] ergänzen, der mir die definierte Anzahl Einträge um jeden Treffer herum ausklappt"*)**.
  - Root cause: only clicks, the nav arrows and `revealInHighlightView` ran `applyContextJumpExpansion`; a plain tab switch (`showFhTab`) never did. It now runs it for the selection under `"aroundJump"` (`expandContextAroundSelection`); a stale view defers it to `renderHighlightView` after the gaps are rebuilt (`contextSelectionExpansionPending`). This also fixes `revealInHighlightView` expanding against stale gaps when the Context view was hidden during the last render.
  - New toolbar button `#ctxExpandAround` (`expandAroundAllMatches`): one expansion step above and below every match, entries or time mode (`contextStepEdge`, now shared with `applyContextJumpExpansion`).
  - **Tests**: new **Group 276**; Groups 138/151 clear the selection before switching where they need a still-shut view. Full suite: **5838 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: text and image files survive a reload / app restart like log files (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested: *"dass wenn man die Seite neu lädt … nicht nur Logdateien, sondern auch Bilder und Textdateien entsprechend wiederhergestellt werden. Am besten auf dem gleichen Pfad"*)**.
  - An opened loose or folder-watch viewer gets a `files` record (`persistViewer`, `"viewer-…"` key): its path on the desktop (re-read on restore, skipped if gone), else a Blob. `meta.viewers`/`meta.activeViewer` list the open ones with Pretty Print and the shown one; `restoreViewersFromCache` rebuilds them after the files and folders (from the meta read once at boot, `bootMeta`), and sweeps records the meta no longer lists. Closing a viewer deletes its record.
  - `openInlineViewer` is split into `buildInlineViewer` + `registerInlineViewer` (restore reuses both). `restoreSessionFromCache` takes the boot-time meta too, so both restores see what the previous session left.
  - **Bugfix found in the real browser**: a text version was never written to the cache at all (`addFile` persists nothing — only the file-loading path does), so it was listed in the meta but dropped on restore; `openInlineViewerAsTextLog` now calls `persistFileNode`.
  - A "Filter lines" text version's file record now carries `viewerSource`/`textSyntax`, so it nests under its restored viewer again — this removes the earlier "top-level after a reload" limitation (still the case for a ZIP entry: ZIPs aren't restored). `isNestedUnderViewer` also requires a folder viewer's entry to be listed (a restored folder waiting for permission has no rows yet).
  - **Tests**: new **Group 275**. Full suite: 5826 passed; the one failure is Group 266b's wall-clock scroll assertion ("rendered within 50ms"), which fails the same way on unchanged `main` whenever all 4 cores are busy — the new groups lengthen the other shards so they now overlap it. It passes alone (5/5), and a scroll render measures the same on both trees (8.5 ms).

- **fix: a nested text version's subtree had no tree connector lines (this session, 2026-09-25, person-reported with a screenshot comparing a log file's filters with a JSON text version's)**. `renderNode` only calls `decorateTreeGuides` for a depth-0 subtree, and a text version renders at depth 1 under its viewer row; `buildInlineViewerEntryRow` now decorates that subtree itself. **Tests**: Group 274c extended. Full suite: **5815 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: Ctrl+F on a text viewer filters it directly (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested: *"Strg+F auf einer Textdatei soll diese direkt in ein Textfilter File umwandeln und den Suchdialog öffnen"*)**. The `newFilter` shortcut, while a text viewer is shown, runs `openInlineViewerAsTextLog` (creating or re-activating its "Filter lines" text version — pretty-printed if Pretty Print is on) and then opens the filter popup on it. **Tests**: new Group 274d. Full suite: **5814 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: the Columns panel offered Δt for plain-text files (this session, 2026-09-25, person-reported: *"die Spaltenauswahl bietet mir in der Ansicht noch delta T an, das macht hier keinen Sinn"*)**. With only plain-text files loaded the Δt track is collapsed regardless (line numbers have no time delta), so `renderColumnsPanel` no longer lists it. **Tests**: Group 273c/e extended. Full suite: **5811 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: plain-text rows showed a grey block before every message, and a striped minimap (this session, 2026-09-25, person-reported with a screenshot: *"In den Messages ist noch irgendein Block Element das ich nicht zuordnen kann"*)**. The block was the empty `.level-badge` (padding + background) spilling out of the Level track collapsed to 0px; plain-text entries now render no badge at all (`levelCellHtml`/`levelBadgeHtml` — rows, pair rows, entry detail). The minimap's stripes came from more buckets than lines — consecutive line numbers left every other bucket empty; a plain-text file's minimap now has at most one bucket per line. **Tests**: Group 274b extended (fails on the previous code). Full suite: **5809 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: plain-text rows keep indentation and JSON/XML highlighting; a text version nests under its file (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested follow-up: *"die anführenden Leerzeichen Tabs, sowie das Syntax Highlighting in der Filter Darstellung zu behalten"* and *"hänge die Filterdarstellung von Dateien unter die jeweilige Datei im Files-Tree. Also Datei->PlainTextFilterVersion->Filter1"*)**.
  - A plain-text row's message cell is `.col-msg.plaintext` (`msgColClass`): `white-space:pre` (`pre-wrap` under word wrap), `tab-size:4`.
  - The viewer's tokenizers now produce ranges (`jsonTokenRanges`/`xmlTokenRanges`, rendered by `tokenRangesHtml`); `highlightJsonText`/`highlightXmlText` are thin wrappers, byte-identical for JSON. XML is now tokenized on the raw text, so an attribute value containing `&`, `<` or `>` is highlighted too (it wasn't before). `markCombinedHtml`/`markFieldHtml` take the ranges as an innermost layer next to filter and highlight-rule marks, so both row renderers highlight a JSON/XML text version's lines (`plainTextSyntaxOf`: `node.textSyntax`, falling back to the file name) without losing match marks.
  - "Filter lines" now tags the new file with its viewer (`node.viewerSource`), and the tree renders it one level below that viewer's row (ZIP, folder or directly opened), filters below it (`viewerTextLogIds`, `isNestedUnderViewer`, arrow-key order via `flattenTreeIds`). A second "Filter lines" for the same version re-activates it. Closing the viewer entry closes its text versions (undoable; restored ones go top-level while their viewer stays closed). `viewerSource`/`textSyntax` survive undo, but not a page reload: viewers aren't restored, so a restored text version shows top-level.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 274** (19 assertions). Full suite: **5807 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: filter text files line by line — "Plain text" format and the text viewer's "Filter lines" button (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested: *"wie sich das Konzept der Filter auch auf Textdateien übertragen lässt … Zeilen mit bestimmten Werten suchen können, inklusive Wildcards Tabellen und Plots"*, plus *"Wenn Dateien mit Pretty Print angezeigt werden, soll der Filter auf dieser Darstellung wirken"*)**.
  - New code-level builtin format `fmt-plaintext` (`PLAINTEXT_LOG_FORMAT`, `mode: "plaintext"`, resolved by `findLogFormat`, not stored in `state.logFormats`): every line is one entry, blank lines included, the message verbatim (no `stripQuotes`), no trailing empty entry for a final newline. Entries have no level (`""` → OTHER) and their `ts`/`tsRaw` are the **1-based line number** (`numberPlainTextEntry`, set when the entry is appended — main-thread loop, worker adoption, tail), so every order-based mechanism (sorting, OR re-sort, link, context windows, minimap buckets, time-range filters) works unchanged, in lines.
  - The text viewer's new funnel button `#itvFilterLinesBtn` (`openInlineViewerAsTextLog`) loads the **displayed** text (`inlineViewerDisplayedText`: pretty-printed JSON while Pretty Print is on, named `"<file> (pretty)"`) as a new root file under that format.
  - Display: `entryTimeText`/`formatTsFor` show line numbers wherever a time was shown (row, detail, pair row, context menu, nav tooltip, filter samples, minimap labels — "Lines N" instead of Start/End/Duration — and time-filter names, "line 2 → line 3"); no Δt for a plain-text row. With only plain-text files loaded (`allRootsPlainText`) the row grid titles the Time track "Line" (own width key `line`, 64px) and collapses Δt/Level; the level bar is empty; the filter chips are Line/Message. The extraction table's `t (ms)` slot becomes **Line** (absolute line number) and is the plot's default X.
  - Plain-text files can't be merged (their ts are line numbers). No native (Rust) parse: `nativeFormatSpec` returns null, the JS path runs.
  - **Bugfix found on the way**: `activeColumnDefs()` fell back to Thread/Location/Method whenever the loaded formats defined no middle columns at all; it now only does so with no file loaded.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 273** (36 assertions). Full suite: **5788 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: live tracking, folder watch, ZIP and viewer performance (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested: "Performance Review in Tracking, folder Watch (and settings), loading ZIP Files, Textiles and Images as well as partial or combined loading from folder Watch")**. Measured on the 650k-entry / 100 MB file (`docs/performance-testing.md` → "Level 2d", "Level 3b", new `tools/perf/chromium-tail-bench.js`, `desktop-tail-bench.sh`):
  - **Tail ticks re-ran every filter over the whole file.** A pure append now keeps a per-entry filter's (text, level, time range, id set, bookmarks, notes; inverted or not; whole chain per-entry) old result as `node._tailBase`; the next `getEntries()` tests only the new entries plus the previous last one (`extendCachedEntries`). Tailed files are polled concurrently. One tick with three filters: ~340 → ~57 ms (Chromium).
  - **Every tail tick re-fingerprinted the whole file** for its filter-history record (`buildFileHistoryRecords` → `getFileFullHash`, ~280 ms on 100 MB), because each tick renders and the history write is debounced from `render()`. A record is now only written when the file's filter tree changed (`_historySig`), which is what the docs already promised; the earlier record still matches the grown file via tier 2.
  - **A growing file's cache write rebuilt its whole text every 4 s.** A browser-tailed file's record is a Blob slice of a fresh handle snapshot at the tail offset (`tailSnapshotBlob`), text only as a fallback; restore resumes tailing at the Blob's exact size (`restoredTailOffset` — rebuilt text undercounted a CRLF file). Stall after the ticks: ~435 → ~35 ms.
  - **Desktop: every poll re-read the whole file.** `protocol.rs` serves `Range` requests (`read_local`/`parse_range`); tail polls, the folder minimap's head/tail probe and windowed loads read through `urlTailHandle.getRangedFile()`/`rangedUrlFile`. Idle poll of a 100 MB file: ~2,340 → ~2 ms; a poll with 1000 new lines: 2,348 → ~135 ms.
  - **Folder watch**: an in-flight minimap probe is shared instead of restarted by every render; the "Merge (full)/(window)" actions load their sources concurrently (`loadFolderMergeSources`, ids in target order) with one render per finished source instead of two; browser mtime reads run concurrently; `mergeScannedFiles`' removal check is a set lookup instead of O(n²); unopened folder and ZIP rows are reused across renders (also keeps a double-click working during a live tick) and open roots are looked up once per section.
  - **Viewers**: the text viewer rebuilds its markup only when the viewer or Pretty Print changed (unrelated renders reset folds and re-tokenized the file); the image viewer keeps its `<image>` element through pan/zoom (each mousemove re-parsed the data URL). **Bugfix**: the image's natural size was measured once for the first image ever opened and reused for all later ones — now per viewer.
  - Tried and dropped: a linear merge of sorted sources in `fillMergedEntries` — slower than the existing sort (TimSort already merges sorted runs linearly).
  - **Tests**: new **Group 272** (42 assertions); `cargo test` covers `parse_range`. Full suite: **5752 passed, 0 failed**.

- **License: PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 + commercial evaluation, third-party notices, LICENSE.md in every build (this session, 2026-09-25, person-requested: "free for private users, open source developments or non profit organisations … take a license fee for commercial usage"; hand test versions to colleagues)**. The German proprietary test-phase terms in Settings → License are replaced by `LICENSE.md`: the PolyForm Noncommercial License 1.0.0 plus an additional permission to use PhiLogg commercially for up to 30 days per person to evaluate a commercial license; any other commercial use needs a paid license. Settings → License: English summary, the full `LICENSE.md` text in a collapsed `<details>`, the street address removed (mail only), and a new "Third-party components" part (Catppuccin palettes, MIT, full text; the desktop build's Rust crates via `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). New `desktop/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (468 crates, generated by cargo-about from new `desktop/about.toml`/`about.hbs`, regenerated by both build workflows before bundling). `tauri.conf.json`: `bundle.license`/`licenseFile`/`copyright`, `LICENSE.md` + notices as resources (licenseFile also becomes the NSIS installer's license page); both Cargo.toml files get `license`/`publish = false`. Workflows: the HTML artifact/release asset gets `LICENSE.md` beside it, the portable zip both files. The `homepage/` feature tour was removed in the same session; its screenshots moved to `docs/screenshots/` for the README. **Tests**: new **Group 271** (in-app text equals `LICENSE.md`, also after comment stripping; English, no address; third-party credits; distribution wiring in `tauri.conf.json` and both workflows; notices vs `Cargo.lock`); Group 65's German-text assertions replaced.

- **fix: faster loading and filtering of large files (this session, 2026-09-24, person-requested: four measured proposals from the previous session)**. Measured in headless Chromium 141 (WebView2's engine family, new `tools/perf/chromium-load-bench.js`, `docs/performance-testing.md` → "Level 2c") on a 650k-entry / 100 MB file through `#fileInput`, median of 4: load 2.87 → 2.10 s, longest main-thread stall 460 → 225 ms, JS heap 357 → 326 MB, level filter (162k matches) create+render 530 → 100 ms; text filter unchanged (~190–220 ms). Desktop app (WebKitGTK/Xvfb, launch-argument route): JS path 7.3 → 3.8 s, native unchanged (2.7–3.1 s).
  - **Session cache off the load path.** `persistFileNode` used to rebuild the file's text and structured-clone it into IndexedDB before the load's render (0.5–1.3 s blocked). A record now holds the original `File` (a Blob — IndexedDB copies it off the JS thread) for a drop/dialog/folder load, **only the path** for a desktop file (re-read and re-parsed from disk on restore — the person chose this over storing text: a file deleted or moved since is not restored), and rebuilt text only for merges, meta-format streams, ZIP entries and tailed files, written when idle (`persistFileNodeWhenIdle`). `fileCacheSource`, `reopenLocalPathForRestore`, `pinFileFormat`, `newTailState`. No migration (0.x).
  - **One file parses on every core.** `splitTextAtHeaderLines` cuts the text before header lines into one piece per core; each worker sends binary batches (`encodeEntryBatch`, the native parser's `batch.rs` layout, read by the same `decodeNativeBatch`; the string section is posted as a JS string, which also keeps lone surrogates intact) instead of structured-cloned entry objects — the main thread's unpacking went from ~1.4 s to ~0.2 s. Pieces are adopted in file order with ids assigned there; a failed worker rolls back and the main-thread loop takes over. Output stays byte-identical (golden fixture, GROUP 264).
  - **Per-file aggregates built during adoption** (`updateFileAggregate`, `node._agg`): level counts per raw level + format, per-level entry indices, sortedness, ts range, the longest message/line. The first render reads them instead of full passes (`getLevelCounts`, minimap via `minimapBucketBounds` + per-level overlay, `updateMinimapFullRange`, `computeMaxMessageWidth`): 230 → 118 ms on the (unsorted) test file, 43 ms on a chronological one. Incremental on tail appends; invalidated by in-place reorders (`invalidateOrderIndexMap`, now also called for a meta-format stream's sort) and clock offsets; a format level edit needs nothing (bucketed when read).
  - **The parsed batches are adopted a few at a time** (`PARALLEL_PARSE_DRAIN_BATCHES`), yielding through `queueTask` — a `MessageChannel` message: a first version yielded with `setTimeout(0)` and the desktop JS path went from 3.8 to 9.2 s, because a hidden window's timers are throttled (Xvfb's unmapped window; a minimized or tray-hidden app is the same case).
  - **Context view only while visible, match/gap part memoized.** `renderMainView` rebuilt the Context view on every render even with only the Filtered tab on screen (~300 ms for the level filter above). Hidden, it is now marked stale (`contextViewStale`) and built when revealed; `contextMatchesAndGaps` memoizes the match set, gaps and match ordinals per active node + result + root entries, and finds the root indices with one walk instead of an id map where the result is a subsequence of the file. Re-render with the Context tab showing 222 → 98 ms.
  - **Tests**: new **Groups 267–270** (each fails on the code before its change); Groups 165b/c and 261 decode the worker's binary batches; Groups 55a/55d/61a/97a/137/202b/202c, which assert on both Log views at once, run in Stacked layout. The suite's `structuredClone` shim lets fake-indexeddb store jsdom Blobs; `withApp` takes a `beforeParse` hook. Full suite: **5688 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: log view scrolling — no more snapping content or blank rows (this session, 2026-09-24, person-reported: "Scrolling … feels a bit flickering and bigger jumps always lead to empty lines that need a moment to fill up")**. Measured in headless Chromium (WebView2's engine family) on a 650k-entry / 100 MB file with the new `tools/perf/chromium-scroll-bench.js`; three causes, all fixed:
  - **Scroll-height cap detection read Chromium's number format wrong.** `detectMaxTableScrollPx` compared `parseInt(getComputedStyle(probe).height)` with the requested height, but Chromium prints lengths of 1e6px or more in exponent form (`"2e+07px"`), so every probe failed and the cap fell back to 950,000px. Every Chromium/WebView2 view above ~34k rows was therefore scroll-compressed — 650k rows about 19x: one wheel notch moved 68 rows instead of 4, every scroll render snapped the content by ~170 rows, and the 10-row buffer shrank to ~15 physical px, so scrolling up showed blank rows. Now `parseFloat` with a 1e-5 relative tolerance; a 650k-row file is no longer compressed on Chromium (compression starts at ~1.1M rows).
  - **The 100ms scroll-render throttle** (a 2026-09-21 workaround for Firefox's End key stalling on a huge compressed merge) let the viewport scroll up to six frames past the rendered rows. Replaced by `makeScrollRenderer`: one render per animation frame, reading the live position, backing off only after an expensive render (> 12ms → the next waits 3x its cost).
  - **Every scroll render rebuilt all rows** (~5.5ms, 4.5ms of it style/layout). `renderVisibleRows(true)`, passed only by the scroll listener, keeps the rows still in the window and builds just the new ones (~0.8ms), with a one-screen overscan per side; a jump with no overlap falls back to the small full rebuild. All other callers still rebuild everything.
  - **Result** (same file, wheel scrolling, `chromium-scroll-bench.js`): content jumps while scrolling down 14 of 15 renders (up to 195 rows) → 0; blank rows while scrolling up 15/15 renders → 0/40; a 400px-per-frame flick 13/15 → 0/40; a scroll render 13.8 → 0.8 ms. Results table in `docs/performance-testing.md`. The remaining case is a scrollbar-thumb drag: each step is a jump beyond any buffer and shows about one frame of stale rows — inherent to native scrolling with virtualized rows. Not yet confirmed on Windows/WebView2 or in Firefox.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 266** (Chromium- and Firefox-style probe stand-ins, uncompressed 40k-row view; per-frame renders + backoff; row identity kept, window/order/top, markup identical to a full rebuild, no reuse across a list change or cleared rows; note rows stay paired) — 12 of its assertions fail on the old code. Group 258 updated (a burst collapses onto one render that already shows the final position; its "trailing catch-up render" assertions dropped). Full suite: **5556 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: loading a file renders once instead of twice; per-entry level work in every render memoized (this session, 2026-09-24, person-requested follow-up to the native-parsing measurements)**. Profiling a 650k-entry file showed every `render()` costing ~0.6 s even with warm caches, most of it the timeline minimap calling `levelBucket`+`levelSortRank` for every entry (each call re-resolves the format's level list), and every load paying two full renders (`loadOneFileIntoTree`'s own `flushLoadRender` plus its caller's `render()`).
  - `loadOneFileIntoTree` now only invalidates caches; callers render once, after the history match and tail setup. `loadFileDescriptors` renders per finished file (batches still show each file as it completes) and adds a final render only after a failure; `loadUrlIntoTree`'s native branch the same.
  - New `makeLevelBucketer()` (per-pass memo, never long-lived) in `getLevelCounts`, `applyLevelFilter`, both level-filter-node paths and the filter popup histogram; the minimap memoizes bucket+rank per (format, level).
  - Measured in the desktop app (WebKitGTK/Xvfb, 100 MB, 650k entries, launch-argument route, until fully rendered): native 4.6 s → ~3.3 s, JS path 12.1 s → 8.1 s. Warm re-render in jsdom 660 → 440 ms (the rest there is jsdom's own `querySelectorAll`).
  - **Tests**: new **Group 265** (exactly one render after the load on the drop, batch-with-failure, desktop drop and file-association routes — fails with 2 on the old code; memo equals `levelBucket` for text and int formats; levels resolved per pair, not per entry). Full suite: **5524 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: desktop native parsing — binary transport instead of JSON (this session, 2026-09-24, person-reported: a tester build loaded a 100 MB file in just under 5 s, same as the HTML build)**. Reproduced on Linux (WebKitGTK/Xvfb, 100 MB, 650k entries): Rust finished after 1.0 s, but ~310 MB of JSON through the IPC plus `JSON.parse` on the main thread ate the rest (8.2 s vs. 8.6 s JS path). Batches are now binary (`logparse/src/batch.rs`): all strings of a batch in one UTF-8 buffer decoded by a single `TextDecoder` call (`decodeNativeBatch`), fields as substrings located by varint offsets relative to `raw` (fields already inside `raw` aren't sent twice), `ts` as a `Float64Array`; 25,000 entries per batch. Same run: 4.3 s vs. 8.4 s; the rest is the page's first render, shared by both paths. Windows re-measurement pending.
  - **Tests**: `tests/fixtures/native-batch-golden.json` (every golden case encoded by Rust; `cargo test` pins encoding + round trip) is what Group 264's stub now streams, decoded by the page's own `decodeNativeBatch` — the layout is checked across the language boundary. Full suite: **5510 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: desktop — native, parallel log parsing in Rust (this session, 2026-09-24, person-requested: "Einlesen und Parsing nach Rust verlagern … gleiche Logik … möglichst viel Performance")**. Under the Tauri wrapper a file opened from disk (dialog, drop, folder watch, file association) is read and parsed by the new `philogg-logparse` crate (`desktop/src-tauri/logparse/`) on all cores via rayon, and streamed back to the page in 10,000-entry batches over a Tauri IPC channel (`parse_log_file` command, `window.philogg.parseLogFile` in `inject.js`). The file no longer passes through the webview as text (no `fetch`, `FileReader` or worker copy).
  - **Same logic, one definition**: the page still compiles each format; `nativeFormatSpec` (sharing the new `compileFormatRegex` with `compileOneFormat`) hands Rust the regex *source* this page would run. Rust translates JS-regex semantics exactly (`jsregex.rs`: ASCII `\d`/`\w`/`\b`, JS `\s`, JS `.`) and refuses what it can't mirror (lookaround, backreferences…), which makes the page fall back to its own parser for that file. `parseHeaderLine`, `applyFormatMatch`, `formatLocation`, `appendContinuationLine`, the timestamp parsers and `readAsText`'s BOM-sniffing decode are ported one function each.
  - **Timestamps** come back naive (`Date.UTC` of the fields); `makeNaiveTsLocalizer` converts them with the page's own engine, so DST and tz data are identical to the JS path.
  - **Falls back** (unchanged JS path) for meta-formats, unsupported regex constructs, a missing bridge and read errors; tailing appends, session restore, windowed loads and ZIP entries stay JS.
  - **Measured** (crate alone, 1M entries/154 MB, 4 cores): read ~0.25s + parse ~0.6s + JSON ~0.3s (a regex-mode format: parse ~1.3s), vs. ~3.7s for the page's own parse loop on one thread. What the page then spends turning the JSON batches into entries on its main thread is not in these numbers. Not yet run inside a real webview (see `desktop/README.md` → "Status").
  - **Tests**: new **Group 264** (spec, golden equivalence, adoption shape, rollback, the three load routes + fallbacks, DST localizer in five time zones) and a shared golden fixture `tests/fixtures/native-parse-golden.json`, which the crate's `cargo test` also checks (at slice sizes 1 B–1 MB), plus a brute-force `formatLocation` check. Full suite: **5509 passed, 0 failed**.

The rank→class table moved out of `renderTimelineMinimap` into a shared `minimapLevelClass(rank)`. Group 263 extended. Full suite: **5280 passed, 0 failed**.

Group 263 updated. Full suite: **5277 passed, 0 failed**.

The Insert chips, being actions, got the app's framed button look. `.filter-toggle*`/`.column-chip` CSS removed; `docs/ui-standard.md` documents the label-only toggle exception.

- **feat: filter popup redesign — scope hint, Text/Regex switch, live result summary (this session, 2026-09-23, person-requested, mockup screenshot; the app's own styling kept)**. The "Filter on:" header with its `#filterTargetChain` pills is gone (the last `.crumb`/`.crumb-sep` CSS went with it); instead an inline `in <name>` tag inside the input box names the direct parent, cut to 15 characters. "Interpret input as regex" became a **Text / Regex** segmented switch.
