- **feat: one format dialog — define a log format by example, with an automatic suggestion, marking, direct regex editing, auto-filled levels, a table preview, and an optional filename rule (this session, 2026-09-23, person-requested, built in three rounds: a "Set up from examples" wizard first, then preview double-click + undo/redo, then merging the old format editor into it)**. Settings → Log Formats → "Add format…"/"Edit" now open one large dialog on top of Settings. It replaces the old inline editor panel, whose Pattern field, single example field, and separate column editor are gone. `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Format dialog" has the full mechanics.
  - **Examples**: paste (Ctrl+V or a Paste button), open a file, or drop a file anywhere while the dialog is open. A drop becomes example lines instead of loading, in the browser and under the desktop wrapper (`loadDesktopLocalFiles`).
  - **Automatic suggestion (new formats)**: the old `suggestPatternFromSample`, run on the first line with a timestamp, then generalized through the same derivation as hand marks. It shows up immediately as suggested marks plus the preview.
  - **Edit mode**: pasted examples are always shown against the format's **own** regex, never a silent new suggestion; the status line warns if it matches none of them.
  - **Re-suggest**: an explicit button that starts over from a fresh suggestion, also when editing, after an inline warning that the current marks, regex and timestamp format get discarded. A single Undo reverts it. A regex typed into the Regex field stands in above all of these.
  - **Marking**: pick a column (Time/Level fixed; Thread/Location/Method/custom removable, reorderable, renamable by double-click; Message last), then select its value in a line. The first mark on a line adopts that line's suggested marks, so correcting one column keeps the rest.
  - **Derivation**: after every change a whole-line regex is re-derived from all marked lines (`deriveFormatRegexFromMarks`): literal separators with space runs loosened to `\s+`, per-column character classes that generalize from few examples, the unmarked tail becoming Message, and an auto-detected `tsFormat` (`detectTsFormatFromValues`). Unmarked lines never feed it, which answers the stack-trace question; whether a line is a new entry is decided by the regex alone.
  - **Direct regex editing**: the Regex field is editable. Typing replaces the marks (re-shown as suggested marks from the typed regex), adds any new named group as a column, and is one undo step.
  - **Levels**: auto-filled from the captured level values for a new format. All-numeric values give Integer mode with one level per code, named after the code (`3` → `3`). Custom levels are renamable, and renaming onto an unused fixed name merges (`3` → ERROR keeps code 3). A hand edit switches auto-fill to append-only.
  - **Preview**: a table shaped like the log view, whose level badges follow the unsaved level list. Clicking a badge finds its level; double-clicking a row scrolls to and flashes its source example line.
  - **Undo/Redo**: covers marks, columns, lines, and regex typing (buttons, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y / Ctrl+Shift+Z; whole-state snapshots, max 100).
  - **Filename rule**: the optional "Use for files matching" field adds a rule on save, appended last and never duplicated. It's pre-filled from a dropped file's name (`app-2026-09-23.log` → `app-*.log`), and the dialog lists the rules already using the format.
  - **Save**: always **Regex mode**. An existing Pattern-mode format is converted to its compiled regex, with the date's inner groups made non-capturing via `compileFormatPattern`'s new `plainDateGroups` option; the builtin default's Reset still restores its pattern. Only the columns the regex captures are saved. The examples + marks are stored as `fmt.sampleSetup`, so Edit reopens where it was left.
  - **Default-format examples** (person-tested with a real default-format file):
    - **Location**: the suggestion now claims a path-like field as Location. Before, it stayed literal, pinned the regex to one file, and matched nothing.
    - **Suggestion source**: it takes its column spans directly as virtual marks. Before, it re-ran the raw regex, whose `\S+` thread failed on `"(1) "` and silently fell back to the unusable raw suggestion.
    - **Closing quote**: it strips a message's closing quote.
    - **Multi-line messages**: text after a marked Message at the line end is now optional in the derivation (`"(?<message>.*?)(?:")?$`). A message whose closing quote sits on a continuation line (`… 'Evaluate` ⏎ `Evaluate with DirectInspect"`) no longer glues its entry onto the previous one.
    - **Continuation lines in the examples**: they now show a Message mark across the line (dashed, "continued from line N"), minus a quoted message's closing quote. Before, they stayed unmarked (person-reported).
    - **Closing quote on the continuation line**: it's dropped from the message instead of kept (person-reported follow-up). A header line whose closing quote is missing tags the entry as open (`messageWrapQuote`/`markOpenMessageQuote`). The shared `appendContinuationLine`, now used by the main-thread parser, the worker, tailing, and the preview, strips the quote from the line that closes it. `raw` stays verbatim.
  - **GROUP 70j fixed**: it had been crashing its whole shard since the Custom Columns commit, because a space-separated `%d %p %m%n` never matched `makeLog`'s tab-separated lines. That shard's `##SHARD` line was lost, so the previous session's reported total was short.
  - **Tests**: new **Group 261** (261a–h). Groups 70e/70j/116d/117d/234a/260b/260c were rewritten for the dialog.
  - **Full suite**: **5262 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: Custom Columns — per-format custom columns populated by Pattern/Regex captures, a configurable order, and Time/Level as the only two mandatory columns (this session, 2026-09-21, person-requested)**. Every format used to produce the same fixed six-field entry schema (`ts, level, thread, location, method, message`); this generalizes it while keeping backward compatibility exact for any format that never touches the new fields. **Time and Level are now the only two mandatory columns** (confirmed with the person during planning) — Thread/Location/Method/Message are each individually optional per format, and a format may additionally define **custom columns**: a new `%X{name}` pattern token (log4j MDC-style — captures free text into a user-chosen field name, rejecting a name that collides with one of the six reserved fields) gives Pattern mode parity with Regex mode's pre-existing `(?<name>...)` named groups (no parsing-side change needed there at all). Any captured group beyond the six reserved ones lands on a new `entry.fields` bag (`applyFormatMatch`) — a format defining none (every builtin/legacy format) shares one frozen empty object, so this costs nothing extra in the common case. **Time/Level being mandatory is a save-time-only rule** in the Format Manager (`saveFormatEdit`) — `compileFormatPattern`/`validateFormatRegex` themselves have no hard field requirement any more (every field already had a graceful runtime default: `ts`→`NaN`, `level`→`"INFO"`, `message`→the whole raw line when ungrouped), so a format saved before this rule existed, or compiled at runtime for a file already on disk, keeps parsing exactly as it always has — only *saving* a pattern/regex missing `%d`/`ts` or `%p`/`level` is newly blocked. A format's `columnDefs: [{key, kind, label}]` is its own ordered, reorderable **middle**-column list (Thread/Location/Method, each individually removable/re-addable, plus any custom columns) — edited via a new "Columns" section in the Format Manager (`renderFormatEditColumns`, same ▲/▼-reorder idiom the level list already used, plus one-click "+ Thread"-style chips to re-add a removed default and a text-field pair to add a custom column, validated live against the pattern/regex's own captured group names). Time/Level stay pinned first, never hideable, not part of this list; Message stays pinned last (the row grid's flexible `1fr` remainder when shown) with its own new `messageVisible: boolean` flag instead of an order position. `formatColumnDefs`/`formatMessageVisible` read one format's own list (falling back to the legacy fixed three / always-visible for any pre-existing record); `activeColumnDefs`/`activeMessageVisible` union every currently-loaded root file's own list (same idiom as the pre-existing `activeLevelOrder()`) and drive the row/header renderers (every column span now carries `data-col`, including Time/Level/Message which didn't before), the columns-visibility panel (now JS-rendered from the dynamic set instead of four fixed checkboxes), the context menu's generalized "Filter for this ___" (`activeTextFilterColumns()`, replacing the old fixed 6-entry `TEXT_FILTER_COLUMNS` table and its parallel `CTX_FILTER_COLUMN_CLASSES` lookup — `resolveContextFilterColumn` is now a plain `closest("[data-col]")` walk), and the filter popup's column-restriction chips (JS-rendered from the same dynamic list). `entryColumnValue(e, key)` replaces the old fixed-switch `textColumnValue` — the six reserved fields keep their historical accessors, anything else reads `e.fields[key]`. The two filter-node persistence chokepoints that used to whitelist `node.columns` against the static `TEXT_FILTER_COLUMN_KEYS` (`materializeSerializedRoots`, `materializeCachedFilters`) now validate against a new `knownTextFilterColumnKeys()` — deliberately broader (every format `state.logFormats` knows about, not just currently-loaded ones), so a saved filter's restriction to a custom column survives export/import and session-cache restore even when that column's owning format isn't open right now; a genuinely unknown key is still stripped. **Level** gained an int-or-text value type with explicit per-level colors: `LogFormat.levels` moves from a plain `string[]` to `{value, name, color}[]` (a legacy plain-string record upcasts on read, no migration needed — this project stays pre-1.0), `levelValueType: "text"|"int"` decides whether `levelBucket` matches a captured token against each level's `name` (unchanged default) or its numeric `value` (e.g. RFC 5424 syslog severity 0–7, mapped explicitly per level rather than through a fixed severity table); `color` is an explicit CSS color picked via the existing reusable color-picker popup (`openColorPicker`, already designed for a non-highlight-color caller), falling back to the automatic fixed-name/rotating-slot color when unset. Retires `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #80 (numeric-level-derivation), covered by the more general int-mode mapping. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 260** (parsing both custom-column mechanisms, the data-model fallback, the save-time gate, the Columns editor end to end, rendering/context-menu/filter-popup integration for a loaded custom-column format, the union-of-loaded-formats behavior, the permissive persistence policy, and the level int-mode/explicit-color mechanics); Groups 58/70/116d/117/154/212b updated in place where their assertions depended on now-superseded specifics (the "loc"→"location" column-key rename bundled in while touching this code; the dynamic columns panel's checkboxes now queried by `[data-col]` instead of fixed ids; `%m`/`(?<message>...)` no longer being a compile-time requirement). `PROJECT.md` → "The log formats it parses", `docs/filters.md`, `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Log view column visibility / width" / "Per-format log levels" updated in place. Full suite: **4423 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: two ways partial content could leak into view before a load actually finished — a meta-format merge's per-stream focus flicker, and the live-growing scrollbar exception — both closed, restoring "nothing renders until fully loaded" as an exception-free rule** (this session, 2026-09-21, person-reported: watching a meta-format load, the Filtered/Full/minimap view briefly showed each per-grammar stream's own already-complete content before settling on the merge, and separately, an ordinary single-file load could already show partial rows for the active view before it finished). Two independent causes, both against the same design principle documented in "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`. **(1)** `loadMetaFormatText` loads each stream via `addFile(..., placeholder)`, which internally calls `activateQueuedFileNode` — unconditionally flipping `state.activeId` onto that stream for the duration of its own read+parse. That stream's own natural-completion render (a real, full `render()`) then painted the main view with ONE grammar's complete content, before the loop's own trailing `state.activeId = merged.id` restored focus to the merge only at the very end. Fixed with a new `metaFormatSplitInProgress` flag, set for the loop's duration and checked in both `createFileNode`'s and `activateQueuedFileNode`'s `state.activeId` assignment — mirroring the pre-existing `sessionRestoreInProgress` guard those same two functions already had for an analogous "don't let a transient internal node steal focus" case. **(2)** A previous session's `updateLiveGrowingTotal` (called from `scheduleLoadRender`) deliberately kept `#tableSpacer`'s height and the visible table rows tracking an actively-loading node's live entry count, for the active view specifically — a real fix at the time for a large merge's stale scrollbar, but itself a narrow exception to the "nothing else updates live during a load" rule, and the second way partial content could show early. Removed outright (not narrowed further): `scheduleLoadRender` is back to only `updateLoadRowProgress`/`updateMergeLoadFraction`, no view/scrollbar write of any kind. Accepted tradeoff: `#tableSpacer`'s height (and `End`/`Ctrl+End`'s reach) goes back to staying stale until the load's natural completion, same as every other part of the view during a load — the scroll-cap/rescale mechanism (`detectMaxTableScrollPx`/`computeTableSpacerContentHeight`) that a later session added is unaffected, since it applies at every real render regardless. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "File loading"/"Create-first loading" updated in place. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 259** (the meta-format merge stays the active node throughout a real, multi-chunk two-stream split/load — checked synchronously right after the call, mid-loop, and once finished); **Group 254 REWRITTEN** (the spacer now stays stale across a load tick in all three of its former cases — active/background/level-filtered); Group 50's own scrollbar assertion flipped to match. Full suite: **5034 passed, 0 failed**.

- **perf: rebuilding `#tableRows`' entire DOM on every single scroll animation frame was heavy enough to interfere with Firefox's own native keyboard-scroll (End/Home) animation on a huge compressed merge — throttled to at most one render per 100ms during a rapid scroll burst** (this session, 2026-09-21, same-day immediate follow-up: the requested targeted instrumentation from the entry directly below WAS captured, and it settled the open question — `currentViewEntries.length` stayed perfectly constant across the whole sequence, ruling out the "total fluctuates" theory, and `needsRowOffsets()` was confirmed off, ruling out the variable-row-height branch). The new telemetry showed something else: closing the DevTools console mid-capture changed `#tableBody`'s `clientHeight` (242 → 853), incidentally producing two independent real "End" key attempts at the same 853px viewport to compare — both stalled **150,000-190,000 physical px short** of the true native `scrollTop` max (`scrollHeight - clientHeight`), landing at slightly different positions each time (non-deterministic). That magnitude rules out every scroll-math fix from the last three rounds (all on the order of tens to a few hundred px) — this needed a genuinely different explanation. Root-caused to `renderVisibleRows()` fully rebuilding `#tableRows`' DOM (`innerHTML = ""` + up to ~51 rows' worth of bracket detection, match highlighting, note rows, ...) on **every single `requestAnimationFrame` tick** of a scroll — cheap for an ordinary file, but on a huge compressed merge this is enough main-thread work per frame to visibly compete with (and, per the telemetry, apparently cut short) Firefox's own native keyboard-scroll animation, which runs a timed interpolation rather than "scroll until the target is literally reached" — heavy JS work on the main thread during that window can make the browser's own animation finish well short of the real target, non-deterministically depending on exactly how much work landed in each frame. Fixed with a new `makeThrottledScrollRenderer` helper (`SCROLL_RENDER_THROTTLE_MS = 100`), applied to both `#tableBody`'s own scroll listener and its twin `#highlightBody`/`renderHighlightVisibleRows` listener: during a rapid burst of scroll events, renders are capped to at most one per 100ms (via `setTimeout`, not `requestAnimationFrame` alone) instead of one per animation frame, freeing up most of each frame's time budget for the browser's own scroll physics — a single, isolated scroll event (mouse wheel, trackpad) still renders on its very next frame, unchanged from before. The render functions always read live DOM/state at call time, so the trailing catch-up render at the end of a throttled burst still reflects the true final scroll position with no extra bookkeeping needed. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **GROUP 258**: 258a proves no regression for ordinary single-event scrolling; 258b is the real regression check — ten rapid-fire scroll events collapse onto exactly one leading-edge render (not ten), with exactly one trailing catch-up render once the burst settles, reflecting the true last of the ten positions, not a stale intermediate one; 258c covers the `#highlightBody` twin identically. **Honesty note, continued**: real Firefox still isn't available in this sandbox, so whether this is *sufficient* to let the native scroll animation complete cleanly remains for the person's own retest — but this round at least replaced two more rounds of guessing with a fix grounded in a concrete, measured magnitude (150K+ px) from real captured telemetry, not an assumption. Full suite: **5027 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: the physical scroll range used for `#tableBody`'s scale was 22px short of the real native range — `computeTableSpacerContentHeight` never accounted for `#tableRows`' own padding, which every call site adds to the declared spacer height** (this session, 2026-09-21, immediate person-reported follow-up: the previous two rounds' fixes didn't fully resolve it, so instead of guessing again the person instrumented `#tableBody`'s scroll listener with a small pasted console script and captured a real End/Home/End sequence in Firefox on the 822k-entry merge). The captured telemetry confirmed the last round's fix genuinely holds — `scrollHeight` stayed rock-stable (`9054710`) throughout the whole sequence, no more drift from `#tableRows` overhanging the capped spacer. Working the numbers by hand found a second, smaller, independent defect: the true native `scrollTop` max is `scrollHeight - clientHeight = 9054468`, but `computeTableSpacerContentHeight`'s `physicalRange` was computed as `cap - clientHeight = 9054446` — exactly 22px short, because every `tableSpacer.style.height` call site sets it to `computeTableSpacerContentHeight(...) + 22` (`#tableRows`' own top+bottom padding), never the bare `cap`, and the scale computation never accounted for that same pad. Fixed by extracting the literal `22` into a named `TABLE_SPACER_PAD` constant and using `(cap + TABLE_SPACER_PAD) - viewportH` as the physical range; no call site or the `renderVisibleRows()` tail-anchor bounds from the previous round needed touching (their own `cap - trueRenderedHeight` math already implicitly cancels the same pad against a rendered box that also carries it once). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUP 256 extended: 256a/256f updated to the pad-aware formula, new 256i reproduces the bug class directly (scrolls to a stubbed TRUE native max and asserts a healthy last-page render — confirmed failing without the fix, rendering only 17 of an expected 25 rows, passing with it). **Honesty note**: the captured telemetry's most striking symptom — the rendered row count collapsing from ~30 rows to a single row exactly at/near the native max, followed by a large backward `scrollTop` jump — is NOT fully explained by this fix's own arithmetic (worked by hand against the telemetry's implied real total of ≈822,731 entries, the *old, buggy* math already predicted ~17-21 rows there, not 1). This fix is shipped because it's independently correct and verified, not because it's confirmed to fully resolve the reported symptom — flagged for a follow-up round with more targeted instrumentation (logging `currentViewEntries.length` and the real rendered row count directly from inside `renderVisibleRows`, and checking whether `needsRowOffsets()`/Multiline/Wrap/Notes is active) rather than claimed fixed. Full suite: **5019 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: `#tableRows`' own uncompressed rendered box could still overhang `#tableSpacer`'s capped height at the tail — End landed short with blank space below the last row, Home needed several presses to reach the top** (this session, 2026-09-20, immediate person-reported follow-up after retesting the scale-formula fix directly below in Firefox on a real 2-file drag-drop merge: selecting the first entry then pressing `End` landed close to but not exactly at the true end — about 5 entries still below the visible area, blank space under the last row; pressing `Home` afterward only scrolled up ~5% of the log, needing several more presses, each landing further (~50%, then finally the top); pressing `End` again reversed through similar intermediate steps; Chrome unaffected). Two Explore passes confirmed a second, independent defect from the one fixed below: `#tableRows`' own rendered row heights are always TRUE/uncompressed (only its `top` offset goes through `logicalToPhysicalScrollPx`), and its `BUFFER_ROWS` lookback (`start` offset back by 10 rows, so a small scroll doesn't need a full re-render) leaves the rendered block taller than the viewport right at the tail — while `end` was always clamped to `total`, the block's `top` was still positioned via a plain top-anchored `logicalToPhysicalScrollPx(start*ROW_HEIGHT)`, which lands close enough to the physical cap at the tail that `top + true rendered height` provably exceeds `#tableSpacer`'s own capped declared height there. `#tableSpacer`/`#tableRows` are deliberately `overflow:visible` (so a long log message's horizontal overflow bleeds up to `#tableBody`'s own horizontal scrollbar — ruling out `overflow:hidden` as a fix, since CSS's own overflow-x/overflow-y pairing rule would force that axis to `auto` too and break the bleed-through), so nothing clips the overhang, letting it inflate the browser's own real `scrollHeight` beyond what `computeTableSpacerContentHeight` assumed — a smaller, render-geometry-driven resurgence of the same "no `overflow:hidden` sits between them" mechanism `PROJECT.md`'s gotcha entry already blames for the original version of this bug. This explains both symptoms: a physical `scrollTop` genuinely past the assumed max converts to a logical `start` past `total`, which (previously) had no upper clamp to catch it, producing an empty/blank render; and each native Home/End keypress (Firefox's own always-on keyboard smooth-scroll) recomputes against a `scrollHeight` that itself shifts as `#tableRows` repositions — a real feedback loop. Fixed with two changes to `renderVisibleRows()` (both branches): **(1)** bottom-anchor `#tableRows`' `top` instead of top-anchoring it whenever the render reaches the very last entry (`end === total`) and compression is active — `Math.min(naiveTop, cap - trueRenderedLogicalHeight)`, verified algebraically monotonic across the whole tail region (no visible snap/jump); **(2)** a defensive upper clamp on `start`/`centerIdx` (previously only `end` was ever clamped against `total`), so any residual out-of-range `scrollTop` still degrades to a sane last-page render instead of an empty one. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUP 256 extended again: 256g asserts the rendered block's true height never overhangs the capped spacer at the native `scrollTop` max and the true last entry is still rendered there; 256h asserts an out-of-assumed-range `scrollTop` still renders a non-empty last page reaching the true last entry. Real Firefox isn't available in this sandbox — same limitation as the prior two rounds of this bug — so final on-screen confirmation is left to the person's own retest. Full suite: **5017 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: holding/repeatedly pressing `End` on a height-capped merge in Firefox still couldn't reach the true last row, and the last row wandered a few pixels rather than settling** (this session, 2026-09-20, immediate person-reported follow-up after retesting the scrollbar fix directly below: the scrollbar now looked proportional, but `End` needed several presses/holds to actually get there, and continuing to press it made the display jump a few pixels forward and sometimes back with no clear final position). An Explore pass over the shipped fix ruled out every app-side feedback-loop theory first: no custom keyboard handling exists for `End`/`Home` (it's 100% native browser scroll on `#tableBody`), the `scroll` listener never writes back to `scrollTop` itself, and `tableScrollHeightScale`/`#tableSpacer`'s height are provably constant while scrolling a static, already-loaded merge (`computeTableSpacerContentHeight`'s 4 call sites are all gated on load-progress/user actions, none on scroll/resize/rAF). Working through the actual math instead exposed a real defect: `tableScrollHeightScale` was derived as `cap / contentPx` — the ratio of the two **content** heights — and applied as a linear scale straight to `#tableBody`'s native `scrollTop`. But the native scrollable *range* on each side is `scrollHeight - clientHeight`, not raw content height, so a content-ratio scale leaves the logical position computed at the true native `scrollTop` maximum short of the true logical bottom by `clientHeight * (contentPx/cap - 1)` px — several row-heights under real compression, meaning `End` could sit at its native physical ceiling while the render still never revealed the actual last rows (a residual pixel-level wobble from Firefox's own native keyboard-scroll animation, amplified by the same undersized scale, remains a separate, likely-irreducible cosmetic effect — out of scope for this round, per person's explicit choice). Fixed by deriving `tableScrollHeightScale` from the two scrollable **ranges** instead of raw content heights (`(cap - tableBody.clientHeight) / (contentPx - tableBody.clientHeight)`) in `computeTableSpacerContentHeight` — its signature, all 4 call sites, and every `logicalToPhysicalScrollPx`/`physicalToLogicalScrollPx` call site stay unchanged; only how the scale itself is computed differs. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUP 256 extended (256a's expected-scale math updated to the same range-based formula; new 256f asserts the native `scrollTop` max now converts exactly onto the true logical bottom, plus a sanity check that the old content-ratio scale really would have undershot it by more than a row). Full suite: **5013 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: the log view's scrollbar was still broken for a large merge — real root cause was a hard browser height limit, not a stale-total bug; single continuous byte-weighted progress bar replaces the segmented design** (this session, 2026-09-19, immediate person-reported follow-up: the previous entry's own `updateLiveGrowingTotal` fix didn't hold up — their repro was a 2-file drag-drop merge, 822,697 entries, reproduced *after* full load completed, surviving a file switch and back, which a load-tick-only fix can never touch). Root-caused through several ruled-out hypotheses (a generic browser-height-limit guess, PhiLogg's own "UI scale" zoom setting, device pixel ratio, inflated row height from Multiline/Wrap/Notes — each directly tested via a real Playwright/Chromium harness built for this investigation and refuted) to a genuine, pre-existing browser limitation — confirmed present on `main` too, unrelated to any session's own changes: every major engine has a hard practical ceiling on a single element's CSS height, and `#tableSpacer`'s declared height had no cap at all. Chromium clamps to exactly **33,554,428px** (empirically confirmed this session); Firefox — where the person was actually testing — instead discards an oversized declaration entirely and falls back to `height:auto`, which resolves to **0px** for `#tableSpacer` since its only content (`#tableRows`) is absolutely positioned and doesn't count toward auto-sizing, exactly matching the person's own `getComputedStyle` → `"0px"` reading. Once collapsed, `#tableSpacer` stops contributing to `#tableBody`'s scrollable region at all (no `overflow:hidden` between them), leaving `#tableRows`' own drifting `top` offset as the only thing driving `scrollHeight` — explaining every symptom (~2/3 of the data unreachable, the scrollbar jumping while scrolling, the thumb shrinking while holding `End`). Fixed with `detectMaxTableScrollPx` (`philogg.html`, near `ROW_HEIGHT`): a lazy, cached, per-session **binary-search feature-detection of the actual running engine's own ceiling**, deliberately not a fixed worst-case constant — person-requested, since the app's primary target (the Tauri desktop build on Windows, embedding WebView2/Chromium) has roughly 2x Firefox's own headroom, and hardcoding to the tighter limit would needlessly halve scroll resolution there. `computeTableSpacerContentHeight` caps `#tableSpacer`'s content height under that detected ceiling and tracks a `tableScrollHeightScale` factor; `logicalToPhysicalScrollPx`/`physicalToLogicalScrollPx` convert at the boundary everywhere `#tableBody`'s scroll position is read or written for position math (`renderVisibleRows`, `scrollToIndex` + new `setTableScrollLogical`, `captureViewAnchor`, `minimapRenderedSpan`) — real on-screen row geometry (row height, viewport height, rendered row spacing) is never touched, only the compressed physical spacer position. `setTableScroll`'s existing "scroll to true bottom" callers (tail-follow) are untouched — the physical bottom already equals the logical bottom under a linear scale.

  Separately, the person clarified their earlier progress-bar request (previous entry's `(n+1)`-segment bar wasn't quite what they meant): **one continuous bar**, weighted by each source's real byte size — not an equal split — with a small reservation for the merge-copy step, and only because it's needed (a quick/disjoint merge still jumps straight to 100%). The whole segmented design (`node.loadSegmentSourceIds`, `mergeSegmentFractions`, `updateMergeSegmentedProgress`, `.tree-load-fill-segment` CSS/markup) is retired — replaced by `node.loadSources` (`{id, weight}` pairs; `weight` is a real `File.size` for `loadFileDescriptors`, a character-count proxy for `loadMetaFormatText`'s in-memory streams, and an honest equal-weight fallback for folder-watch merges, which have no size available and already load strictly sequentially) plus `updateMergeLoadFraction`, which computes one weighted `node.loadFraction` and writes it through the exact same plain `.tree-load-fill` markup/`updateLoadRowProgress` an ordinary file's own bar already uses — a net simplification, no new CSS or rendering branch. A new `MERGE_STEP_BAR_FRACTION = 0.1` constant reserves a small, deliberately fixed tail for the merge-copy/sort step (person-confirmed: not worth modeling exact relative effort); `fillMergedEntries`'s chunked-copy path rebases its own progress into that tail, and the disjoint/quick-merge fast path still sets `loadFraction = 1` — now the whole bar, not just a segment. Regression coverage: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUPs 256 (the scroll-cap/rescale mechanism — jsdom has no real layout engine, so a `T.forceTableScrollCap` test hook pins a small deterministic ceiling) and 257 (the byte-weighted bar, the reserved tail, the disjoint fast path), plus GROUPs 244/253 rewritten in place against the retired segmented design. Full suite: **5011 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: four refinements and three bugs in the "Sources"/create-merge-first rework, found by actually using it** (this session, 2026-09-19, person-reported after the previous session's rework shipped). **Refinements**: (1) a nested Sources row (rendered via `opts.mergeSourceColor`) no longer carries its own ✕/middle-click delete — `renderNode`'s delete-button and `auxclick` handlers now also gate on `!(opts && opts.mergeSourceColor)` — only deletable together with its owning merge; a bulk-merge-visible source's own top-level row (no `mergeSourceColor` at that call site) is unaffected and keeps both. (2) `deleteNode` now cascades over a merge's own `node.sources`: a `mergeSourceHidden` source (create-first — no life outside the merge) is deleted right along with it; a bulk-merge-visible (`mergeOwnerId`-only) source instead survives at top level, un-orphaned (`mergeOwnerId`/`mergeSourceHidden` cleared) — previously neither case was handled, leaving hidden sources permanently orphaned (invisible, unreachable, still tagged) forever. (3) Undoing a deleted merge now restores its cascade-deleted hidden sources too: `snapshotSubtree` captures each hidden source's own full snapshot under a new `hiddenSourceSnapshots` field (root-level siblings aren't reached by the normal `children` recursion), `restoreSubtree` recreates them under a transient `hiddenSourceIds`, and `undo()`'s `"deleteFile"` branch splices each restored id back into `state.rootIds` (every file node must live there) and re-persists it. This also surfaced a latent gap: `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` never carried a file node's own `mergeOwnerId`/`mergeSourceHidden` tags at all (never needed before, since a hidden source was never independently snapshotted) — now they do, or a restored source came back untagged. (4) `fillMergedEntries` now sets the Sources node's own `collapsed = true` once a merge completes (both the create-first and old bulk "Merge N files" paths funnel through it) — `createMergeShell` still leaves it expanded so per-source progress is visible while loading; only the completion moment changed. **Bugs**: (1) the `(n+1)`-segment combined progress bar on a create-first merge's own row was gated `&& !showSourcesSetting` — with Sources on (the default), a two-file ~100MB drag-drop merge showed **no progress at all** on the merge row while both sources loaded, only changing once each source finished; the gate is removed, the segmented bar now always shows while a create-first merge is loading, alongside each nested source's own ordinary bar when Sources is also shown. (2) the log view's scrollbar total (`#tableSpacer`'s height, which drives the native `scrollHeight`/max-`scrollTop` — there's no custom scrollbar thumb) only ever reflected the entry count as of the last full `renderTable()`, which a load tick never triggers (deliberately cheap, per the "a load tick never rebuilds `#tree`" rule) — for a large, actively-growing merge (reported: 782,000 entries across two 100MB files) the scrollbar showed a far smaller, constantly "catching up" total, and `End`/`Ctrl+End` (the browser's own native scroll-to-`scrollHeight`) could only ever reach that same stale ceiling. New `updateLiveGrowingTotal(rootId)`, called from `scheduleLoadRender`, fixes the common case cheaply: when the active node's `currentViewEntries` IS `node.entries` by reference (proven by reference-equality alone — true whenever no level filter/pinned-bookmarks union/temp anchor is narrowing the view), its `.length` is already live the instant `node.entries.push()` runs, so only the frozen spacer height needs rewriting, plus a cheap `renderVisibleRows()` — deliberately not a `renderTable()` call, which would make a many-chunk load O(n²). A narrowed view is left to catch up at the next real render, unchanged. (3) deleting a file mid-load left it leaking entries/`entryIndex` ids into the now-orphaned node for the rest of its read/parse (no existence check on any entry-write site, unlike `updateLoadRowProgress`'s own `if (!node) return;`), and the load's own completion `flushLoadRender`/render fired for the dead node regardless. `loadOneFileIntoTree` and `addFile` (a second call site with the identical unconditional-`flushLoadRender` bug, found while fixing the first) now both check `state.nodes[node.id] === node` before their trailing render; `parseLogTextAsync`'s plain-loop fallback (jsdom/this sandbox has no `Worker`, so this is also what the regression suite exercises) and `parseFileWindow` both bail out of their per-chunk yield loop once the node's gone, mirroring the guard `fillMergedEntries` already had for the identical race; `parseFileWindow`'s own caller (`loadFolderFileWindowed`) now distinguishes "unsafe window, fall back to a full reload" from "deleted mid-load, leave it deleted" instead of always reviving the node via a full-file fallback on any `false` return. Regression coverage: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUPs 249-255 (one per item above), plus GROUP 50 and GROUP 237 updated in place — both asserted the exact stale/no-collapse behavior these fixes intentionally reverse. Full suite: **4994 passed, 0 failed**.

- **feat: "Sources" is now a real tree-level sibling of Bookmarks/Notes/Selection N (not an extra nesting level), a new "Show Sources" setting, and every merge-into-path now creates the merge first and loads sources into it without ever deleting them (this session, 2026-09-18, person-requested follow-up correcting the previous session's design)**. Three corrections to the "Sources" feature shipped last session (below): **(1) Tree structure.** "Sources" was a purely presentational group (`renderSourcesGroup`/`renderSourceRow`), not a real `state.nodes` entry, reading as an extra indentation level under a merged file instead of sitting where Bookmarks/Notes/Selection N already live. It's now a real, locked `filterType:"sources"` node created once per merge (`createMergeShell`), positioned via a new shared ordering helper — `specialChildRank`/`insertSpecialChild` (`philogg.html`, near `syncBookmarksFilterNode`) — that replaces what used to be four independent, ad hoc `unshift`/splice calls (`syncBookmarksFilterNode`, `syncNotesFilterNode`, `createSelectionFilterNode`, and now `createMergeShell`'s own Sources node) with one rank-based rule (Sources=0, Bookmarks=1, Notes=2, Selection N=3+its own creation ordinal, captured as `node.selectionOrdinal` so a later rename can't disturb sort order). A file's auto-managed rows now always read **Sources, Bookmarks, Notes, Selection 1, Selection 2, ...** regardless of creation order — previously, a second Selection node even unshifted itself *above* an existing Bookmarks/Notes node. The Sources node's own `children` stays empty (file nodes must stay root-level, per this app's own data-model invariant — they can never be reparented under a filter node); nesting is driven by the owning file's `sources` array plus each source's real file node, rendered a *second* time via the ordinary `renderNode(src.id, depth+1, {mergeSourceColor:src})` — reusing the exact same "real root node, rendered nested, skipped from the plain top-level walk" pattern the ZIP/folder-watch containers already use (new `mergeOwnerId`/`mergeSourceHidden` tags on a source file node, mirroring the existing `zipId`/`folderId` tags; `renderTree`/`renderFolderSection`/`flattenTreeIds` all gained the matching skip). The per-source color swatch moved onto that nested file row (a new `opts.mergeSourceColor` branch in `renderNode`'s existing swatch block) instead of a bespoke row type; `getEntries` on a Sources node is a pure passthrough of its parent's own result; it's excluded from the filter-tree persistence carriers exactly like Bookmarks/Notes. **(2) New Settings → Behavior → Filter tree toggle, "Show Sources"** (default **on**, `philogg-show-sources` in `localStorage`, `showSourcesSetting`) — a pure display toggle (`renderNode` simply skips rendering the node when it's off; the underlying `sources`/`mergeOwnerId` data is never touched), same `pill-toggle` shape every other Behavior-tab boolean uses. **(3) Create-merge-first loading.** Previously every "load files straight into a merge" path (meta-format split, drag-drop-then-"Merge" confirm, folder-watch's "Merge (full)"/"Merge (window)") loaded every source to completion *first* — visibly, as separate top-level rows, and for the meta-format case those virtual per-grammar files were then *deleted* once merged. `mergeFiles` (`philogg.html:9478`) is now split into `createMergeShell(name, formatId)` (builds the empty merge node + its locked Sources child, inserted into the tree immediately, before any source is even read) and `fillMergedEntries(node, files)` (the actual entries copy/sort, unchanged in substance, just now filling a node that may already have existed and been visible for a while); `mergeFiles` itself becomes a thin "sources already loaded" wrapper calling both back to back, used unchanged by the pre-existing manual "Merge N files" bulk action. `loadMetaFormatText`, `loadFileDescriptors`' merge-on-load path, and `folderMinimapMergeFull`/`folderMinimapMergeWindow` now all call `createMergeShell` first, then load each source into a `mergeOwnerId`/`mergeSourceHidden`-tagged node — pre-tagged before its own read starts where a placeholder exists to pre-tag through (`loadFileDescriptors`/`loadMetaFormatText` reuse `addFile`/`loadOneFileIntoTree`'s existing `existingNode` parameter and `createQueuedFileNode`), or tagged right after that source's own load finishes where it doesn't (folder-watch's `loadFolderFile`/`loadFolderFileWindowed` own their own node creation — an accepted, brief visibility gap rather than a larger refactor) — before finally calling `fillMergedEntries` once every source is done. **Meta-format vnodes are never deleted any more** — reversing last session's own design, they stay real, independently clickable, filterable file nodes for as long as the merge exists, reachable under "Sources". If fewer than 2 sources end up loading successfully, the shell is discarded and any lone survivor is un-tagged back to a normal, visible file. While "Show Sources" is off, a loading create-first merge's own row shows a combined **`(n+1)`-segment progress bar** instead (`node.loadSegmentSourceIds`, new `.tree-load-segments`/`.tree-load-fill-segment` CSS, `updateMergeSegmentedProgress` hooked into the existing `scheduleLoadRender` per-tick path) — one segment per source plus one for the final copy/sort step; with Sources shown, the merge row carries no bar of its own during the loading phase (each nested source's own row already shows its own ordinary single-fill bar, completely unmodified) — the existing `typeof node.loadFraction === "number"` gate already means "no bar" for free in that case, since `fillMergedEntries` is the only thing that ever sets it. `deleteNode` gained a small cleanup (drops a deleted source from its owning merge's `sources` array, wherever it's deleted from — the original top-level row or its own nested row — instead of leaving a stale reference). Regression coverage: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUPs 240-248 (the ordering helper, Sources' real-node mechanics, the Show Sources setting, create-first loading via each of the three paths, the segmented progress bar, and the deleteNode cleanup), plus Groups 232b/236c/237/238 (last session's Sources coverage) rewritten in place against the new design rather than left describing the old one.

- **feat: multi-pattern parsing (FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #81) via a new "meta" log format, plus a "Sources" grouping/coloring feature for any merged file (this session, 2026-09-18, person-requested)**. Implements option (c) from #81's own discussion, chosen in full generic form: a third `LogFormat` mode, `"meta"`, alongside the existing `"pattern"`/`"regex"` — a meta-format carries no pattern/regex/tsFormat/levels of its own, only an ordered `targetFormatIds` list (≥2) naming other, ordinary formats to classify a file's lines against (`compileOneFormat`'s new `"meta"` branch is a defensive-only fallback; real files never reach it). `splitTextByMetaFormat(text, metaFmt)` classifies each non-blank line against every target's own already-compiled `isHeaderLine`, first match per line wins; a non-matching, non-blank line joins whichever target's stream most recently matched (the same continuation rule `parseLogTextAsync` already applies, just resolved per-stream before parsing starts); blank lines are dropped during the split entirely, never emitted into any stream — provably equivalent to today's single-format behavior, since `parseLogTextAsync`'s own loop already treats a blank line as a silent no-op. `loadMetaFormatText(name, text, metaFmt)` parses each classified stream as an ordinary transient file (reusing `addFile`/`parseLogTextAsync` unmodified), sorts each transient file's own entries by `ts` (a virtual syslog-block stream isn't guaranteed chronological on disk — the risk this pre-sort specifically guards against, since `mergeFiles`' disjoint-time-range fast path trusts each source's own order), then merges them via the existing, unmodified `mergeFiles`, renames the result to the physical filename, and removes the transient per-grammar vnodes (safe: `mergeFiles` already pushed the same entry objects by reference into the merged node first, so `deleteNode`'s `releaseEntriesFromIndex` purges nothing still live). Wired into both file-load entry points (`loadOneFileIntoTree`, `addFile`'s live-resolution branch) so drag-drop, the file picker, and folder-watch's on-demand open all pick it up automatically; folder-watch's minimap probe and windowed/partial loading explicitly decline a meta-format file in this first version (probe returns its existing "no range found" sentinel) rather than probing it wrong — no live-tailing either. Format Manager (Settings → Log format definitions) gained a third "Meta" mode button next to Pattern/Regex: an ordered target-format picker (add/reorder/remove, mirroring the existing level-order editor), a ≥2-targets save guard, and `removeLogFormat` now also blocks deleting a format still referenced as some meta-format's target. Three concrete formats from #81's own reference case (a log4net-style app grammar and an RFC 5424 syslog grammar, chat-uploaded this session — sample file + structural spec, previously flagged in #81 as needed before implementation) are seeded once via `loadFormatConfig()` (non-builtin, freely editable/deletable), with **no** filename rule attached — dormant until added by hand, per explicit request ("Only the Formats for now, i'll add a File association later"). Prerequisite fix found while wiring these up: the syslog sample's 6-digit fractional-second timestamps didn't parse at all — `DATE_TOKEN_FRAG.SSS` was hardcoded to `\d{1,3}`; widened to `\d{1,6}` and `parseTimestampGeneric` now normalizes the captured fraction by its own digit count (`Math.round(+frac * 10^(3-len))`) instead of treating it as a literal millisecond value (a bare `+"711324"` would have meant 711324ms — over 11 minutes' overflow into the wrong second — not `.711324s`); reduces to the exact previous behavior for a 3-digit fraction. Separately, and person-requested to generalize beyond just the new multi-pattern case: any merged file — the new meta-format auto-merge AND the pre-existing manual "Merge N files" bulk action alike — now gets an additive `node.sources` breakdown (`{id, name, color, count}` per source) and a matching `entry.sourceId` stamp on every copied entry (`mergeFiles`, both the disjoint-fast-path and chunked-copy paths), rendered as a new expandable **"Sources"** group directly under the merged file's own tree row (`renderSourcesGroup`/`renderSourceRow`, mirroring the ZIP/folder-watch "presentational container row" pattern — not real `state.nodes` entries), each source with its own color swatch reusing the existing highlight color-picker popup (generalized with an optional `onPick` callback, `openColorPicker`/`commitColorPick`, so a pick can land on `node.sources[i].color` instead of a filter node's `highlightColor`). `computeHighlightMap` gained a matching branch so a colored source's entries pick up the same gutter-marker lane filter highlights already use, composable with them. Session-only by explicit choice for this first version (no IndexedDB persistence — a session-cache restore of a merged file already reparses from scratch and loses this kind of state, same as the pre-existing "Merged-file gotcha") but threaded through `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`'s file branch so an in-session delete+undo of a merged file doesn't silently drop its Sources group or any assigned colors. Known, accepted limitation: entries are shared by reference across merges, so merging an already-merged file's entries again overwrites `sourceId` on the same objects, making the earlier merge's own Sources coloring stale — not fixed here. New regression coverage: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUPs 232-239 (split/classification against the real reference sample, the disjoint-fast-path chronology risk specifically, the new Format Manager UI, degenerate split outcomes, `sources`/`sourceId` stamping for both merge origins, the Sources group's rendering/color/undo, and the SSS date-token fix) — 3 pre-existing Group 70 assertions updated for the new seeded-format count (1 default + 3 seeded → 4 initially, 5 after adding one more).

- **feat: "Open here" for clickable local file paths (this session, 2026-09-17, person-requested: "Es gibt schon die Option Datei Links in Messages zu öffnen oder den beinhalten ordner zu öffnen. Für Dateiformat die unterstützt werden, soll oben die Option 'Open here' hinzugefügt werden. Damit soll das File in der Anwendung selbst geöffnet werden.")**. The clickable-local-path popup (`#fpPathMenu`, `docs/desktop.md` → "Clickable local file paths in log lines") already offered "Open file" (OS default app) and "Open containing folder"; it now offers a third, topmost action — **"Open here"** — that loads the detected path straight into PhiLogg's own tree instead of handing it to the OS. Shown only when the path's extension is one PhiLogg itself can load (`isCompatibleFolderFile` — the same "supported log format" check folder watch and ZIP entries already use, currently `.log` only). Desktop-only, like the rest of the popup (it never renders without `window.philogg`). New Rust command `open_local_path` (`desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`) re-validates the path still exists (it may have moved/been deleted since `path_exists` last checked) and registers it exactly like `pick_files`/`list_folder` already do, returning the same `LocalFile` shape (`url`/`path`/`name`/`mtime`); exposed to the page as `philogg.openLocalPath` (`inject.js`). The click handler (`openFpPathHere`) feeds the result straight into the page's own `loadDesktopLocalFiles` — the exact route drag-drop/pickFiles/folder-watch already use to get a wrapper-known path into the tree — so the new file gets the queued placeholder, tailing, and "Open File Location"/"Copy Path" treatment any other wrapper-opened file gets, with no new ingestion path. A rejected `openLocalPath()` surfaces a toast naming the path instead of throwing or leaving anything stuck. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 231**: the extension gate (offered and listed first for a `.log` path, absent for a `.txt` one which still gets the other two actions), the real click wiring end to end (registers via the mocked bridge method, lands as a fully parsed node under its own path/name, popup closes), and the failure path (a toast, no orphaned node). `docs/desktop.md` → "Clickable local file paths in log lines" updated in place. Full suite: **4817 passed, 0 failed**.

- **fix: an actual "Release Please" run cut `v1.0.0` instead of the intended `0.1.0` — a manifest value of exactly `"0.0.0"` is release-please's own "never released" sentinel and forces `1.0.0` regardless of `bump-minor-pre-major`** (this session, 2026-09-17, person-reported after watching it happen live: propose-release → merge → cut-release built and published a real `v1.0.0` GitHub Release with Windows/HTML assets — not the `0.1.0` the previous session's manifest reset was supposed to produce). Root cause confirmed against release-please's own source and a matching upstream report (googleapis/release-please#2087): the manifest's literal `"0.0.0"` isn't treated as "start bumping from zero," it's a hardcoded sentinel meaning "this component has never been released," and for that specific case release-please skips conventional-commit bump math entirely and defaults straight to `1.0.0` — independent of `bump-minor-pre-major`, which only ever affects *breaking-change* bumps pre-1.0, not this path. Person confirmed the intent explicitly: ordinary `feat:` commits bumping MINOR is fine, but nothing should cross `1.0.0` without an explicit, conscious decision. Fixed the supported way: `release-please-config.json`'s `.` package now carries `"initial-version": "0.1.0"`, which is exactly what release-please computes a first release to instead of the `1.0.0` default, with the manifest staying at the correct fresh-repo `"0.0.0"` sentinel. Recovery: `.release-please-manifest.json` and the four version files the erroneous release commit touched (`philogg.html`'s `PHILOGG_VERSION`, `desktop/package.json`, `desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` — plus `Cargo.lock`'s matching entry, which the release commit doesn't touch but had drifted to `1.0.0` too) rolled back from `1.0.0` to `0.1.0`/`"0.0.0"`. **The `v1.0.0` GitHub Release/tag itself still needs manual deletion** — no available tool exposes GitHub release/tag deletion, so this is a manual cleanup step before running `propose-release` again. `PROJECT.md` → "Release builds" gets a permanent "Gotcha" callout for this so it's never re-triggered by accident. No test changes: version-tracking metadata and CI-only files, outside both the regression suite and `desktop/`'s own build.
- **chore: `.release-please-manifest.json` reset from `0.1.0` to `0.0.0` so the next "Release Please" run cuts `0.1.0`** (this session, 2026-09-17, person-requested). No GitHub Release has ever actually been published for this repo (confirmed via the GitHub API — none exist), so the manifest's `0.1.0` was a leftover from an earlier rolled-back attempt rather than a real released version. With `bump-minor-pre-major: true` and the many `feat:` commits merged since that rollback, leaving the manifest at `0.1.0` would have made the next `propose-release` run compute `0.2.0`. Rather than forcing a specific version via a `Release-As:` commit footer (which `CLAUDE.md` reserves for an explicitly-requested major-version bump — not the case here), the manifest baseline itself was lowered so release-please's ordinary minor-bump math lands the first real release at `0.1.0`, matching the version already committed in `philogg.html`/`desktop/package.json`/`desktop/src-tauri/{tauri.conf.json,Cargo.toml}`. `PROJECT.md` → "Release builds" updated to note this. No test changes: version-tracking metadata only, outside both the regression suite and `desktop/`'s own build.
- **chore: `release-please.yml`'s `build_desktop` job now builds only Windows (installer + portable), macOS and Linux dropped from the matrix** (this session, 2026-09-17, person-requested: only ship HTML/Windows/Windows-portable builds off a cut release for now). `build_desktop`'s `strategy.matrix.os` (was `[windows-latest, macos-latest, ubuntu-22.04]`) is gone in favor of a plain `runs-on: windows-latest`; the Linux-only apt-get dependency install step and the now-dead `runner.os == 'Windows'`/multi-OS branching in the stamp and upload steps were removed along with it (the upload loop only globs `nsis/*.exe` now, `dmg`/`appimage` dropped). `build_html` (the standalone HTML asset) is unaffected. `PROJECT.md` → "Release builds" updated to reflect the reduced matrix. No test changes: this is a GitHub Actions workflow file, outside both the regression suite and `desktop/`'s own Rust/Tauri build.
- **fix: `VsInstance`'s `solution_path` field serialized as `solution_path`, not `solutionPath` — the Settings dialog always read `undefined` and showed "no solution open" no matter what the PowerShell/COM side actually found** (this session, 2026-09-17, person-tested: after every PowerShell/COM fix in this story checked out — confirmed via three separate standalone-script reproductions, including one byte-for-byte matching the app's exact `-EncodedCommand`/`-NonInteractive`/`CreateNoWindow` invocation, and even a 32-bit-vs-64-bit-process theory ruled out by checking Task Manager — "Connect" in the real app still showed "(no solution open)" for an instance whose solution the standalone script printed correctly. Every one of those tests exercised the PowerShell/C# side in isolation; none of them ever went through the actual Rust struct that gets serialized back to the page, which is exactly where the bug was hiding.). `VsInstance`'s `solution_path` field had no `#[serde(rename)]` (unlike the intermediate `RawInstance` used to *parse* PowerShell's own JSON, which already had `#[serde(rename = "solutionPath")]` for the same reason) — so Tauri serialized it to the page as `{"solution_path": "..."}`, and `philogg.html`'s `inst.solutionPath` read was always `undefined`, always falsy, always "(no solution open)" regardless of what PowerShell actually reported. One-line fix: `#[serde(rename = "solutionPath")]` on `VsInstance.solution_path`, matching `RawInstance`'s own convention. A good reminder that a bug can survive extensive testing of everything **except** the exact boundary it's actually sitting on. No test changes (this field only exists on the Windows-only side of the Rust↔JS boundary, outside jsdom's reach). Full suite unaffected: **4806 passed, 0 failed**.
- **fix: retrieve each Visual Studio instance's DTE object via `IRunningObjectTable.GetObject` (in C#), not `[Marshal]::BindToMoniker` (from PowerShell) — the latter fails with `MK_E_SYNTAX` on this moniker shape** (this session, 2026-09-16, person-tested: to iterate faster than a full Tauri rebuild per attempt, this session handed the person two standalone `.ps1` scripts mirroring `vs_integration.rs`'s embedded logic 1:1 — run directly via `powershell -File`, no build in the loop. That confirmed the ROT walk itself now works (`!VisualStudio.DTE.17.0:27228` found, matching the person's actually-open solution) but binding to it failed: `Exception calling "BindToMoniker" with "1" argument(s): "Invalid syntax (Exception from HRESULT: 0x800401E4 (MK_E_SYNTAX))"`). `[Marshal]::BindToMoniker(name)` re-parses the moniker from its display-name *string* via `MkParseDisplayName`, and a bare item-moniker fragment like `!VisualStudio.DTE.17.0:1234` isn't something that parser grammar understands standalone. The actually-correct way to turn an *already-enumerated* `IMoniker` into its live object was `IRunningObjectTable.GetObject(moniker)` all along — the same method a previous session in this story reached for and then reverted, only because calling `.GetObject(...)` directly on `$rot` *from PowerShell* doesn't work (the `IDispatch` problem two fixes ago). The fix combines both lessons: `GetObject` now runs **inside the C# class** (compile-time typed, no `IDispatch` issue) alongside the ROT walk, bundling each match's moniker string and its live `EnvDTE.DTE` object into a plain `PhiloggVsHandle { Moniker, Dte }` returned to PowerShell — which then reads `.Dte.Solution.FullName`/`.Dte.MainWindow.Caption`/etc. directly, since `EnvDTE.DTE` itself genuinely is Automation/`IDispatch`-compatible (that part was never the problem). `open_file`'s script gets its own small `GetVsDte(moniker)` C# method doing the same re-enumerate-and-`GetObject` lookup for one specific instance. Both standalone test scripts confirmed working on a real machine (Visual Studio 2022) before this got ported back into `vs_integration.rs`. `docs/desktop.md` → "IDE Integration" updated in place. No test changes (Windows/PowerShell-only). Full suite unaffected: **4806 passed, 0 failed**.
- **fix: `IRunningObjectTable.EnumRunning`'s C# signature was wrong — `void EnumRunning(out IEnumMoniker)`, not `IEnumMoniker EnumRunning()`** (this session, 2026-09-16, immediate follow-up to the fix below, person-tested: moving the ROT walk into C# got past the previous error but the embedded class failed to compile with "No overload for method 'EnumRunning' takes 0 arguments"). Verified against the actual .NET reference source this time rather than recalling it from memory: unlike `GetRunningObjectTable`/`CreateBindCtx` (plain `ole32.dll` P/Invokes returning a raw HRESULT `int`), `IRunningObjectTable.EnumRunning` is a COM-interop interface method with no `[PreserveSig]` — it takes the `IEnumMoniker` as an `out` parameter and returns `void`, with the CLR checking the HRESULT itself and throwing a `COMException` on failure. `vs_integration.rs`'s embedded `PhiloggRot.ListVsMonikers()` now calls it as `rot.EnumRunning(out enumMoniker)`. No test changes (Windows/PowerShell-only). Full suite unaffected: **4806 passed, 0 failed**.
- **fix: Visual Studio "Connect" still failed — PowerShell can't call methods on the raw COM interfaces the Running Object Table walk needs** (this session, 2026-09-16, person-tested: Rider now works after the previous fix, but "Connect" returned the exact PowerShell error `[System.__ComObject] does not contain a method named 'EnumRunning'` — the diagnostic-surfacing work from the earlier session in this same story did exactly its job, handing back a concrete, root-causable error instead of a generic "not found"). The cause: PowerShell's own dynamic (`$obj.Method()`) dispatch on a COM object only works for Automation/`IDispatch`-compatible interfaces; `IRunningObjectTable`, `IBindCtx`, `IEnumMoniker`, and `IMoniker` are plain vtable-only interfaces with no `IDispatch` support at all, so `$rot.EnumRunning()`/`$rot.GetObject($m)` (the latter added by the previous session's own hardening pass, believing it more standard/robust than `[Marshal]::BindToMoniker`) could never work from PowerShell script text, regardless of how correctly the P/Invoke signatures were declared — a COM object without a registered interop class reports its runtime type as the generic `System.__ComObject` to PowerShell's reflection-based method lookup, which then has no `IDispatch.Invoke` to fall back to. This is a PowerShell-specific limitation that doesn't affect compiled C# (where `.EnumRunning()` resolves at compile time against the *declared* interface type and never needs reflection) — every reference example for this technique found via research is C#, and none of it warned that it wouldn't translate 1:1 into a PowerShell script. Fixed in `desktop/src-tauri/src/vs_integration.rs` by moving the entire ROT walk (`GetRunningObjectTable`/`CreateBindCtx`/`EnumRunning`/`Next`/`GetDisplayName`) into a new static method on the embedded, compile-time-typed C# class (`ListVsMonikers()`), which returns only plain moniker **strings** — the one thing PowerShell can safely receive back from it. PowerShell then binds each moniker via `[Marshal]::BindToMoniker` (a plain static .NET method, not a COM call — this part was fine all along, and is exactly what the *original* version of this script did before the previous session "corrected" it to `GetObject`) and reads `Solution.FullName`/`MainWindow.Caption` off the resulting `EnvDTE.DTE`, which is itself Automation/`IDispatch`-compatible by design and works fine from PowerShell. `open_file`'s script no longer needs to re-enumerate the ROT at all (the moniker string is already known), removing the shared `ROT_SETUP` fragment and its duplication between the two scripts entirely. `docs/desktop.md` → "IDE Integration" updated in place to describe the corrected split and explain why it's necessary. No test changes: this is Windows/PowerShell-only, outside jsdom's reach. Full suite: **4806 passed, 0 failed** (unchanged — no JS touched).
- **fix: IDE Integration's anchor mismatch duplicated a path segment for Visual Studio and included one it shouldn't for Rider, the anchor was only an exact folder name with no way to disambiguate, and Rider's line number was off by one** (this session, 2026-09-16, person-tested: got "Open in Rider" working by hand via Win+R with `jetbrains://rider/navigate/reference?project=MyCompany.App&path=MyCompany.Controller.Scripting/ScriptingService.cs:1` for a log entry located at `D:\ThisUser\dev\myRepo\Code\Projects\MyCompany.Controller.Scripting\ScriptingService.cs line 1`, then reported line 1 opened at line 2, 15 at 16, and so on). That confirmed two things the original design got wrong. **(1) Anchor semantics**: the very first design discussion's own example had the solution sitting *directly inside* the anchor folder (`...\Code\Projects\myProject.sln`) — i.e. the anchor **is** the local solution/project directory, not its parent — but `resolveIdeSourcePath` kept the anchor segment itself as part of the relative path it joined onto that directory. For Visual Studio this only happened to produce the same total string as before when the anchor occurred exactly once (never actually exercised on a real machine yet); for Rider it produced `Projects/MyCompany.Controller.Scripting/ScriptingService.cs` where the confirmed-working link needed `MyCompany.Controller.Scripting/ScriptingService.cs` — no `Projects`. Fixed by joining only what comes AFTER the anchor match, never the match itself. **(2) A real pattern, not just a folder name** (person-requested: "an actual pattern like used in other places of the app, so it could handle something like 'Projects' being twice within the full path"): the anchor setting ("Path anchor pattern" now, was "Path anchor folder") is compiled by a new `compileIdeAnchorPattern` using the exact same `*`/`?` convention `compileGlob` already uses for folder-watch's file-to-format matching (`*` = any run of characters, `?` = any one character, everything else literal; a literal `\`/`/` in the pattern matches either separator) — reused rather than inventing new syntax — matched as a substring anywhere in the path (not anchored to a whole segment), so a longer pattern like `Code\Projects` can disambiguate a folder name that occurs more than once, where the old exact-segment "last occurrence wins" heuristic had no way to. **(3) Rider's line number is 0-based**, confirmed by the person's own report — `parseIdeLocation`'s 1-based line (matching Visual Studio's own `GotoLine` contract) is unchanged; `buildRiderUri` now subtracts 1 (clamped to 0) only when building the URI string. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 230** reworked in place (not just extended): the motivating example with the corrected solution-directory understanding, a sanity check the anchor is never duplicated, a "tricky" fixture proving a bare anchor name picks the wrong (coincidental, deeper) occurrence while a longer pattern disambiguates correctly (either slash style), a `*` wildcard, and the exact real-world Rider path from this report confirming the excluded-anchor join; `buildRiderUri`'s cases updated for the 0-based line, including the exact reported "line 1 → :0" case. `docs/desktop.md` → "IDE Integration" updated in place for both the pattern convention and the 0-based line note. Full suite: **4806 passed, 0 failed**.
- **fix: IDE Integration's Visual Studio "Connect" swallowed every failure into the same generic "no instance found," and "Open in Rider" gave no feedback at all** (this session, 2026-09-16, person-reported after the very first real-machine test of the feature above: Visual Studio was running with a solution open and "Connect…" still reported nothing found; enabling Rider and clicking "Open in Rider" on a log row did nothing, no error). This sandbox has no Windows toolchain, so the diagnosis leaned on external research into both failure modes rather than a live repro. **Visual Studio**: `vs_integration.rs`'s PowerShell script never checked the HRESULT either `ole32.dll` P/Invoke call (`GetRunningObjectTable`/`CreateBindCtx`) returned, and any failure anywhere in the script came back as the exact same empty result as "Visual Studio really isn't running" — indistinguishable by design ("never fail outward"), which meant a genuine script bug and a clean "zero found" looked identical to the Settings dialog. Both scripts now wrap their whole body in one top-level `try`/`catch` that checks those HRESULTs explicitly and writes the real exception message to stderr (`exit 1`) instead of letting an unhandled COM/.NET exception produce an opaque result; `list_instances()`'s Tauri command (`vs_list_instances`) now returns `VsListResult { instances, error }` instead of a bare list, and the Settings dialog's "Connect…" handler shows that diagnostic text directly ("No running Visual Studio instance found — &lt;the script's own error&gt;") the next time it comes back empty. Also switched both scripts from retrieving the DTE object via `[Marshal]::BindToMoniker($name)` (a re-parse of the moniker's display-name string) to `$rot.GetObject($m)` on the already-enumerated moniker object directly — the more standard technique per the research done this session, and one fewer thing that can go wrong. **Rider**: re-verified `buildRiderUri`/`openEntryInRider` and the `open_path` bridge — both structurally correct, and (per `tauri-plugin-opener`'s own source) `open_path`/`open_url` delegate to the identical underlying OS-open call on desktop when invoked from Rust directly, so there's no obvious PhiLogg-side bug. Research strongly suggests the actual cause is that the `jetbrains://` URI scheme's registration in Windows is owned by JetBrains Toolbox App's `jetbrainsd` background service rather than a standalone Rider install — not something PhiLogg's own code can fix. Added a success toast ("Opening in Rider…") after `openPath` resolves, so a person can at least tell PhiLogg's own side ran, and documented the Toolbox prerequisite plus a Win+R self-test in `docs/desktop.md`/`desktop/README.md`. **Still unverified against a real Windows machine** (same sandbox limitation as the feature's original session) — see `desktop/README.md` → "Status". No test changes: the pure remap/URI functions (Group 230) are untouched, and the PowerShell/COM hardening is still Windows-desktop-only, outside jsdom's reach. Full suite: **4800 passed, 0 failed**.
- **feat: jump from a log entry's Location straight into a running IDE — Visual Studio (Windows-only, live "Connect" instance picker) and Rider (fast-follow via a `jetbrains://` deep link)** (this session, 2026-09-16, discussed and scoped with the project owner before implementation: build Visual Studio first with a real per-instance picker, keep Rider lighter with no picker, Windows-only for both). New Settings → **IDE Integration** section (hidden entirely outside a Windows desktop build): a shared **"Path anchor folder"** setting (e.g. `Projects`) plus per-IDE controls. `resolveIdeSourcePath` (next to `formatLocation`) bridges the log's own possibly-stale absolute path onto a person's local checkout by finding the LAST path segment matching that anchor folder and joining everything from there onto the target IDE's own solution/project directory — the log's default path (e.g. `C:\git\myProject\Code\Projects\MyCompany.Core\...`) and the local one (e.g. `D:\dev\myProject\Code\Projects\MyCompany.Core\...`) only need to agree from the anchor onward. **Visual Studio**: a new Rust module `desktop/src-tauri/src/vs_integration.rs` shells a small embedded PowerShell script (`Add-Type -TypeDefinition`, P/Invoking `ole32.dll`'s `GetRunningObjectTable`) to enumerate every running `devenv.exe`'s Running Object Table entry, reading each one's open solution path via `Marshal.BindToMoniker` + `EnvDTE.DTE`; the Settings dialog's "Connect…" button lists them, and the chosen instance's moniker + solution directory are kept in a session-only JS variable (never persisted — a stale PID/moniker is meaningless after a restart). "Open in Visual Studio" (new context-menu item, shown whenever the row's Location resolves against the connected instance) shells a second script that opens the file and moves the caret to the right line (`OpenFile`/`Selection.GotoLine`/`MainWindow.Activate()`); the log-derived path/line/moniker travel through environment variables on the spawned process, never interpolated into the (fixed, `-EncodedCommand`-sent) script text. **Rider**: no connection step at all — `buildRiderUri` builds JetBrains' own documented `jetbrains://rider/navigate/reference?project=...&path=...:<line>` deep link from the same remap and hands it to the existing `window.philogg.openPath` bridge (the IDE itself resolves which running instance/project to focus); Settings just needs an enable toggle + the project name the link's `project=` parameter needs. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 230** covers the pure remap/URI-building functions (`parseIdeLocation`, `resolveIdeSourcePath`, `buildRiderUri`) — anchor-folder matching (including case-insensitivity and "last occurrence wins" when the name appears twice), missing-anchor/missing-line error paths, and URI percent-encoding; Groups 79 and 223 updated in place for the new Settings nav item and the two new context-menu items. The Visual Studio COM/PowerShell side, the Settings Connect flow, and the `jetbrains://` link itself are Windows-desktop-only and still need manual verification on a real machine (see `desktop/README.md` → "Status") — this session's sandbox had neither a Windows toolchain nor the system packages to even build the Linux desktop wrapper. See `docs/desktop.md` → "IDE Integration" for the full mechanism. Full suite: **4800 passed, 0 failed**.
- **feat: 3D plot colorbar now matches 2D's full-plot-height one, plus selectable colormaps for both** (this session, 2026-09-16, person-requested: *"Die colormap der 2D Plots erstreckt sich über die gesamte Fensterhöhe. Beim 3D Plot ist die Leiste deutlich kleiner. Gleiche die Darstellung in 3D an die der 2D Plots an. Zusätzlich für weitere colormaps ein zwischen denen man wählen kann. Die aktuelle weil die Default Einstellung bleiben."*). Two changes to the scatter/3D "color by value" dimension. **(1) Colorbar height**: `renderPlot3D()`'s legend strip was capped at `Math.min(140, cssH - 32)` — visibly shorter than 2D's, which has always spanned the full plot height (`legendH = plotH`). Dropped the cap (`legendH = Math.max(20, cssH - 32)`), so 3D's colorbar now spans the chart area the same way 2D's does. Its canvas gradient also grew from 3 hardcoded `addColorStop` calls to 10, so a longer colormap (see below) reads correctly across the whole strip instead of just at its 0/50/100% points. **(2) Colormap picker**: `plotColorScale(t)` used one hardcoded blue→yellow→red gradient; it's now `plotColorScale(t, mapId)`, reading from a new `PLOT_COLOR_MAPS` table (`default` — the exact pre-existing scale, kept as the default so nothing changes for a plot that never touches the new control — plus Viridis, Plasma, Cool–Warm, Grayscale, Turbo). A new **Colormap** dropdown (`#plotColorMapSelect`, `plotConfig.colorMap`) appears next to the existing "Color by (optional)" column select on both 2D scatter and 3D — shared markup, since both chart types already read the same `plotColorScale`. Threaded through `defaultPlotConfig()`/`sanitizePlotConfig()` like every other `plotConfig` field, so it rides along through all four existing persistence carriers (copy/paste, undo/redo, filter save/load, session cache) with no extra carrier-specific work. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 229**: the picker's option list against `PLOT_COLOR_MAPS`' own keys and its "default" default; the 2D legend's 24-stop gradient and a mark's own fill before/after switching colormap, plus the legend still spanning the full plot height (y=20..348); and, for 3D (via a small recording mock installed in place of `#plot3dCanvas`'s real 2d context, since jsdom has none), the colorbar's `fillRect` height now `Math.max(20, cssH-32)` instead of capped at 140, an 11-stop gradient, and both the colorbar and the max-value point's fill switching together with the dropdown while the colorbar's own height stays unaffected by which colormap is picked. See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Scatter" and "3D scatter". Full suite: **4778 passed, 0 failed**. **Follow-up, same session (person-requested: "Macht die Anzeige des Dropdowns davon abhängig, ob... Color by... was anderes als None ist. Also nur wenn du etwas ausgewählt hast, soll auch Colormap erscheinen")**: the Colormap picker was shown unconditionally once a chart type offered "Color by" at all, implying it did something even with Color by left at None. `renderPlotControls` now builds it only when `plotConfig.colorCol != null` (empty string otherwise), and `#plotColorSelect`'s own change handler calls `renderPlotControls()` (not just `renderPlotChart()`) so the picker appears/disappears immediately as Color by is set/cleared — the underlying `plotConfig.colorMap` choice itself stays remembered while hidden, same as any other plotConfig field. **Group 229** extended: the picker is absent right after picking a chart type (Color by still None), appears the instant a color-by column is chosen, and disappears again — with `colorMap` unchanged underneath — when Color by is reset to None; same coverage added for 3D.
