- **feat: configurable "start filters" for folder auto-open, several selectable with one Primary (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #71)** (this session, 2026-09-16). A watched folder's per-pattern rules (`docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "Per-folder settings", `defaultFolderPattern`/`applyFolderAutoRules`) already decide WHICH file a pattern opens on its own — "Auto-open newest file", "Auto-close — keep only N open". This session adds a fourth per-pattern setting, **"Start filters"**, that decides what happens right after: a checkbox list in the folder watch settings dialog (`#fwPatternList`, `fwPatternStartFiltersBlock`) lets a pattern reference any number of saved **Filter Library** presets (the existing named, file-agnostic filter presets from `saveFilterToLibrary`/`listFilterLibrary` — see the "Filter library" section) to apply automatically the moment that pattern's own auto-open/auto-close rule opens a file, with one of the checked presets marked **Primary** (a same-`name`-grouped native radio per pattern card, disabled until its own checkbox is checked) — person-requested, so opening a file that gets several filters applied at once still lands the person's view on one specific filter of their choosing instead of an arbitrary one. Person-requested framing throughout: the Filter Library, not a fresh ad-hoc filter editor, is the source for this selection — reusing it means no new storage tier and no new "apply" mechanism, just `applyFilterFromLibrary` invoked once per selected key, one step earlier in the same flow the right-click "Apply from library…" action already uses, so every filter re-evaluates against the newly opened file's own entries exactly like every other library application. A pattern's `startFilterKeys` (`[]` by default) are looked up and applied inside `loadFolderFile`'s existing `auto` branch (new helper `applyFolderStartFilter`), so they fire only for a file THIS RULE opened — never for one a person opens by double-click/context-menu, mirroring the "person vs. auto" distinction `rec.openedByAuto`/`rec.autoOpenFired` already draw for auto-close; `startPrimaryFilterKey` (`null` by default) is applied LAST among them, so its own `applyFilterFromLibrary` call is what actually sets `state.activeId`/reveals the view. The dialog keeps the invariant "a non-empty selection always has exactly one Primary" on its own — checking a pattern's first preset auto-picks it as Primary, unchecking the current Primary auto-promotes another still-checked preset — so there's no separate "no Primary chosen" state to stumble into by hand. Both fields travel with the rest of `folder.settings` through the existing `persistFolder`/`restoreWatchedFolders` round trip, so no new persistence carrier was needed. The folder watch settings dialog has always opened and rendered its pattern cards synchronously (no `await` between the gear click and the cards appearing), so the new checkbox list can't read the library through the always-async `listFilterLibrary()` directly without reopening that gap; it reads a new in-memory mirror instead, `filterLibraryCache`, refreshed by `refreshFilterLibraryCache()` at boot and inside all three library mutators (`saveFilterToLibrary`/`updateFilterLibraryEntry`/`deleteFilterLibraryEntry`). See `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "Per-folder settings"/"Storage" for both; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` GROUP 228 covers the checkbox list, the auto-managed Primary invariant, applying several filters onto an auto-opened file with the Primary one ending up active, and the auto-vs-manual-open distinction.

- **feat: a TIME-based unit for the Context view's existing "expansion step" (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #79, scope narrowed by the project owner)** (this session, 2026-09-16). `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #79's own raw text sketched a much broader, unresolved idea — making the whole Context view's visible window itself configurable, as a lighter alternative to the `context`/`countContext` filter-node types. Asked to resolve that entry's own open question, the project owner gave a concrete, narrower answer instead: **not** a new view-level window concept, but a unit toggle on the mechanism the Context view already has — "Lines revealed per step" (`contextExpandStep`, see `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Revealing a step at a time"), which governs both a "Show more (+n)" click's own reveal size and the width of the auto-expansion window around a jump (`applyContextJumpExpansion`'s `"aroundJump"` mode). That mechanism has always measured a step in a fixed ENTRY COUNT; this session adds **Settings → Behavior → "Expand step counts in"** (`contextExpandStepUnit`, `philogg-context-expand-step-unit`, `"entries"` **default**, preserving today's behavior for anyone who never touches the new setting, | `"time"`), which switches it to a fixed TIME SPAN instead — **"Timespan revealed per step (ms)"** (`contextExpandStepMs`, `philogg-context-expand-step-ms`, default **500**, clamped to a minimum of 1 with no upper cap, same as the Time-context-filter dialog's own ms fields). In time mode, a "Show more" click reveals however many entries fall within the configured span from whichever edge it sits against — a genuinely **variable** entry count, since how many lines a fixed timespan covers depends entirely on how dense the log is at that edge — and its own label/tooltip read the fixed configured *duration* ("Show more (+500ms)", via the existing `formatMs` helper) rather than that variable count, which would promise a precision the setting doesn't actually offer. `buildContextView`'s `addHidden` computes a time-mode step's two candidate boundaries via `lowerBoundByTs`/`upperBoundByTs` — the same binary-search helpers the minimap and the Time-context filter already use — clamped so a step always reveals **at least one entry** and never crosses into rows a different run already owns; whether the step row is shown at all is derived from those same two boundaries (would this step reveal the ENTIRE remainder anyway?) rather than a separately-computed check, so the two can never disagree at an edge. The **"… N lines" filler is deliberately unchanged in both modes** — per the project owner's explicit instruction ("if less than that is available just the '...n line' as by now") it always reports a plain entry count, since it reveals the whole remaining stretch regardless of how that stretch's edges were measured. The **entries-mode path is untouched**: `addHidden`'s `"entries"` branch and `applyContextJumpExpansion`'s `lo = idx - contextExpandStep; hi = idx + contextExpandStep + 1;` are exactly the pre-existing logic, gated behind `contextExpandStepUnit !== "time"`. Both new values are plain `localStorage` UI preferences, same tier as `contextExpandStep` itself — **not** filter-node fields, so no persistence-carrier threading was needed (`cloneSubtree`/`snapshotSubtree`/`serializeFilterBranch`/session-cache — see `CLAUDE.md`'s "Known gotchas"). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 227**: the Settings unit select's two dependent step-width rows (each needing its own scoped `.hidden{display:none}` CSS rule — this codebase has no generic one) show/hide and persist correctly in both directions, including re-hydration from `localStorage`; the ms field's Dec/Inc/typed-value/clamp behavior, mirroring the existing entries-step field's own coverage; and, via a 71-entry fixture with a sparse stretch on one side of a jump target and a dense one on the other, that the SAME configured 500ms step reveals a starkly different entry count on each side (1 vs. 50) — the whole point of time mode — plus the "reveal at least one entry" clamp, a step correctly withheld when it would reveal everything left, and that `applyContextJumpExpansion`'s `contextAutoRanges` take-back-the-previous-jump mechanism (Group 138o) still leaves a hand-revealed range in a different gap untouched. `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Context view (Context/Filtered split)" extended in place ("Revealing a step at a time", "Jumping in still works exactly as before"). Full suite: **4733 passed, 0 failed**.
- **feat: entries-based (line-count) binning for the timeline minimap, as an alternative to the existing time-based layout** (this session, 2026-09-16, implements `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #2, person-approved design — an earlier draft of this same idea wasn't liked and was shelved pending a better approach; this session's design was worked out and confirmed with the project owner before implementation). **Problem**: the minimap's bars have always been positioned by proportion of the file's *timestamp span* — a burst of fast activity (many entries in a tight time window) compresses into a handful of pixels, while a quiet/idle stretch (few entries over a long wall-clock gap) silently eats most of the width, even though it's the least interesting part of the file. **New Settings → Timeline minimap → "Minimap binning"** select (`time` | `entries`, default `time`, `philogg-minimap-binning-mode`) switches a bar's x-position from "proportional to timestamp within the file's time span" to "proportional to entry INDEX among the root file's entries" instead — bursts now spread out evenly across the width, and idle stretches stop dominating it. **Scope, confirmed explicitly by the project owner: visualization only.** Every interaction — click-to-jump, drag-select (→ a `"timerange"` filter), the right-click time-range dialog, the two range-indicator rectangles, the hover tooltip, the selection marker — needed **zero changes of its own**, because none of them contain their own notion of "where in time/space is this pixel"; they all already routed through three low-level conversion functions, which are now the ONLY place `minimapBinningMode` is ever branched on: `minimapTsToX(ts)`/`minimapXToTs(x)` (the position pair) and the newly-factored-out `minimapBucketOf(ts)` (which bucket/bar a timestamp falls into — previously inlined separately inside `minimapBarSpan` and the render pass's own local `bucketOf` closure, now a single shared function both call, so they can never disagree with each other or with the new mode). In `"entries"` mode, `minimapXToTs` resolves a clicked/dragged pixel to the **real timestamp of the actual entry** at that index (`minimapRootEntries[idx].ts`, binary-searched via the same `lowerBoundByTs` helper used everywhere else in the file for a chronological array) rather than an interpolated point in time — this is precisely why every existing caller above the conversion layer kept working unchanged: they already handled "some entry's real ts" correctly, and now just get a more exact one instead of an approximation. `renderTimelineMinimap`'s own `bgCounts`/`ovCounts` bucketing passes got the same mode branch, but inline their own index-based formula (array position for the whole-root-file pass, `buildOrderIndexMap(rootId)` — already memoized per file — for the filtered `viewEntries` pass, since a `viewEntries` entry's array index is "Nth match", not "Nth line in the file") rather than calling `minimapBucketOf` per entry: that function's `"entries"` branch does a binary search per call, the right cost for the O(1)-ish click/hover/drag handlers, but calling it inside this O(file size) render pass — which runs on every node/filter switch, level toggle, and tail tick — would have turned it into O(file size · log file size). Both still compute the exact same formula, so the hot path and the interaction layer can never disagree on which bucket a ts belongs to. The hover tooltip's `mousemove` listener, which used to re-derive the time-proportion formula by hand (`minimapTMin + (b / bucketCount) * (minimapTMax - minimapTMin)`) instead of calling the shared functions, was rewritten to route through `minimapXToTs`/`minimapBucketOf` too — that duplication would otherwise have silently stayed correct only in time mode. **Expected side-effect of `"entries"` mode, not a bug**: the grey BACKGROUND density bars render essentially flat/uniform, since every bucket now covers the same fixed slice of the file's line count by construction rather than a data-dependent slice of wall-clock time — informative time-based background density, by design, has nothing left to vary. The value in this mode is entirely in the colored overlay bars, which still show where in the file's line order the current filtered/leveled result concentrates. `minimapBinningMode` is a plain `localStorage` UI preference (same tier as `hideMinimapFullRangeInFullView`/`contextExpandStep`), **not** a filter-node field, so no persistence-carrier threading was needed (`cloneSubtree`/`snapshotSubtree`/`serializeFilterBranch`/session-cache — see `CLAUDE.md`'s "Known gotchas"). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 226**: the Settings select's persistence to `localStorage` and re-hydration on (re-)init; an irregular-gap fixture (a 20-entry burst one millisecond apart, then a 20-minute idle gap, then 19 more entries a second apart) proving `minimapTsToX` resolves the post-gap entry's x by time-proportion (~98%, dominated by the idle gap) in time mode and by index-proportion (~51%, entry 20 of 39) in entries mode — a visibly different x for the exact same entry — and that the actual rendered SVG bar for a lone matched entry lands at the same index-proportional bucket `minimapBucketOf` computes; and that click-to-jump, drag-select (→ a `"timerange"` filter built from two real entries' own timestamps), and the hover tooltip all still resolve correctly with entries mode active. Every existing minimap group (31/32/34/95/119/120/197/...) keeps passing unchanged, since `"time"` stays the default. See `docs/filters.md` → "Timeline minimap: bars, range indicators, selection markers" for the full mechanism. Full suite: **4701 passed, 0 failed**.
- **Fix: the "›" separator between filter path pills (`#breadcrumb`/`#filterTargetChain`) looked small, lost and unaligned** (this session, 2026-09-16, person-reported, screenshot: a log-file pill, a bare small arrow character, then a "TEST" filter pill — the separator barely registered against `--text-tertiary`'s low contrast and read as visually detached from the pills either side). `.crumb-sep` is now a fixed 18x18 bordered circle (`--bg-elevated-2` fill, `--border-soft` border, bold `--accent-strong` glyph) instead of plain inline text sized off an 11px font — a distinct connector element with its own visible boundary, matching the pills' own rounded shape language, rather than a stray character floating in the gap. Its margin-top re-centers that fixed-size circle on the flanking pill's own center in each context (`5px` base, `7px` inside `#breadcrumbBar` — same "match the pill's own margin-derived center" approach the existing rules already used, just simplified since a fixed-size box no longer needs its offset re-derived from a line-height sum every time the pill's own margin changes). Same shared CSS class powers both `#breadcrumb` and the "Add filter" popup's `#filterTargetChain`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 225** (numbered 225, not 224, to avoid colliding with the unrelated Show-Notes-auto-enable Group 224 merged into `main` the same day): shape/centering via computed style, plus a stylesheet-source check that the new color/background/border are actually declared (jsdom can't resolve `var()` inside the `background`/`border` shorthands used here). See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "`#breadcrumbBar`". Full suite: **4663 passed, 0 failed**. **Follow-up, same session (person-reported via screenshot: "das symbol ist nicht zentriert" — with the new circle in place, the "›" glyph itself still looked off-center inside it)**: measured directly (`Range.getBoundingClientRect()` on the text node) — the glyph's own advance-width box WAS centered by the container's flexbox, but "›"'s visible ink sits left-of-center within that box, an ordinary font right-side-bearing quirk baked into the glyph itself (varies by font/OS) that no amount of container margin/padding tuning can fix. Swapped the text glyph for `ICON_CARET_RIGHT` — the same hand-drawn SVG chevron already used for the Context view's match nav, authored symmetric inside its own viewBox and therefore centered by construction regardless of font. `renderBreadcrumb`/`renderFilterTargetChain` now set the separator's `.innerHTML` instead of `.textContent`; `.crumb-sep`'s CSS lost its now-pointless font-size/font-weight/line-height (an icon has none of those), keeping only `color` to drive the icon's `stroke="currentColor"`. **Group 225** updated in place to assert on the SVG (its exact `path[d]`, `stroke="currentColor"`) instead of the retired text glyph. - **Fix: the Files & Filters sidebar toolbar's "Edit filter…" button (and every other action on it) did nothing when clicked** (this session, 2026-09-15, person-reported: "Der Edit Filter Button löst aktuell keine Aktion aus. Erwartungshaltung: Filter Edit Popup öffnet sich"). Root cause: `#sidebarToolbar`'s click listener dispatched to `handleSidebarToolbarActionClick` without calling `ev.stopPropagation()` first, unlike the pre-existing `[data-row-actions]` delegated listener it was modeled on (`#viewBar`/the per-view toolbars). The same click that opened `#filterPopup` (via `editFilterNode` → `openEditFilterPopup`) kept bubbling up to the document-level "click outside a popup closes it" listener, which saw a click outside the popup that had *just* opened and closed it again in the same tick — an instance of `CLAUDE.md`'s own "stopPropagation on any click handler that opens a popup" gotcha, missed for this one new listener when it was added. One-line fix: `ev.stopPropagation()` added to `#sidebarToolbar`'s click listener. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 222**: a real, bubbling `fireClick()` on the actual "Edit filter…" button confirms `#filterPopup` stays open afterward (pre-existing Group 220i only ever called `handleSidebarToolbarActionClick` directly, which never bubbles and so could never have caught this class of bug) — confirmed to actually fail without the fix. - **feat: give the Files & Filters sidebar its own selection-dependent toolbar** (this session, 2026-09-15, implements `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #77; revised in place the same session, before ever shipping, per project-owner review — this entry describes the FINAL shape directly). New `#sidebarToolbar`, sitting between `#sidebarHeader` and the scrollable `#sidebarScroll` (always visible once a file is loaded, never scrolled away with the tree), uses the same icon+hover-label button concept as `#viewBar`/the per-view toolbars — the **"rect"** shape specifically (the per-view toolbars' own look, not `#viewBar`'s pill/circle), so it also follows the "View Toolbar button labels" Settings toggle. Its button SET tracks the current tree selection via `describeSidebarToolbarActions()`, grouped into **at most two `|`-separated groups** — "what this node itself looks like" then "library": **1 file** → Adjust clock… (omitted for a merged/empty file) `|` Apply from library…; **2+ files** → Merge `|` Apply from library… (falls back to `state.activeId`, no single node to target); **1 unlocked filter** → Rename…, Edit filter… (per-filterType), Invert (NOT) `|` Add to library…, Apply from library… (both target that specific node); **1 filter, the locked "Bookmarks" node** → nothing in group 1 (Copy is gone from the toolbar, see below) — just Apply from library…, disabled; **2+ filters, same root** → Combine (AND), OR, Link… only, no library group; **different roots / mixed** → the existing explanatory note, zero actions; **0 selected** → Apply from library… only (`state.activeId` fallback). `handleSidebarToolbarActionClick(action)` dispatches every click into the pre-existing function each action already had — zero duplicated business logic. **`copy`/`cut`/`saveFilter`/`loadFilter` are removed from the toolbar entirely, for every selection shape** — unaffected on the tree's right-click menu. **`context`/`countContext` (Time context…/Count context…) are removed from the toolbar AND from the right-click menu** (`openTreeContextMenu`'s edit bucket no longer pushes them) — there is now no user-facing way to CREATE a fresh context/countContext node at all (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #79 has the reasoning + a planned replacement); existing nodes of either type — from an old session, a saved filter file, or a library preset — are completely unaffected, since only the two creation menu items were touched (`openContextDialog`/`openCountContextDialog`, the dialogs, and every `context`/`countContext` branch in `getEntries`/rendering/every persistence carrier stay fully intact, just unreferenced by any button for now). **New `addToLibrary` action + the pre-existing `applyFromLibrary` replace `#viewBar`'s dedicated `#btnAddToLibrary`/`#btnOpenLibrary` buttons** (`#libraryManageBar`, removed outright from `#viewBar`'s markup/CSS/JS) — same dialogs (`openFilterLibrarySaveDialog`/`openFilterLibraryDialog`)/icons (`ICON_DISK`/`ICON_BOOK`), now targeting the *specific selected node* instead of always `state.activeId`; `#libraryPresetBar` (pinned preset pills) is untouched. **AND/OR/Link… stay EXCLUSIVE to this toolbar** (person-confirmed, unchanged from the initial pass): removed from the old always-visible `#treeActionBar`/`renderTreeActionBar` (retired outright, its Merge case folded into this toolbar's own "2+ files" case) and from `openTreeContextMenu`'s own 2+ multi-select bulk-action rendering (Merge stays there, unaffected). **`setupHitExpandGroups` re-bind gotcha**: every pre-existing caller renders its buttons once at boot; this toolbar rebuilds its whole button set on every render and must call it again each time, which would otherwise attach a stacking, duplicate `mousemove`/`mouseleave` listener onto the same container — fixed with a small idempotency guard (`container.dataset.hitExpandBound`) plus switching the listener to re-query the container's current children on every event instead of closing over a bind-time snapshot. **Edge-of-viewport label clamping** (project-owner review): `#sidebarToolbar` sits at the app's LEFT edge, so the shared `.row-action-label` floating-pill mechanic (centered via `translateX(-50%)`, no bounds checking) could clip a nearby button's label past the browser window's edge — fixed generically inside `setupHitExpandGroups` itself (not a sidebar-specific hack, since `#viewBar`/the per-view toolbars share the same mechanic and could in principle hit the same problem near their own edges): a new pure `clampLabelOffset(rect, viewportWidth)` measures the label's own rendered `getBoundingClientRect()` against `window.innerWidth`/0 and returns the extra px to shift it back on-screen (added to the existing `translateX(-50%)` via an inline `transform` override, cleared on collapse so it never leaks into the next hover). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 220** (`describeSidebarToolbarActions`/`handleSidebarToolbarActionClick` across every selection shape, including a dedicated absence guard for the six removed actions) and **Group 221** (toolbar visibility, the `setupHitExpandGroups` re-bind guard, AND/OR/Link's removal from the tree context menu, and `clampLabelOffset` unit-tested directly — the real `getBoundingClientRect()`/`window.innerWidth` measuring stays untested under jsdom, no layout engine); Groups 59/60/193/194 updated in place for the library-button relocation and the context/countContext menu removal; Group 182c's `#treeActionBar` PiP-hide-list entry dropped (element retired). See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "`#sidebarToolbar`", `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "Reusable filter library", and `docs/filters.md` → "Time context filter"/"Count context filter". - **feat: complete the log-entry right-click context menu** (this session, 2026-09-15, implements `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #78). `openContextMenu`'s menu (`#contextMenu`) reorganized and extended: (1) **"Filter for this ___"** (`#ctxFilterForColumn`) moved to the very top, directly under the timestamp meta row's separator, instead of being buried below After/Before/Bookmark/Note. (2) New **"Extract"** item (`#ctxExtractMessage`) right next to it — a one-click version that skips the filter popup and commits straight to a new filter node via the unchanged `extractMessageFilter(entry)` (the same function the `#viewBar` row-action's own "Extract" button calls); shown only when the message actually has something extractable, reusing the exact same gate the toolbar button's `extractActionEnabled` already checks (`buildNumericExtractPattern(textColumnValue(entry, "message")) !== null`), toggled via `style.display` like every other conditional item in this menu. (3) The remaining items regrouped by separators: meta → Filter-for-this-___ + Extract → After/Before/Time filter from selection → Bookmark/Note/Add to selection → **Copy** (new, bottom). (4) New **"Copy"** item (`#ctxCopy`) — deliberately NOT a plain call to `copyLogSelectionToClipboard()` (which reads whatever `state.logMultiSelect`/`state.selectedId` currently holds, ignoring which row was actually right-clicked): copies the right-clicked row, UNLESS that row is already part of the current `state.logMultiSelect`, in which case it copies the whole current selection (calling `copyLogSelectionToClipboard()` unmodified). A new shared `formatEntriesForClipboard(entries)`, factored out of `copyLogSelectionToClipboard`'s own inline `entries.map(e => e.raw).join("\n")`, is what both the single-row and whole-selection paths format through, so they can never diverge. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 223** (numbered 223, not 219, to avoid colliding with the unrelated stepper-control Group 219 merged into `main` the same day): the reordered menu's DOM order, the Extract item's visibility gate (shown/hidden) and its `extractMessageFilter` outcome, the Copy item's three cases (row outside selection, row inside a 2+ selection matching `copyLogSelectionToClipboard()` byte-for-byte, and nothing selected at all), plus a regression guard that After/Before/Time-range-from-selection/Bookmark/Note/Add-to-selection still work unchanged in their new positions. See `docs/filters.md` → "'Filter for this ___'" and `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Log row multi-select + copy". Full suite: **4532 passed, 0 failed**.
- **Harmonized "stepper" control — one shared −/+ (or ‹/›) pill for every value that has a decrement/increment or prev/next control flanking it** (this session, 2026-09-15, person-requested: "Alle sollten klickbare Bereiche links und rechts haben, für &lt;&gt; oder -+ die aber eindeutig zu dem Element gehören"). Before this session each of these grew its own one-off styling: the Value assertion dialog and Plot settings' axis-range fields relied on the browser's own native `<input type="number">` spin buttons; Plot view's zoom control and the inline image viewer's zoom control were flat, ungrouped buttons with a separately-boxed value between them; the Context view's match navigator (‹ *n*/*m* ›) had each button as its own separate bordered icon, not visually joined to the label between them; and the UI scale / Log text size / Anchor fade duration steppers already had the RIGHT look (a single seamless bordered pill) but via two near-identical, independently hand-rolled CSS blocks. All of these now share one component: `.stepper`/`.stepper-btn`/`.stepper-value` CSS (a single bordered pill, its flanking buttons carrying no border of their own) plus `initNumberStepper(inputId, opts)` (generic click-to-nudge wiring for an editable field — reads/clamps/writes `.value` and fires the same `"change"` event typing+blur already did, so every existing change-driven listener needed zero changes) and `stepperInputHtml(id, opts)` (the HTML-string-building counterpart, for the one caller — Plot's axis-range fields — that builds its markup from a template string on every re-render). The value area is a real `<input>` wherever a person can usefully type a number — **Value assertion**'s four fields, **Plot settings**' X/Y/Z range fields (2D and 3D), Settings → Behavior → **"Lines revealed per step"**, and the **Time context**/**Count context** dialogs' before/after fields — and a plain read-only `<span>` where it's a pure position readout: **Plot view's** and the **inline image viewer's** zoom-level displays, the **Context view's** match position (‹ *n*/*m* ›, buttons stay `.toolbar-icon-btn` so the hover-reveal-label mechanic and disabled styling are untouched — only their box merges into the pill), and the pre-existing UI scale/Log text size/Anchor fade duration readouts (their old `.font-scale-stepper`/`.temp-anchor-fade-stepper` duplicate CSS blocks are gone, folded into `.stepper`). No behavior changed for any of the underlying fields — every `type="number"` became `type="text" inputmode="…"` (native spinner suppressed, the pill's own buttons replace it) but keeps writing the exact same `.value` string the existing "change" listeners already parse; the plain-typing path is untouched. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` new **Group 219**: the shared CSS contract (one bordered pill, borderless flanking buttons, the old duplicate blocks gone) and the new Dec/Inc wiring across a representative field of each kind (Settings' Lines-per-step, Value assertion min/max/tolerance including empty-field and min-clamp behavior, Plot's X-range min, Time context's before field, and the Context match nav's now-single pill). See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Shared 'stepper' control" and `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Value assertions"/"Manual axis ranges". **Follow-up, same session (person-reported via screenshots: the pill was "stellenweise zu groß oder zu klein" — sometimes way too wide with the number lost in empty space, sometimes so narrow the buttons themselves disappeared)**: root cause was `input.stepper-value` having no explicit `width` — a bare `<input>`, unlike a `<span>`, has no content-based intrinsic size, so with `flex-basis:auto` and no `width` set it fell back to the browser's native ~20-character default text-input width, ballooning the whole pill (worst in the Plot zoom field, where a large typed/scrolled-to percentage like `3777068%` made it grow further still since nothing capped it). Fixed with an explicit `width:44px` (`38px` for the `.stepper-compact` toolbar variant) on `input.stepper-value` — flexbox uses an explicit `width` as the flex-basis when one is set, so the pill now has a small, correct default size everywhere, while still flex-growing to fill its row evenly in the two contexts that want that (Value assertion's paired min/max, Plot's paired range fields) since `flex:1 1 auto` on top of that basis is untouched. No CSS/JS elsewhere needed to change. Full suite: **4528 passed, 0 failed**. **Second follow-up, same session (person-reported via screenshot: Plot settings' X/Y range fields still overflowed `#plotControls` — the second field's own +/- buttons were pushed off the visible edge)**: at the default (non-compact) size, two full steppers side by side need at least `2*(32+44+32)+6 = 226px`, more than `#plotControls`' old `220px` width minus its own `28px` padding (`192px` available) ever had room for. Fixed two ways: the range fields now render with `stepperInputHtml`'s new `compact: true` option (`.stepper-compact` — `26px` buttons, a `38px` value, same size the toolbar zoom controls already use), and `#plotControls` itself widened `220px → 250px` for headroom beyond that. **Also fixed in the same pass, same report ("Passe auch die Chart Type Auswahl an: Alle 4 Elemente sollen das gleiche Format und die gleiche Größe haben")**: the four Chart Type buttons (Line/Bar/Scatter/3D) used `flex:1 1 auto`, which sizes each button off its OWN label's content width before distributing any leftover space — "Scatter" (7 characters) rendered visibly wider than "3D" (2) despite `flex-grow` being equal on all four. Changed to `flex:1 1 0` (a zero basis, ignoring content width entirely), so all four now render pixel-identical regardless of label length. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 219** extended: the range steppers carry `.stepper-compact`, and a CSS-level check that `.plot-type-btn` uses the zero flex-basis. Full suite: **4530 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Fix: a disabled Filter-/View-Toolbar button's hover label never showed, so a grayed-out button gave no clue what it did or how to enable it (this session, 2026-09-15, person-reported: "Aktuell kann man nicht erkennen was ein ausgegrauter Button macht ... Beispiel 'Value Assertion'")**. `setupHitExpandGroups()` — the shared hover-expand mechanism behind every `.row-action-btn`/`.tb-hit` toolbar button's floating label — explicitly excluded disabled buttons from its hit-test, and `updateRowActionButtons()`/`updateContextToolbar()` additionally force-cleared `.expanded` if a button became disabled while already hovered; together these meant a disabled button's label was unreachable by any path. Both are removed: hovering a disabled button now expands it and reveals its label exactly like an enabled one, and staying expanded across a disable transition is no longer treated as a stuck-state bug. Also fixed the generic `.toolbar-icon-btn:disabled{opacity:.32}` rule, which dims the whole button including its label as one composited layer — a revealed label would otherwise still be barely legible at 32% opacity; dimming is now scoped to just the icon span (`.row-action-hit`/`.tb-hit`), so the label stays fully readable while the icon still reads as disabled. Same fix applied to `#libraryPresetBar`/`#libraryManageBar`'s plain-`:hover`-driven labels (dropped their `:not(:disabled)` exclusion). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 176** (176a) rewritten: the old "a newly-disabled button's stuck-open pill is force-collapsed" assertion is replaced with "stays open" + a new check that hovering a disabled button's hit-zone expands it. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Filter-Toolbar" for the updated write-up. Full suite: **4510 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Fix: the Value assertion dialog's Range/Target±Tolerance mode toggle didn't actually switch which fields were shown (this session, 2026-09-15, person-reported)**: `updateAssertModeUI()` toggled a `"hidden"` class on `#assertRangeFields`/`#assertTargetFields` correctly, but no CSS rule anywhere in the stylesheet targeted it — this project has no bare `.hidden{display:none}` rule, every one is scoped to its own selector, and this pair never got theirs — so both field groups stayed visible regardless of which mode button was active. Fixed with a scoped `#assertRangeFields.hidden, #assertTargetFields.hidden{display:none;}` rule. Regression-tested in Group 14 via `isVisible()` (real `getComputedStyle`, not just `classList.contains`) — confirmed to actually fail without the fix.

- **Value assertion moved from a per-column header button into the Table toolbar as an Action, now settable for several columns at once (this session, 2026-09-15, person-requested)**: the old `.extract-assert-btn` target-icon button that sat in every numeric column's `<th>` was too small to notice. It's now `#tableAssertBtn` in `#tableToolbar`'s Actions group (same "one-off action on the current selection" category as Export-as-CSV/Select, per the app's Controls/Settings/Actions toolbar convention — see `docs/ui-and-views.md`), disabled until one or more whole columns are selected in the extraction table (`assertableSelectedColumns()`, built on the existing header-click cell-selection model). Selecting several plottable columns (Ctrl+click/Shift+click, same gesture as always) and clicking the button once now opens `#assertDialog` for all of them together — Save/Clear apply the one configured rule to every selected column in a single undo step, instead of needing one dialog per column. A single selected column still pre-fills its existing assertion and shows its name, as before. The per-column "N of M outside range" header badge is unaffected. `.extract-sort-btn` (the ▲/▼/⇅ sort glyph) also grew from 10px to 13px, matching the other 13px toolbar icons, since it read too small next to the new button. See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Value assertions" for the full write-up. Regression-tested: **Group 14** (rewritten for the toolbar flow, plus new coverage for the multi-column single-dialog behavior and the button's disabled state), **Group 24** and **Group 46** updated for the same button relocation.

- **Fix real semantic versioning: correct the release-please marker, stop it overwriting this changelog, roll back the premature v0.2.0 release, make release-cutting explicit** (this session, 2026-09-15, person-reported, follow-up to the versioning session below). The very first live `release-please` run surfaced three real bugs in that session's setup:
  1. **CI couldn't open the release PR at all** — `##[error]release-please failed: GitHub Actions is not permitted to create or approve pull requests`. Root cause: the repo's `Settings → Actions → General → Workflow permissions → "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests"` toggle, off by default and independent of the `permissions:` block already declared in the workflow YAML. The person enabled it directly in GitHub's UI (no code fix needed/possible from this session).
  2. **`PHILOGG_VERSION` silently never got updated.** The `// x-release-please-version` marker comment was placed on the line ABOVE the constant; release-please's generic extra-file updater only replaces a value on the *marker's own line*, so it found nothing there and quietly did nothing — the run advanced `.release-please-manifest.json` and all three desktop version fields to `0.2.0` while `philogg.html`'s own `PHILOGG_VERSION` stayed stuck at `0.1.0`. Fixed by moving the marker to a trailing same-line comment: `const PHILOGG_VERSION = "0.1.0"; // x-release-please-version`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 218** updated to pin the corrected same-line shape.
  3. **`release-please-config.json` was missing `"skip-changelog": true`.** Without it, release-please's default behavior applied its own auto-generated release-notes block on top of this hand-written `CHANGELOG.md` — renaming its `# PhiLogg — Changelog` title to a bare `# Changelog`, prepending a mechanically-generated `## [0.2.0] ...` section (a raw commit-message dump, including the 100+ pre-convention historical commits release-please happened to also parse as conventional), and demoting the original title/intro to a stray `## PhiLogg — Changelog` sub-heading further down. Repaired by hand (restoring the original header/intro, discarding the auto-generated block) and the missing config flag added so it can't recur — release notes live on the GitHub Release page instead, never this file.

  Beyond the three bugs, the actual v0.2.0 release (tag + GitHub Release, `PHILOGG_VERSION`/manifest/desktop files all at `0.2.0`) didn't match the intended rollout: testers should all get `0.1.x` builds for now, with only bugfix numbers counting up until a deliberate decision to open `0.2.0`. **Rolled back**: `.release-please-manifest.json`, `philogg.html`, and all three desktop version fields reverted to `0.1.0`; the `v0.2.0` git tag deleted (the GitHub Release page itself needs a manual delete via the UI — no MCP tool here can delete a published release).

  **Also redesigned the trigger**, since the automatic `push`-triggered release PR is what led to an unplanned release in the first place (person-requested: cutting a real, asset-building release should be an explicit action, not a side effect of an ordinary feature-PR merge landing on `main`). `.github/workflows/release-please.yml` is now two stages: **`propose-release`** (`workflow_dispatch` only — run by hand from the Actions tab; `skip-github-release: true` so it only ever opens/refreshes the standing release PR for review, never tags or publishes) and **`cut-release`** (still `push`-triggered, since catching "the release PR was just merged" has no other event to listen for, but `skip-github-pull-request: true` makes it a fast no-op on every ordinary feature-PR merge — it only tags+publishes on the one push that is the release PR's own merge). New `build_html`/`build_desktop` jobs, gated on `cut-release`'s `release_created` output, absorb `build-release.yml`'s asset-building logic (HTML + Windows + Windows portable + macOS + Linux, always the full set — no more picking variants for a real release) and upload straight to the just-cut GitHub Release; **`.github/workflows/build-release.yml` is deleted**, fully replaced by this. `build-tester-files.yml` is untouched — still the separate, manual, unversioned path for ad-hoc tester drops. See `PROJECT.md` → "Release builds" and `README.md` → "Versioning & releases". Full suite: **4502 passed, 0 failed**.
- **Real semantic versioning (`PHILOGG_VERSION`/`PHILOGG_BUILD` split, release-please)** (this session, 2026-09-15, person-requested, implements FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #72). Replaces the old single commit-short-SHA "version" with a real, committed semver plus a demoted build identifier. `philogg.html`'s one `PHILOGG_VERSION` constant is now two: `PHILOGG_VERSION` (real semver, cut over to `"0.1.0"` in this commit — PhiLogg's first deliberate tester milestone — shown next to the product name with no `"v"` prefix, and from here on bumped only by merging a **release-please** release PR) and `PHILOGG_BUILD` (takes over the old constant's exact role: stays `"dev"` in source, stamped to the commit's short SHA only by the two existing manual "Build Tester Files"/"Build Release" GitHub Actions, and moved out of the toolbar into a new `#licenseBuild` line in Settings → License, hidden away as requested rather than shown as the primary identifier). **Automation**: a new `.github/workflows/release-please.yml` (triggered on every push to `main`) runs `googleapis/release-please-action@v4` against `release-please-config.json`/`.release-please-manifest.json`; it reads Conventional Commits (see the new `CLAUDE.md` rule below) to maintain a standing release PR, and merging that PR bumps the version, tags the commit, and publishes a GitHub Release — it does not touch this `CHANGELOG.md` (kept hand-written; release notes live on the GitHub Release itself). `philogg.html`'s `PHILOGG_VERSION` literal carries a `// x-release-please-version` marker comment so release-please's "generic" extra-file updater rewrites only that one line, not every `X.Y.Z`-shaped string in a 29,500-line file. **Commit convention** (new `CLAUDE.md` non-negotiable): `feat: ...` bumps MINOR, `fix: ...` bumps PATCH, anything else doesn't move the version; `bump-minor-pre-major: true` means even a breaking-change marker bumps MINOR while major stays `0` — a real `1.0.0` only ever happens via an explicit `Release-As:` commit footer, which nothing here writes automatically, so the project stays under `1.0.0` by design until that's a deliberate choice. **Desktop sync**: `desktop/package.json`, `desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, and `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` — previously a static, never-bumped, unrelated `1.0.0` — are hand-set to `0.1.0` in this same cutover and from now on kept in lockstep automatically via release-please's `extra-files`. The two existing manual build workflows are otherwise unchanged, just retargeted to stamp `PHILOGG_BUILD` instead of `PHILOGG_VERSION`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 65** updated (checks a real semver in `#brandVersion`/`#licenseVersion` instead of the old hardcoded `"dev"`, plus the new `#licenseBuild` line); **Group 146** updated (its stripped-file "version line" assertion now greps for `PHILOGG_BUILD`, since that's what the build workflows stamp post-split); new **Group 218** pins the exact source-literal shape (including the marker comment) that both the workflow `sed` steps and release-please's extra-file updater depend on. See `PROJECT.md` → "Release builds" and `README.md` → "Versioning & releases". Full suite: **4503 passed, 0 failed**.
- **Windows Explorer context-menu integration (desktop wrapper, Windows-only)** (this session, 2026-09-15, person-requested). Three new right-click entries, added by the Windows installer as an *optional* component (a checkbox on its own installer page) and cleanly removed on uninstall regardless of whether it was ever ticked: **"Open in PhiLogg"** on a single `.log` or `.zip` file, and **"Watch this Folder"** on a folder. Multi-selecting several `.log` files and merging them from Explorer was considered and deliberately dropped — Windows can't hand multiple selected paths to one process launch through a plain registry verb (that needs a full COM shell-extension DLL, a disproportionate maintenance cost here); each verb instead just relies on Explorer's normal per-item launch behavior, which degrades acceptably on its own for a multi-selection (see `docs/desktop.md`). Two Rust changes make this work: `windows.rs`'s `file_arg` became `classify_launch`, which now also recognizes a `.zip` path or a directory (not just `.log`) and returns which of two routes to take — `LogFile` reuses the existing, unchanged `open_file`/`philoggLoadUrl` single-URL route (that route has no ZIP awareness, so it stays `.log`-only), while the new `LocalTarget` goes through a new `open_local`, which registers the file(s)/folder(s) and calls `window.philoggLoadLocalFiles` — the same call drag-drop already makes, so a `.zip` opens via its existing zip special-case and a folder starts a watch exactly like a dropped one. A cold launch (no window yet) can't `eval()` into a page that hasn't loaded, so `open_local` stashes the payload in a new `AppState.pending_local_load` and a new `windows::flush_pending_local` (called from `commands::app_ready`, the same "first paint" signal that already dismisses the splash) runs it once the page is actually ready. On the installer side: Tauri's NSIS `installerHooks` only inject code at four fixed points *inside* the existing single install section — none of them can add a whole new wizard page — so getting a real checkbox meant forking the generated `installer.nsi` itself (`desktop/src-tauri/windows/installer.nsi`, pulled from `@tauri-apps/cli` `2.11.4`, the version `desktop/package-lock.json` resolves to) via `tauri.conf.json`'s `bundle.windows.nsis.template`, adding a `MUI_PAGE_COMPONENTS` page and one new optional `Section`, with the three previously-unnamed core sections renamed with a leading `-` (NSIS's "hidden section" convention) so they don't also show up as separate, individually-uncheckable rows. The verbs register under `HKCU\Software\Classes\SystemFileAssociations\<ext>\shell\...` and `HKCU\Software\Classes\Directory\shell\...` (not a new ProgID), so they add a menu entry without disturbing whichever app is currently the default `.log`/`.zip` handler. Not covered by the jsdom suite (Rust/NSIS/Tauri-config only, per `CLAUDE.md`'s carve-out) — verified by `cargo check` and `cd desktop && npm run build`; installing/uninstalling the built installer to confirm the registry keys appear and disappear still needs a person on a real Windows machine. See `docs/desktop.md` → "Windows Explorer context-menu integration".
- **UI overhaul: resizer discoverability, level-bar redesign, chrome-free toggles, minimap per-level counts** (this session, 2026-09-15, design review, person-requested; four independent fixes to the Filter-Toolbar/panel-resizer area). (1) **Panel resizer grips** (`#sidebarResizer`/`#fhSplitResizer`/`#detailResizer`) were still low-contrast against the content background at rest (their permanent grip bar, added in an earlier session, used `var(--border)` — near-identical to the surrounding neutral background in every theme). Resting color changed to `var(--border-hover)` (already used elsewhere for resting-but-interactive affordances, one step more visible); hover/drag still brightens to `var(--accent)`, unchanged. `.col-resize-handle` (table column-width handles), previously fully invisible at rest, got a matching resting background for consistency. (2) **Level-quick-filter buttons** (`#levelBar`) changed from an always-visible outline/solid pill (dot + level name + count drawn inline) to circular 28x28 icon buttons matching the filter-creation buttons' own shape (`.row-action-btn`: a plain circle, hover/focus reveals a floating `.row-action-label` pill). Each button is colored in its own level color, unchecked/checked distinguished by fill: **unchecked is a full-strength colored ring** (`border-color`, transparent fill), **checked fills that same color in solid** — replacing an initial dimmed-opacity-fill attempt for "unchecked" that read too similar across levels to tell apart at a glance (follow-up, person-reported, same session). Name+count moved entirely into the hover label (e.g. "ERROR 69"), whose own background/border now matches the level's color instead of the neutral `--bg-elevated-2` every other row-action label uses. `#levelBar` also moved back to the LEFT of the filter-creation group (`[data-row-actions="viewbar"]`), reversing an earlier session's move to the right — the buttons are fixed-size circles now, so the width-shift risk that right-side placement guarded against no longer applies. **Follow-up (person-requested, same session): a dividing line between the level filter and the standard filters**, "analogous to" the existing divider between the standard filters and the library-presets group — a third `.row-action-separator` now sits right after `#btnApplyLevelToTree`, and `#levelBar`/`#btnApplyLevelToTree`'s own margins dropped from a bespoke 14px blank gap to the row's ordinary 4px/0px button-gap values, so the whole row (level filter → standard filters → library presets → New) now reads as one consistent separator-delimited group rhythm instead of the level filter being a special-cased exception. The label reveals via plain CSS `:hover` (same fix already used for `#libraryPresetBar`) rather than the JS `setupHitExpandGroups` mechanism, since `#levelBar` is fully rebuilt on every level toggle/file change and that binding only ever runs once at boot. (3) **Toggle buttons** (Notes/Multiline/Wrap/Columns/TextMatch/HighlightMatch/FilePaths/Pin in both log toolbars, plus the Text-View's Wrap/Pretty-print — 17 instances) were visually identical to plain action buttons in their inactive state (same `.toolbar-icon-btn` frame+fill). A new `.icon-toggle` class, added to exactly those 17 buttons, strips the border/background in both states and recolors the icon to `var(--accent-strong)` only when active; a thin accent-colored bar under the icon (a `::after`, absolutely positioned so it sits inside the existing 28x28 footprint without affecting the icon's own centering on the whole box) acts as an on/off status light. (4) **Timeline minimap header** ("Start … End … Duration") gained one more `·`-separated segment per active level (same order as the level-bar buttons, `activeLevelOrder()`), showing only the count (no level-name label) colored via `levelColorVar()`. Counts originally came from the same source the level-bar buttons themselves use (`getLevelCounts(levelBarCountsBaseId())`), so the two rows could never disagree. **Follow-up (person-requested, same session): the counts now show the whole file's global totals** (`getLevelCounts(rootId)`) instead — the rest of the line (Start/End/Duration) already describes the file's global state regardless of the active filter, so the level counts shouldn't silently narrow with whatever node happens to be active either. **Each count is also now clickable**: clicking one attaches (or, on a repeat click, re-activates) a single-level filter node directly under the ROOT FILE — regardless of whatever node currently happens to be active — and shows it active immediately (`applyLevelFilterUnderRootFile`, reusing an existing matching child instead of duplicating on repeat clicks). (5) **Follow-up (person-reported via an annotated screenshot): the panel resizers still read as a bare GAP, not a divider.** The earlier grip-bar fix (above) only brightened a short 30px bar centered in the resizer's own transparent 7px hit area — most of that strip still punched a stark `--bg-app` hole between the neighboring panel's `--bg-panel` and its own 1px border, which sat flush against the PANEL's own edge rather than at the resizer's far edge, 7px away from where the visual boundary actually looked like it should be. Fixed by moving each border onto the resizer itself and giving the resizer the neighboring panel's own `--bg-panel` background instead: `#sidebar`'s `border-right` moved to `#sidebarResizer`, `#detailPanel`'s `border-top` moved to `#detailResizer`, each resizer now also carrying `--bg-panel` so the panel's background continues seamlessly through the full drag-handle strip, with one clean border line exactly at the resizer's own far edge (touching the content) instead of a punched-out gap — the grip overlays this instead of floating in an unstyled void. Since each resizer is hidden entirely while its panel is collapsed (no manual resize then), `#sidebar.collapsed`/`#detailPanel.collapsed` each restore their own fallback border so the collapsed rail/bar still has a visible seam. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 90's resizer-grip assertion updated for `--border-hover`, plus a new `.col-resize-handle` assertion; Group 90's sidebar-border sanity check moved to `#sidebarResizer`; new **Group 217** (both moved borders/backgrounds, the base panel rules no longer double-declaring the border, and each `.collapsed` fallback); Groups 52/116/117 (read the level bar's count via `.cnt`) updated to read it from the button's `title`; Group 102's margin assertion updated for `#btnApplyLevelToTree`'s new 0px margin; Group 194e's DOM-order assertions flipped for the new left-of-filters placement and extended for the new separator/margin values; new **Group 214** (level-bar shape/ring-vs-fill/hover-label/position/click behavior), **Group 215** (toggle chrome removal + status bar), **Group 216** (rewritten: minimap counts are the file's global totals independent of the active node, plus the new click-to-filter behavior). See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Filter-Toolbar", "Timeline minimap", and "Collapsible sidebar / detail panel". Full suite: **4498 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Bugfix: "Syntax highlighting" set to "Follow app theme" didn't change when the app theme changed** (this session, 2026-09-14, person-reported: "Mit 'Syntax highlighting' eingestellt auf 'Follow app theme', wechselt die Darstellung nicht, wenn man das App Theme ändert"). Root cause: `applySyntaxScheme()`'s follow-theme branch was a pure no-op, relying entirely on the CSS cascade to pick up `--syntax-*` from the active theme — but only Dark and Light declare their own `[data-theme]` `--syntax-*` block in CSS (`:root` / `:root[data-theme="light"]`); the four Catppuccin flavors don't, so under follow-theme they all silently fell back to Dark's colors and switching the app theme to/between any of them never visibly changed the highlight colors, even though a matching curated pendant already existed for each in `BUILTIN_SYNTAX_SCHEMES`. Fixed by having `applySyntaxScheme()` fall back to the `BUILTIN_SYNTAX_SCHEMES` entry matching the active theme id (`currentThemeId()`) when follow-theme is chosen and the active theme is a built-in one — applied inline exactly like an explicitly-picked scheme. A custom app theme (no matching built-in id) is unaffected: still the pre-fix cascade (its own optional `syntaxColors` block, else the `:root` defaults). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 210a** updated: its old assertion that Catppuccin Mocha "cascades to the :root (dark) defaults" under follow-theme encoded the bug itself and is replaced with checks that every Catppuccin flavor resolves to its own pendant colors, and that switching Mocha → Latte actually changes `--syntax-tag`. Alt+Enter and the brace's own right-click → "Filter for this message" already special-cased the Link view's pair selection for exactly this reason (Group 168), but the toolbar buttons — added later, in the viewBar row-action reorg — were never taught the same rule. Fixed by giving `singleMessageActionEntry()` the identical Link-view pair check: when the Link view is on screen and a pair is selected, it now returns `linkPairsData[linkSelectedPairIndex].pair` (the same combined-message pair object the brace path already used) instead of falling through to the stale single-entry selection. If it recurs after this fix, it likely needs a more exact repro. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 213**: the Extract button's pattern covers both sides of the tuple (not the stale single entry) with a stale `state.selectedId` present; "Filter for this message" gets the same fix; a regression guard that a real full-pair extraction viewed on Plot still lets a tree click switch away and fall back to Filtered. - **Dialog UI consistency sweep + the `.pill-toggle` boolean control** (this session, 2026-09-14, implements FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #69, confirmed with the user). (1) **New `.pill-toggle` component** — the standard on/off control for a single boolean: a real `<button type="button" role="switch" aria-checked>` with an accent track + sliding knob (theme vars only, matching the `.panel-toggle-btn`/`.settings-switch` transition feel), replacing the native-checkbox `.settings-switch` used before. State lives in `aria-checked` (+ an `.on` class); read it with `pillGet(el)`, set it with `pillSet(el, on[, fireChange])` — **never** a `.checked` property (a button has none). One delegated document click handler toggles every pill, present or dynamically re-rendered (e.g. the plot controls), and a click on its adjacent `<label for=…>` is browser-forwarded to the button, so label clicks toggle it too; toggling dispatches a `change` event so existing change listeners keep working. **Converted** every listed single boolean: the filter popup's Regex/Case/Whole-word/NOT toggles, the link dialog's Exclusive/Order-enforce, CSV export's header/full-entry, the plot's Normalize/Equal-axis, and every `settings*`/`fwSettings*` boolean — all their `.checked` reads/writes rewritten to `pillGet`/`pillSet`. **Left as native checkboxes** (deliberately — they're set-selections, not on/off options): column visibility (`colToggle*`), the Plot Y-column picker (`plot-y-item`), and session-export per-file `session-include`/`session-embed`. (2) **Buttons** collapsed to the one-size `.btn-mini` (primary) + `.btn-mini-secondary` (secondary) pair — `.btn-mini-secondary` is now self-contained (same box as `.btn-mini`), and every dialog-footer Cancel/dismiss/alternative that had been a `.btn-mini-outline` is now `.btn-mini-secondary` (footer order stays secondary→primary); `.btn-mini-outline` is reserved for lightweight/additive actions (Reset, Add…, Change, Edit, Download template…, Clear). (3) **Hints** — trimmed every overlong `.settings-row-hint` to a single ≤~90-char line stating what the option does (multi-paragraph walls of caveats/history removed from the UI). The clock-offset (#29) and syntax-scheme (#67) dialogs added earlier this cycle already followed the standard and needed no structural change. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 212** covers the `.pill-toggle` contract (click + label-click flip `aria-checked`/`.on`, read back, `change` dispatched, `pillGet`/`pillSet` round-trip, and that the multi-select lists stayed native checkboxes); every existing group that drove a converted control via `.checked` was updated to the pill (`pillChecked`/`setPill` test helpers, or a plain `fireClick`). - **Fullscreen Focus Mode** (this session, 2026-09-13, implements FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #66, confirmed with the user; desktop-only in practice). It's a body-level class flip (`body.focus-mode`), so it never rebuilds the tree (render-scope gotcha). **F11 no longer toggles plain OS maximize** — it now toggles Focus Mode, and is **rebindable in the Shortcut Manager** (new `toggleFocus` action, default F11). Real OS fullscreen is a **Tauri-only** capability, so the shortcut is only *handled* under the desktop wrapper (`window.philogg` present) — in the plain browser build F11 is not bound at all and the browser's own F11 keeps priority — and entering/leaving asks the wrapper to go fullscreen via a new `window.philogg.setFullscreen` bridge method → new Rust `window_set_fullscreen` command (`window.set_fullscreen`). `inject.js`'s old F11→`window_toggle_maximize` capture-listener is removed (the page owns the key now). Esc is a secondary exit, but only when no popup/menu/dialog is open (an overlay closes first — a single Esc never does both). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 208** covers the page-side toggle: enter (header removed, sidebar+detail force-collapsed & hover forced, viewBar/minimap kept, `setFullscreen(true)` sent, prior state captured), exit (exact prior collapse+hover restored, `setFullscreen(false)`), a pre-collapsed sidebar honoured on exit, the F11 shortcut guarded to the desktop wrapper, and Esc's overlay-first secondary exit; Group 114's rebindable-action count updated 19→20. - **Per-file clock offset** (this session, 2026-09-13, implements FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #29). A manual clock correction for a single (non-merged) source file, reachable from its tree-row context menu ("Adjust clock…"). Approach (a): the signed-ms delta is **baked into every `entry.ts`** of that file (not applied lazily at read time), and the cumulative amount is stored on the file node as `node.clockOffset` (default 0) so it can be shown, re-edited and undone. `entry.raw`/`entry.tsRaw` are deliberately left untouched — every time display reads `formatTime(e.ts)`, so shifting `ts` alone updates the whole UI, while the session cache (which rebuilds a file's text from `entry.raw` and re-parses it) keeps the original raw timestamps and re-applies `node.clockOffset` on restore. The dialog (`#clockOffsetDialog`, following `docs/ui-standard.md`: segmented `.assert-mode-btn` mode toggle, `.settings-row` rows, `.btn-mini-secondary`/`.btn-mini` footer, one-line hints, theme vars only) offers two interpretation modes — **Difference** (a forgiving signed delta: `+1500` ms, `-2s`, or `±HH:MM:SS.mmm` — see `parseClockDelta`) applied additively to all entries, and **Start time** (a datetime input pre-filled with the file's current first `ts`; effective delta = desiredStart − currentFirstTs) — with a **live preview** of the first and last log line each as old → new. **Merged-file gotcha**: `mergeFiles` reuses a source file's entry *objects* by reference, so shifting the source's `entry.ts` also moves those entries inside any merged file that contains them — `applyClockOffset` therefore re-sorts every merged root file and drops its `_orderIndexMap` (`invalidateOrderIndexMap`) after a shift, then `invalidateAllCaches()` (a rare structural-equivalent change); the source file's own uniform shift preserves its order, so its own array/order-map stay valid untouched. **Undo/redo**: a new `"clockOffset"` action captures the applied delta and re-applies the *inverse* on undo (a naive snapshot can't capture the old `ts`, since `snapshotSubtree` copies entries by reference), so apply→undo→redo round-trips exactly. `node.clockOffset` is threaded through every file-node persistence carrier: the session cache (`persistFileNode`/`restoreSessionFromCache` — re-applied to the re-parsed entries, since the cached text keeps the original raw timestamps), undo of a file delete (`snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`), and **session export/import** (`buildSessionExport` writes it; `applyImportedClockOffset` re-applies it to the tier-matched importer file — as the difference from that file's current offset, before any timestamp-based bookmark/note re-anchoring — so the imported file's first/last timestamps match what was exported). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 208** (208a difference mode + parser + menu item + accumulation; 208b start-time mode; 208c undo/redo exact round-trip; 208d merged-view re-sort + merged-file refusal; 208e session-cache round-trip; 208f export/import round-trip). See `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "Per-file clock offset". Full suite: **4216 passed, 0 failed**.

The eight `SYNTAX_COLOR_KEYS` (the embedded-XML/JSON highlight colors in the entry detail, `#detailFormatToggle`) can now be chosen independently of the app theme via a new **Syntax highlighting** picker in Settings → Appearance (`#settingsSyntaxSchemeSelect`). Its default, **"Follow app theme"** (`FOLLOW_THEME_SYNTAX_ID`), reproduces the pre-#67 behaviour exactly — `applySyntaxScheme()` is a no-op, so the `--syntax-*` vars keep coming from the active theme's own optional `syntaxColors` block (custom themes included) or the `:root` / `:root[data-theme="light"]` cascade defaults. Six **built-in syntax schemes** (`BUILTIN_SYNTAX_SCHEMES`), one curated pendant per built-in app theme — Dark/Light reproduce the `:root` defaults, the four Catppuccin flavors get palettes drawn from each flavor's own named colors — are selectable regardless of which app theme is active. **Custom syntax schemes** use our own small JSON format (`format:"philogg-syntax-scheme"`, a `name` + the 8 `colors` keys) with a "Download template…"/"+ Import scheme…" card in Settings mirroring the custom-theme UX; imports persist to `localStorage["philogg-custom-syntax-schemes"]`, the chosen scheme to `localStorage["philogg-syntax-scheme"]` (same tier as the theme/accent choices). `applyTheme()` calls `applySyntaxScheme()` after the theme's own colors are set, so a chosen scheme overrides `--syntax-*` inline on top of any theme; returning to "Follow app theme" (or deleting the active custom scheme) clears the override. A stale/deleted scheme id in storage falls back to follow-theme on init (`resolveSyntaxSchemeId`). Scope unchanged — still only the entry-detail embedded XML/JSON. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 210**: follow-theme default preserves current vars and tracks the app theme; a built-in scheme overrides `--syntax-*` independently of the app theme and survives an app-theme switch; custom-scheme JSON import (validation, template round-trip, apply + persist, list/select rendering, delete → follow-theme fallback); stale-id fallback.
