- **Word wrap in the views** (this session, 2026-09-14, implements FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #68). Two independent, session-cache-persisted soft-wrap toggles, each in its own view's toolbar, both distinct from the existing multiline-message toggle (multiline renders literal `\n` breaks; word-wrap soft-wraps a single long line so it no longer needs horizontal scrolling — the two combine freely). (1) **Log view** (`state.wrapMessages`): a `.toggle-wrap` button in both log toolbars (`#filteredToolbar` and `#contextToolbar`) soft-wraps the message column (`.col-msg`) instead of truncating/scrolling. It also governs the **Entry Detail** message wrap, since the detail panel shows the selected log entry — off (default) the detail scrolls horizontally, on it wraps. (2) **Text View** (`state.wrapTextView`): its OWN `#itvWrapBtn` toggle in the inline-viewer toolbar (now shown for any `.txt`/`.xml`/`.json`, with Pretty Print still JSON-only). Line numbers are a CSS `::before` counter on each `.itv-line` (one per LOGICAL source line), so a wrapped display line shares its line's number and never increments it. **Virtualization**: the log view's wrap is folded into the same exact-height offsets machinery the multiline feature established — `.wrap-messages .col-msg` uses `word-break:break-all` deliberately, so each visual line packs to exactly the message track's character capacity and a row's wrapped line count is `ceil(chars / columns)`, computable from data alone (`messageWrapLines`/`wrapColsForBody`, biased one column conservative so a row can only ever over-allocate, never clip) — no per-row DOM measurement, no scroll drift. Both flags round-trip through the session cache's `meta.settings` (same tier as `multilineMessages`). See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Word-wrap toggles" and `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "Session cache". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 211**: the log-view toggle flipping state + body class + making a long row taller than one `ROW_HEIGHT`, independence from multiline, the session-cache round-trip; the Text-View toggle wrapping without changing the numbered `.itv-line` count. Group 199ad updated (the viewer Settings group/toolbar now show for any text file, since they hold the wrap toggle). Full suite: **4197 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Security: the plain-browser "open a non-log ZIP entry externally" fallback can no longer execute archived HTML/SVG in PhiLogg's origin** (this session, 2026-09-13, person-requested security audit follow-up). When a non-log, non-inline-viewable ZIP entry is opened in the plain browser build (no Tauri wrapper), `openZipEntryExternally` builds a `Blob:` URL and `window.open()`s it in a new tab. A `Blob:` URL inherits PhiLogg's *own* origin, so for an active-content type (`.html`/`.htm`/`.xhtml`/`.xht`/`.svg`) the archived file's scripts would run against PhiLogg's `localStorage` (persisted sessions/filters/themes) — a stored-XSS/exfiltration vector triggerable by opening a crafted archive. New `safeTabBlobMimeType(name)` (added next to `guessZipEntryMimeType`) serves exactly those extensions as `text/plain` — the tab shows inert source instead of executing it — and delegates every other type to `guessZipEntryMimeType` unchanged, so images/PDF/text still render inline and unknown types download. Nothing legitimate is downgraded: images route to the in-app inline image viewer well before this path, and `.svg` is deliberately not among the inline image extensions. The Tauri desktop build never reaches this fallback (it hands raw bytes to the OS via `openExtractedEntry`), so the change is browser-build-only. The rest of the untrusted-input surface was audited in the same pass and found already sound: log/message rendering is consistently `escapeHtml`-escaped (with `linkifyPaths` splitting on tags and operating only on already-escaped text), session/filter/theme imports use `JSON.parse` (no `eval`/`new Function`), XML previews go through `DOMParser` with `application/xml` (no script execution) and an escaping tokenizer, and the inline image viewer's `<image href>` only ever gets a base64 `data:` URL (no attribute breakout) restricted to raster extensions. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 207**: `safeTabBlobMimeType`'s MIME mapping (script-capable → `text/plain`, everything else passes through, including `.png`→`image/png` as a guard against a future refactor routing the image viewer's Blob through it), and an end-to-end drive of a `.svg` entry through the real dblclick → Blob fallback (asserting the Blob is `text/plain` and the embedded `<script>` never executed). See `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` → "Any other entry". Full suite: **4168 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Folder minimap: hover crosshair + drag label, and three explicit Load/Merge actions replacing the inferred single Load button** (this session, 2026-09-14, person-requested). (1) **Hover + drag aids**: hovering the axis now shows a full-height crosshair line (`#folderMinimapHoverLine`) plus a `formatTime` tooltip at the cursor — worth a full-height line here (unlike the plain-tooltip-only log minimap) since this minimap can stack many file rows, and the line shows which bars a given moment crosses. Dragging past the click threshold shows a translucent band + a `"from → to · duration"` label instead (`#folderMinimapDragRect`/`DragLabel`), mirroring `#timelineMinimapDragRect`/`DragLabel`'s own mechanics and — same reasoning — living as plain sibling `<div>`s of the SVG rather than SVG children, so an unrelated `folderScanTick` poll mid-gesture can never wipe them. (2) **Three explicit actions** replace the old single Load button, whose effect used to be inferred from *how* files were picked: **Load individually** (no merge), **Merge (full)** — merges every target file completely with **no** `timerange` filter, even if a window was drawn (that button now means "everything, unfiltered") — and **Merge (window)** — unchanged windowed/partial-load-then-filter behavior from the previous session, only enabled when a window is actually drawn (a plain bar selection never implies one). All three read their target files from a new single `resolveFolderMinimapTargets(folder)`, so the toolbar's enable/disable state and each action's actual effect can never disagree. Presented as icon buttons in a `data-toolbar-group="actions"` row — the same Controls/Settings/Actions visual language `#contextToolbar`/`#tableToolbar` already use — replacing the header's previous non-standard pill button. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 206**: button enablement across all four selection states (none/bars/window-covering-nothing/window-covering-something); `folderMinimapMergeFull` producing a merged node with zero filter children; `resolveFolderMinimapTargets` agreeing between a bar pick and an equivalent window; and, via real dispatched `mousemove`/`mousedown`/`mouseup` events (the same technique the log minimap's own drag test already uses — jsdom's stubbed `getBoundingClientRect`/`clientWidth` plus its genuine `viewBox.baseVal` support make this exercisable, not just state manipulation), the hover crosshair/tooltip appearing on hover and yielding to the drag band/label mid-gesture. Groups 204d/205e updated for the renamed functions. Full suite: **4154 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Windowed/partial file load for the folder-watch minimap's dragged-window Load** (this session, 2026-09-14, person-requested feasibility follow-up: does reading only the drawn time window, instead of the whole file, actually save load time for a large file?). Answer: yes, for a large file — the drawn window doesn't need the file parsed from the front to find where it starts. `findWindowStartOffset(file, formatId, targetTs)` binary-searches for (approximately) the byte offset where a target timestamp starts, via `probeTsAtOffset` (small growing chunks, the same trick `probeFileTimeRange`'s own head/tail read uses) — `O(log(file size))` small reads instead of a full sequential scan. `parseFileWindow(file, node, from, to, onProgress)` then reads/parses forward from that offset only until an entry's timestamp passes `to`, never running to EOF. Both assume the file is chronologically sorted start to end (the same assumption `mergeFiles`' quick-merge and live tailing already make elsewhere) — `findWindowStartOffset` reuses `probeFileTimeRange`'s own head+tail probe as a cheap global bookend sanity check (if the file's first entry's timestamp is already after its last entry's, it's definitely not sorted as assumed) before ever bisecting, plus a lighter in-loop monotonicity check; either one failing returns `consistent: false`. `loadFolderFileWindowed(folder, rec, from, to)` wires this into `loadFolderMinimapSelection`'s dragged-window Load path (in place of the previous full `loadFolderFile` per overlapping file), falling back to a normal full load when the file is already open (its entries already cover any window), below a `WINDOWED_LOAD_MIN_FILE_SIZE` (8MB — the bisection overhead only pays off on genuinely large files), its time range isn't known yet, or `parseFileWindow` reports the file isn't safely sorted. A node loaded this way is tagged `node.partial = { from, to }` — threaded through `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` (undo) and `persistFileNode`/`restoreSessionFromCache` (reload) the same way `node.merged`/`node.formatId` already are, and gets its own tree-row badge (`ICON_FILE_PARTIAL`, a circled "P") plus a tooltip naming the window that was actually read; a merge of two partial sources gets the union of both windows as its own `node.partial`. Individually-picking bars (no dragged window) is unaffected — that path still always does a full load, since no window was drawn to skip past. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 205**: `findWindowStartOffset`/`parseFileWindow` directly on a sorted fixture — correctness (only the requested window's entries) and the actual performance claim itself, via an instrumented `.slice()` byte counter showing a narrow window reads a small fraction of the file; the size/range fallback thresholds; a large file above the threshold gets only its window read and is flagged partial, with an already-open file never re-read; a file that isn't actually chronologically sorted falls back to a full load instead of silently returning wrong/missing entries; `node.partial` surviving a close+undo round trip; and `loadFolderMinimapSelection`'s merge of two windowed sources unioning their ranges. Full suite: **4132 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Folder-watch minimap for picking which files to load/merge** (this session, 2026-09-14, implements FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #64). Clicking a watched folder's own title (`.folder-watch-name`, `selectFolderContainer`) now switches the main content area to a per-file time-range timeline for that folder instead of any single node's log view — a new `state.folderView`, dispatched in `renderMainView` the same way `state.inlineViewer` already is (mutually exclusive with it). The minimap shows exactly the files `folder.files` currently lists (so a folder narrowed by a glob pattern or "show newest M" shows the same subset there, no separate filtering step needed) as one horizontal bar per file on a shared time axis, stacking where spans overlap. Each bar's range comes from `probeFileTimeRange` (added last session) for a file that isn't open yet, or straight from its real entries once it's loaded — cached on the folder's own file record as `rec._range`, invalidated by `mergeScannedFiles` whenever a file's mtime changes underneath it. Two ways to pick files, then a **Load** button: multi-selecting individual bars loads each file with no merge; dragging a time window loads every file overlapping it, fully merges them (`mergeFiles`), and applies a `timerange` filter matching the exact dragged window to the result (same `createFilterNode` call the log minimap's own drag-select uses) — so the merge opens already filtered to what was drawn. Folder-only: a ZIP source's entries are deflate-compressed, so there's no cheap head/tail read the way a real folder file gets — a new backlog entry (#65) captures the deferred ZIP variant instead. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 204**: `probeFolderFileRange`'s caching and its `mergeScannedFiles`-driven invalidation on mtime change, an already-opened file's range coming from its real entries instead of a re-probe, the `folderView` main-view dispatch (shown on title click, cleared by any real node activation, falls back when the folder is removed while showing), and the Load button's two modes (individual bars → no merge; a dragged window → merge the overlap + a matching `timerange` filter, a non-overlapping file excluded). Full suite: **4086 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Inline viewer: unified toolbar, fold-ellipsis position, and two bugfixes (viewer separation, image zoom precision)** (this session, 2026-09-13, four person-requested/reported follow-ups). (1) **Unified toolbar**: the image viewer's own floating overlay pill (`#imgViewerToolbar`) is gone, replaced with `#inlineViewerToolbar` — a real toolbar row using the same Controls | Settings grouping `#contextToolbar`/`#plotToolbar` already establish, shared by both the text and image sub-viewers (zoom controls in Controls, JSON Pretty Print in Settings), each group hiding itself when not applicable to the current file (`updateInlineViewerToolbar`). (2) **Fold-ellipsis position**: the `…` a collapsed fold shows now sits padded in to line up with the line-number column (`padding-left:1.3em` on `.itv-fold-ellipsis`) instead of flush-left under the fold-toggle arrow. (3) **Bugfix: the previously-open file's content stayed visible "behind" a newly opened one** — `#inlineTextViewer` never had its own `.hidden{display:none}` rule (every other `.hidden`-toggled element in this file does), so toggling that class did nothing visually; the `<pre>` kept rendering in normal flow underneath `#inlineImageViewer`'s absolutely-positioned box. (4) **Bugfix: drag-to-zoom on an image was imprecise** — the viewBox used to be built from the raw selection's own aspect ratio and left to the SVG's own default `preserveAspectRatio` to fit it, which silently added its own extra letterbox on top of a separate manual pixel-scale factor, breaking every pointer-to-data coordinate mapping whenever the domain's aspect didn't match the viewport's. Fixed by dropping the separate scale factor (viewBox now uses raw image-pixel units), setting `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, and adding `imgAspectFit` (expands the shorter side of a requested rect to match the viewport's aspect, centered, never cropping) — the drag-select handler now also clips the raw dragged rectangle to the image's own bounds before fitting it. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Inline text/image viewer" for the full writeup. Regression coverage: GROUP 199 — 199ae/199af/199ag added, 199j/199ad updated for the new toolbar structure.

- **Inline viewer: generalized beyond ZIP to folder-watch/direct-open, closable everywhere, JSON Pretty Print, fold-toggle bugfix** (this session, 2026-09-12, four person-reported/requested follow-ups after testing the previous session's rearchitecture). (1) **General file support**: a non-log inline-viewable file (`.txt`/`.json`/`.xml`/`.jpg`/`.png`/etc.) is no longer ZIP-only — `scanFolderHandle`'s folder-watch listing filter now also admits it (was hardcoded to `.log` only), each watched folder gets its own `inlineViewers` cache Map (mirroring `zip.inlineViewers`), and `loadFileDescriptors` (the direct-open/drag-drop funnel) now routes such a file to `openInlineViewer` instead of failing to parse it as a log — these get their own top-level closable tree row via the new `state.looseInlineViewers`. `openInlineViewer`'s viewer objects carry a generalized `ownerKind`/`ownerId`/`mapKey` instead of the old ZIP-only `zipId`; `viewerNavId` (generalized from `zipViewerNavId`) covers all three owner kinds for Alt+Up/Down nav. (2) **Bugfix: closing didn't actually work, in any context, including ZIP** — `.tree-del`'s hover-to-reveal CSS was scoped to `.tree-row`, but every inline-viewer entry row is `.folder-watch-file`/`.zip-source-file`, never `.tree-row`, so the close button was present in the DOM but never visible/clickable; added a matching `.folder-watch-file:hover .tree-del{opacity:1;}` rule, plus middle-click close (`auxclick`) to match a real log row's own affordance. (3) **JSON Pretty Print**: a new `#itvPrettyPrintBtn` toggle in the viewer header (visible only for `.json`) re-renders via `JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(text), null, 2)` without ever mutating the underlying source text; invalid JSON refuses with a toast instead of silently no-op-ing. (4) **Bugfix: the fold-toggle glyph added last session was invisible** — `.itv-fold-toggle`'s `left:-1.5em` measured from `.itv-line`'s own box edge, pushing it entirely outside `#inlineTextViewer`'s clipped content instead of into the gutter as intended; fixed with a dedicated non-negative slot. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Inline text/image viewer" for the full writeup. Regression coverage: GROUP 199 — 199z/199aa/199ab/199ac/199ad added, the `notes.txt`/"incompatible extension" fixtures in the folder-watch groups renamed to `notes.pdf` (an actually-incompatible extension, since `.txt` is inline-viewable now).

- **Inline text viewer: rearchitected for reliable native copy/select, plus collapsible JSON/XML sections** (this session, 2026-09-12, person-reported: copy/select of leading whitespace or tabs was unreliable, the viewer felt "stitched together" from many elements). `renderInlineTextViewer` now builds each line as a single inline `<span class="itv-line">` holding the raw line text directly (inside one `<pre>`), instead of a block `<div>` wrapping separate `.itv-line-num`/`.itv-line-content` spans. Line numbers are now a CSS `::before` counter — generated content, never a DOM text node — so they can never enter a selection or `copy` event; this removes the need for the old `copy` event handler and its `itvContentOffset` Range-offset helper entirely, since native browser select/copy is now correct by construction (byte-identical to the source, including leading whitespace/tabs). Added `findJsonFoldRanges`/`findXmlFoldRanges` (lightweight bracket/tag-matching scans) + `buildFoldTree`/`renderFoldNode` to make multi-line JSON/XML structures collapsible via a small `.itv-fold-toggle` glyph on the opening line — pure CSS collapse/expand, no re-render. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Inline text viewer" for the full writeup. Regression coverage: GROUP 199 (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js`) — 199i/199k/199p/199r/199s rewritten for the new structure, 199t/199x removed as dead code (they only existed to pin down the now-deleted copy handler's Range-boundary edge cases), 199y added for fold behavior.

- **Inline text viewer: rearchitected for reliable native copy/select, plus collapsible JSON/XML sections** (this session, 2026-09-12, person-reported: copy/select of leading whitespace or tabs was unreliable, the viewer felt "stitched together" from many elements). `renderInlineTextViewer` now builds each line as a single inline `<span class="itv-line">` holding the raw line text directly (inside one `<pre>`), instead of a block `<div>` wrapping separate `.itv-line-num`/`.itv-line-content` spans. Line numbers are now a CSS `::before` counter — generated content, never a DOM text node — so they can never enter a selection or `copy` event; this removes the need for the old `copy` event handler and its `itvContentOffset` Range-offset helper entirely, since native browser select/copy is now correct by construction (byte-identical to the source, including leading whitespace/tabs). Added `findJsonFoldRanges`/`findXmlFoldRanges` (lightweight bracket/tag-matching scans) + `buildFoldTree`/`renderFoldNode` to make multi-line JSON/XML structures collapsible via a small `.itv-fold-toggle` glyph on the opening line — pure CSS collapse/expand, no re-render. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Inline text viewer" for the full writeup. Regression coverage: GROUP 199 (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js`) — 199i/199k/199p/199r/199s rewritten for the new structure, 199t/199x removed as dead code (they only existed to pin down the now-deleted copy handler's Range-boundary edge cases), 199y added for fold behavior.

- **Quick merge for non-overlapping files, and a head/tail-only file time-range probe** (this session, 2026-09-12, person-requested feasibility follow-up). Investigating a person-reported "merge takes a few seconds for ~100MB files" observation found the actual bottleneck: `loadFileDescriptors` reads/parses each file exactly once regardless of the merge choice, so the separate merge progress bar people see afterward measures `mergeFiles`' own copy+sort step (chunked reference-copy across `MERGE_CHUNK_ENTRIES`, then `entries.sort`) — genuinely expensive at large entry counts, not a re-parse. `mergeFiles` now checks whether all sources' time ranges are pairwise disjoint (`fileEntryTimeRange`, a linear min/max scan per source, cheap next to a sort) and, if so, skips the chunked copy-with-yield and the sort entirely in favor of a plain `concat()` in chronological source order — already sorted by construction. Any overlap, including sources sharing an exact boundary timestamp, still uses the original copy+sort path unchanged. Also adds `probeFileTimeRange(file, formatId)`: reads a small head chunk and a growing tail chunk (via `getCompiledFormat`, so custom log formats are respected, not just the default) to learn a file's first/last timestamp without loading or parsing it in full — near-constant cost regardless of file size. Groundwork for a future folder-watch minimap (showing per-file time coverage across a whole watched folder before any file is opened) — not wired into any UI yet. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 203**: disjoint-range quick merge (order-independent, still chronological), overlapping-range fallback, boundary-equal-timestamp treated as overlap, and `probeFileTimeRange` on a normal/single-entry/empty file and one with a trailing timestamp-less continuation line. Group 69's fixture changed from disjoint to overlapping ranges so it keeps exercising the async copy+sort path it was written for. Full suite: **3996 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP inline viewers, two more person-reported bugfixes: Entry Detail no longer leaks over a non-log viewer, and multi-line copy is rewritten to stop bailing out on a container-level selection boundary** (this session, 2026-09-11, follow-up to the entry below — the previous entry's "multi-line copy" fix, itself the third attempt, still did not actually fix the underlying issue). (1) **Entry Detail leak**: `renderMainView`'s `state.inlineViewer` early-return branch already hides `#breadcrumbBar` and `#timelineMinimap` for the same reason (see the entries below) but never touched `#detailPanel`/`#detailResizer` at all — added the same `display:"none"` there. (2) **Multi-line copy, rewritten from scratch**: re-investigating from the actual current DOM structure (not the prior fix's assumptions) found the real root cause all three earlier attempts missed — `itvLineElFor(node)` resolved a selection boundary's line via `node.closest(".itv-line")`, which returns `null` whenever the boundary's container is `#inlineTextViewer` itself rather than something nested under a specific line. That's exactly what a real drag reports when it starts before the first line's text or ends past the last line — "select all lines from the very start," the person's own description — so the `copy` handler silently bailed out and let the browser's own inconsistent block-serialization copy through, the very behavior it existed to prevent. Rewritten to find every touched line directly from the `Range` via `Array.from(...).filter(lineEl => range.intersectsNode(lineEl))`, with no dependency on what either boundary's container node actually is; only the first/last touched line need a partial-offset lookup (`itvContentOffset`, now clamped to each line's own length), every line strictly in between is taken whole from the source text. `itvLineElFor` is deleted. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section updated. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199**: new **199w** (Entry Detail + its resizer hide once a non-log entry is active) and **199x** (a container-level start-and-end boundary copies the whole file; a container-level end boundary alone still captures one full line; a normal cross-line selection with no container-level boundary still works). Full suite: **3934 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP inline viewers, three more person-reported bugfixes (screenshot-referenced): a multi-line copy no longer truncates its last line, switching to a non-log entry now actually deselects the previously-active log file, and the minimap now hides while a non-log entry is active** (this session, 2026-09-11, follow-up to the two entries below). (1) **Multi-line copy truncation**: `itvContentOffset` (the `copy` handler's Range-boundary→character-offset resolver) special-cased a boundary landing outside `.itv-line-content` by clamping to 0 or the line's full length via `Node#compareDocumentPosition`'s `PRECEDING` flag — wrong whenever the boundary's element is an ANCESTOR of `.itv-line-content` rather than an unrelated sibling, since `compareDocumentPosition` sets `PRECEDING` for a containing node the same as for a textually-preceding one. A real drag ending past the last character of a selected line commonly resolves to exactly that shape — `(lineEl, lineEl.childNodes.length)`, the `.itv-line` DIV itself, one level above `.itv-line-content` — so the old code always took the "clamp to 0" branch there, truncating the last selected line to empty regardless of how far into it the drag actually went, and reproduced with the person's own report's XML shape (indented closing tags each on their own line). Fixed by dropping the special-case branch entirely: `Range#setEnd(container, offset)` repositions a boundary correctly regardless of how `container` nests relative to `contentEl` (ancestor, descendant, or a sibling like the line-number gutter), so the one general path now handles every case. (2) **Switching to a non-log entry didn't deselect the log file**: opening/re-selecting a non-log inline-viewer entry, and Alt+Up/Down landing on one, only ever set `state.inlineViewer` — never cleared `state.activeId` — even though the reverse direction (selecting a real tree row, or opening a brand-new zip log entry) already clears `state.inlineViewer` as part of the same transition. `state.activeId` stayed pointed at the previously-active log file, so its tree row kept the `active` class (`renderNode`'s `id === state.activeId` check) after switching away from it. All three call sites now go through a new shared `activateInlineViewer(viewer)` helper (`state.activeId = null; state.inlineViewer = viewer;`) instead of assigning `state.inlineViewer` directly, so a future fourth call site can't reintroduce the same gap. (3) **Minimap stayed visible after switching, and even after closing the log entirely**: `renderMainView`'s `state.inlineViewer` early-return branch (the same one that already hides `#breadcrumbBar` — see the entry two below) never touched `#timelineMinimap` at all, so whatever visibility it had before switching just stuck; since that branch returns before the `!hasFiles` code further down (the only other place hiding it) ever runs, the minimap also stayed visible after the log file that was open before the switch was closed outright while the non-log viewer remained active. Fixed by adding the same `classList.add("hidden")` to that branch, mirroring `#breadcrumbBar`'s existing gating — and, since fix (2) makes closing the log file behave identically to it never having been the active node, this required no separate condition. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section updated. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199**: new **199t** (a multi-line copy stays correct when a Range boundary resolves to the `.itv-line` DIV itself, in both drag directions), **199u** (all three "becomes active" paths — open, re-select, Alt+Down — clear `state.activeId` and the previous log's tree-row `active` class), **199v** (the minimap hides once a non-log entry is active and stays hidden after the log file open before the switch is closed entirely). Full suite: **3928 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP inline viewers, three more person-reported bugfixes: Alt+Up/Down nav order now matches the visual listing, Ctrl+C no longer steals a text-viewer selection in favor of a background-selected log row, and a copied selection no longer drops leading whitespace or gets hijacked into a native drag** (this session, 2026-09-11, follow-up to the entry below). (1) **Nav order didn't match the visual order**: the earlier `flattenTreeIds` fix (below) appended every opened non-log zip entry's virtual nav id as ONE block after the whole main tree walk, and even a real tree node for an opened log entry (`node.zipId`) sorted wherever it happened to land in `state.rootIds` — generally "whenever it was opened" — rather than its zip's own alphabetical listing position, since the main walk never excluded `node.zipId` nodes the way `renderTree`'s own walk already does (they render only inside their zip's `renderZipSection`). Fixed by excluding `node.zipId` nodes from the main walk and instead walking each zip's `zip.entries` in the exact order/lookup `renderZipSection` itself uses — real open node (with its children) or virtual nav id, interleaved at its true visual position — so Alt+Up/Down now matches the rendered top-to-bottom order exactly, including mixed log/non-log listings, regardless of open order. (2) **Ctrl+C copied the wrong thing**: the pre-existing global Ctrl+C `keydown` handler (`copyLogSelectionToClipboard`) fires whenever `state.focusRegion === "entries" && state.selectedId` — still true while an inline viewer is open and showing something else, since opening one deliberately leaves that state untouched — and it already had ONE precedence check for a real text selection (the Entry Detail panel), but not for a selection inside `#inlineTextViewer`; since the handler runs on `keydown` and calls `preventDefault()` itself, it pre-empted the inline viewer's own `copy`-event listener entirely rather than merely racing it. Fixed by extending the existing `sel.anchorNode` containment check to also cover `inlineTextViewerEl`. (3) **Leading whitespace dropped + drag-like feel near a selection**: both traced to the same cause — browsers natively make text inside an ACTIVE selection draggable (the same gesture that lets selected text be dragged into another input), with zero app code opting in; clicking/dragging at or near an existing selection in the viewer was being hijacked into that native drag instead of a plain re-select, and since a hijacked drag never produces a fresh `Range`, the subsequent Ctrl+C copied whatever selection pre-dated the drag (typically not starting at column 0), silently losing the line's leading whitespace. Offset computation itself (`itvContentOffset`, the `copy` handler) was verified correct for every DOM boundary shape a real selection can produce — the bug was upstream of it, in the selection never being replaced. Fixed with one `dragstart` listener on `#inlineTextViewer` that unconditionally calls `preventDefault()`, opting the whole viewer out of the native drag gesture — no other mousedown/mousemove/custom-selection JS exists on the text content area, so click-and-drag now falls straight through to ordinary browser text selection. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section updated. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199**: new **199q** (a 3-entry zip — image/text/log, opened out of alphabetical order — ends up in Alt+Up/Down order matching the alphabetical render order), **199r** (Ctrl+C with a background-selected log row AND a genuine inline-viewer text selection leaves the global handler un-prevented), **199s** (a copy starting exactly at column 0 preserves leading whitespace; `dragstart` on the viewer is prevented); **199o** updated for the corrected cross-zip nav order. Full suite: **3915 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP inline viewers, three person-reported bugfixes + one UX simplification: opened non-log entries actually look opened and reach Alt+Up/Down, drag-selecting text works again, and copy no longer adds extra line breaks** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-reported follow-up to the entry below). (1) **Removed click-to-highlight, kept hover-only** (the UX ask, done first since it also fixes (2)): the delegated click listener on `#inlineTextViewer` and its `.itv-line-selected` class/`state.inlineViewer.selectedLine` field are gone outright — a line highlights purely via the existing plain CSS `.itv-line:hover` rule, no JS/state involved. (2) **Root cause of "can't re-select over an existing selection"**: that same click listener fired on every mouseup inside the viewer (a click always fires at the end of a drag-selection too, not just a plain click), toggling `.itv-line-selected` right after the person finished dragging out a selection — removing the listener (fix (1)) removes the interference; verified plain drag-to-select, including starting a fresh drag from inside an existing selection, now behaves exactly like ordinary browser text selection. (3) **Copy introduced extra line breaks**: each line is its own block-level `.itv-line` div (needed for the gutter layout), and real browsers are inconsistent about how many newlines a block boundary contributes to a copied selection's plain-text serialization, especially around a selection's first/last partial line and genuinely empty lines — rather than fight per-browser DOM-to-text serialization, a new `copy` listener on `#inlineTextViewer` now intercepts the event (only when the current selection is actually inside the viewer) and writes plain text straight from `state.inlineViewer.text` itself, sliced to the exact line/character range the DOM selection covers (via `Range#toString().length` against each line's `.itv-line-content`, so it's correct regardless of how many nested tokenizer `<span>`s sit in between) — always byte-identical to the source file, never the browser's own reconstruction of it. (4) **Root cause of "opened non-log entries still look grayed"**: `.zip-entry-opened` (added in the entry below) reset `opacity` back to 1 but never reset the dim `color:var(--text-tertiary)` `.folder-watch-file`'s own base rule sets — an opened-but-not-active row was technically un-dimmed but still visibly gray, unlike a real opened `.tree-row` (default `color:var(--text-secondary)`) it was meant to match; fixed by adding the same color reset to `.zip-entry-opened`. (5) **Root cause of "Alt+Up/Down skips opened non-log entries"**: `flattenTreeIds` (which `moveTreeSelection`, the function behind Alt+Arrow, walks) only ever iterated `state.nodes`/`state.rootIds` — a non-log entry never gets a real tree node (it lives only in `zip.inlineViewers`, see the entry below), so it was never a candidate at all. Fixed by appending one virtual nav id (`zipViewerNavId(zipId, entryName)`, distinguished from real node ids by a `"zipviewer:"` prefix) per opened non-log entry, in the same per-zip/per-entry order `renderZipSection` itself renders in; `moveTreeSelection` now tracks "where we are" as either `state.activeId` (a real node) or the active inline viewer's own virtual id, and landing on a virtual id sets `state.inlineViewer` instead of `state.activeId` (landing back on a real node clears `state.inlineViewer`, matching a plain tree-row click). `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section updated. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199**: **199l** (the old click-to-highlight test) replaced by **199n** (hover-only: no click state, the CSS rule for `.itv-line-selected` itself is gone, `:hover` rule present); new **199o** (opened-row color fix + Alt+Up/Down reaching and leaving an opened non-log entry) and **199p** (copy produces exactly the source's line breaks, including the empty-line edge case, and leaves a collapsed selection's copy untouched). Full suite: **3907 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP inline viewers, two refinements: a line-number gutter + click-to-highlight for the text viewer, and unifying non-log entries' open/close/select lifecycle with a log file's own tree row** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-requested follow-up to the entry below, screenshot-referenced for the desired functionality only, not its visual style). (1) **Line-number gutter**: the inline text viewer (`.txt`/`.xml`/`.json`) now renders each line as its own `.itv-line` row — a non-selectable `.itv-line-num` gutter cell (`user-select:none`, plus a genuinely separate DOM element from the text so a selection/copy from the content column never includes the numbers) and an `.itv-line-content` cell. This is a **new**, small, consistently-styled addition rather than a reuse of an existing mechanism — checked first, per the plan: the main log view (`renderVisibleRows`) has no literal per-line-number column at all to copy (it's a field-column table — time/level/thread/location/method/message — not line-numbered free text), so there was nothing to reuse there. Clicking a line toggles a click-to-highlight class (`.itv-line-selected`, reusing the app's existing selected-row look — `.log-row.selected`'s inset accent `box-shadow`), tracked on `state.inlineViewer.selectedLine`; the click listener is attached ONCE to the stable `#inlineTextViewer` container (event delegation), not per-line, since `renderInlineTextViewer` rebuilds every line's DOM node via `innerHTML` on each call — the same DOM-identity trap CLAUDE.md's "`renderVisibleRows()` rebuilds nodes" gotcha describes, sidestepped the same way. `.json`/`.xml` tokenizing now runs **per line** rather than on the whole text (a match spanning a raw newline would otherwise leave an unclosed `<span>` dangling into the next line's markup). (2) **Lifecycle unification**: a non-log entry's own row (`renderZipEntryRow`) now carries the exact open/close/select mechanics a log file's real `.tree-row` already has — `zip.inlineViewers` (a new `Map<entryName, viewerObject>` on each zip) stands in for "already has an open node," so a plain click on an already-opened row re-selects it (no re-extraction) and its own new `.tree-del` ✕ closes it; `#inlineViewerCloseBtn`, the separate close button that used to live on the viewer itself, is gone. New CSS states `.zip-entry-opened` (not grayed, once opened) / `.zip-entry-active` (the accent highlight, only for the entry actually shown) mirror an opened-but-not-selected vs. active log file's tree row. Selecting a real tree row, or opening a brand-new zip log entry, now also clears `state.inlineViewer` (a latent bug: `renderMainView` gave the inline viewer unconditional priority, so a previously-open non-log viewer could keep hiding a newly-opened log file's content). `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section updated. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199** extended with **199k** (gutter structure + numbers-vs-content separation), **199l** (click-to-highlight toggling + surviving a re-render via the delegated listener), **199m** (grayed/opened/active row states, row-driven open/re-select/close, opening a log entry clearing a previously-active non-log viewer); **199i** updated to check `.itv-line-content`'s text instead of the whole viewer's `textContent` (which now also picks up the gutter number). Full suite: **3899 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP sources: fixed an opened log entry not nesting inside its zip's container, plus inline in-app viewers (syntax-highlighted text, pan/zoom/reset image viewer) for common non-log entry types, plus hide the tab bar when nothing log-shaped is active** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-reported bug + feature request). (1) **Bugfix**: an opened zip log entry rendered as a detached top-level file instead of nested inside `renderZipSection`'s own box, the way an opened folder-watch file stays nested via `node.folderId`. Root cause: `openZipEntry`/`loadFileDescriptors` never tagged the resulting node with anything identifying its zip origin. Fixed with a new, separate `node.zipId` field (not `node.folderId` — every consumer of that field assumes a real, reconnectable `state.folders` entry, which a never-persisted zip source doesn't have), set on the node from its very first queued-placeholder render via a new `zipId` field on the `loadFileDescriptors` descriptor; `renderZipSection` now mixes a real tree row (for an entry matched by `node.zipId`+name) in among its inert placeholder rows, same pattern `renderFolderSection` already uses, and `renderTree` skips any `node.zipId` row the same way it already skips `node.folderId` ones. `node.zipId` is deliberately never persisted, so a session-restored zip-opened file just becomes an ordinary top-level file next reload. (2) **Inline viewers**: a non-log entry of a common type now renders in the main content area (`#inlineViewerWrap`) instead of always going to the pre-existing OS-open/browser-tab fallback (`openZipEntryExternally`) — `.txt`/`.xml`/`.json` get a text viewer (`.json`/`.xml` with new lightweight regex-based token highlighting, `highlightJsonText`/`highlightXmlText` — no generic syntax highlighter existed anywhere else in the app to reuse), `.jpg`/`.jpeg`/`.png`/`.tiff`/`.tif` get an image viewer with pan/zoom/reset/drag-to-select-a-region-and-zoom, modeled directly on Plot View's own interaction (same zoom-factor constants, same `.plot-drag-rect` overlay class, same `plotZoom`/`plotLastRender`-shaped state under new names `imgZoom`/`imgLastRender`) rather than spliced into Plot View's own data-extraction-coupled rendering code. Every other extension keeps the existing external-open behavior unchanged; checked whether folder watch or plain drag/drop ever hand a non-log file to an "open externally" mechanism (they don't — this stays zip-specific, no generalized "open any file" system was built). Desktop's `open_extracted_entry` Rust command is untouched — still used for every non-inlined type. (3) **Tab bar**: `#breadcrumbBar` (the thin strip above the main content area) now hides whenever nothing log-shaped is active — no files loaded at all, or a non-log inline viewer is the active content — instead of always staying visible (empty) as before. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199** extended with **199h** (nesting fix: `node.zipId` tagging, nested render, return-to-placeholder on close), **199i**/**199i-2** (inline text viewer + tab-bar-hidden + JSON/XML tokenizers), **199j** (inline image viewer); **199f**/**199g** updated to non-inline-viewable fixtures (`.mp3`/`.pdf`) since their original `.png`/`.txt` fixtures now hit the new inline viewers instead of the external-open paths those tests exercise. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section updated. Full suite: **3879 passed, 0 failed**.

- **ZIP sources, four refinements: matched folder-watch interaction, an archive icon, non-log entries openable via OS/browser hand-off, relative paths for nested entries** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-requested follow-up to the entry below). (1) Confirmed the double-click interaction already matched folder watch's own `renderInactiveFileRow` → `loadFolderFile` pattern exactly (same event, same shared ingestion path) — no change needed there, just verified. (2) The ZIP container header icon is now `ICON_ZIP` (a small archive-box-with-zipper inline SVG, same viewBox/stroke conventions as `ICON_FOLDER`) instead of the generic `ICON_FILE`. (3) Any entry, not just recognized log files, is now openable: `openZipEntry` branches on `isLogZipEntry` (reusing folder watch's own `FOLDER_WATCH_EXTENSIONS`/`isCompatibleFolderFile` — no new "log file" notion invented); a log entry still loads via `loadFileDescriptors` as before, any other entry goes through the new `openZipEntryExternally`, which extracts lazily and hands the bytes off to a viewer — the OS's default app under the Tauri desktop build (new `window.philogg.openExtractedEntry` bridge call → new Rust command `commands.rs::open_extracted_entry`, which writes the bytes to an OS temp file and opens it via `tauri-plugin-opener`'s `open_path`, the same plugin `reveal_path`/`reveal_local_url` already use — **no new Cargo crate needed**), or a new browser tab on a `Blob` URL (`guessZipEntryMimeType`, a small extension → MIME map) in the plain browser build, revoked after 60s. (4) Verified `readZipEntries`/`renderZipEntryRow` already carry and display an entry's full relative path (e.g. `subfolder/app.log`) rather than a stripped basename — no fix needed, just a regression test locking it in. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199** extended with **199e-199g** (nested-path display+load-name, non-log entry → Blob-URL tab in the plain build, non-log entry → `window.philogg.openExtractedEntry` under the desktop wrapper). Full suite: **3857 passed, 0 failed**. `cd desktop && npm run build`/`cargo check` could not be exercised in this sandbox (missing system GTK/GDK dev libraries unrelated to this change — `gdk-sys`'s build script fails on `pkg-config` before reaching this crate's own code at all); the new Rust command was reviewed by hand instead and mirrors the existing `reveal_path`/`reveal_local_url` commands' shape closely. See `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s "ZIP sources" section.

- **Open a `.zip` file as a log source** (this session, 2026-09-11). New "Open… → ZIP…" menu entry / drop-a-`.zip` route (`openZipPickerFlow`, `state.zips`, `readZipEntries`, `renderZipSection` — all in `philogg.html`, next to `loadFileDescriptors`), distinct from folder watch: only the ZIP's central directory is read upfront (no bulk extraction, no watching/polling, nothing persisted across a reload — a ZIP is immutable once opened). Rows render in a folder-watch-styled box (`#zipList`) but without the scanning-ping indicator or settings gear, since nothing here is polled and no auto-load rules apply. Double-clicking a row lazily inflates ONLY that entry — `DecompressionStream('deflate-raw')` for compression method 8, passthrough for method 0/stored — and feeds it into the existing `loadFileDescriptors` ingestion path as a static, non-tailable file (no `handle`, so `tailTick`/folder-scan machinery naturally ignores it). No new runtime dependency and no Rust/Tauri changes: the ZIP container format (EOCD + central directory + local file headers) is a small hand-written inline parser, and the actual inflate rides on the native `DecompressionStream` Web API, available identically in the browser build and the Tauri desktop webview. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` **Group 199** (new, with a synthetic ZIP fixture built via Node's `zlib.deflateRawSync` — test-fixture tooling only, not a runtime dependency): central-directory parsing correctness, `extract()`'s laziness (an untouched entry never invokes `DecompressionStream`), and the double-click → ingestion path producing a parsed, non-tailable node. Full suite: 3847 passed, 0 failed. See `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`'s new "ZIP sources" section.
- **Added: on/off toggle for clickable file paths (default ON), and extended the feature to the Full/Context view** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-requested). A new `.toggle-filepaths` icon button sits in both `#contextToolbar` and `#filteredToolbar` (`philogg.html`), following the exact shared-class convention every other display toggle already uses (notes/multiline/columns/textmatch/highlightmatch) — one button element per toolbar, one shared state. `filePathLinksEnabled` (`localStorage["philogg-file-path-links-enabled"]`, default on) gates the `linkifyPaths` call in both row renderers; off, a message field renders exactly as plain `markFieldHtml` output — no `.fp-candidate` spans, no underline, no hover cost. Since the Context toolbar's copy would otherwise control nothing — `linkifyPaths` only ever ran in `renderVisibleRows` (Table/Filtered view), never in `renderHighlightVisibleRows` (Full/Context view) — this session also wired it into the Full/Context view: `verifyVisibleFpCandidates` now takes a `container` argument (`tableRows` or `highlightRows`), `applyFpVerifiedClass` updates matching spans in BOTH containers (the split view can show the same path in Table and Full at once), and the hover mouseover/mouseout handling is now two shared functions attached to both row containers instead of being Table-only — `fpPathCache`/`fpPathPending` stay single shared maps, so a path appearing in both views still costs exactly one `pathExists()` call. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 200** — both button copies exist and are visible, default on, a click on either flips and persists the shared state and updates both copies; the toggle actually gates rendering in both `#tableRows` and `#highlightRows` (off leaves the plain path text in place, just unlinkified); a path appearing in both views resolves through one shared `pathExists()` call. **3874 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: clickable local file paths still truncated any filename with more than one dot** (this session, 2026-09-11, person found this themselves in two more real log lines: `Filtered C:\eula.1028.txt` and `...assembly: C:\Program Files\MyCompany\My Program\MyCompany.App.Program.exe`). The previous fix's unquoted-with-spaces alternative anchored the path's end on a *lazy* `.ext` search — "lazy" stops at the *first* dot-shaped thing it finds, so it truncated `C:\eula.1028.txt` to `C:\eula.1028` and the .NET-style dotted assembly name to `...MyCompany.App`, silently dropping the rest. That anchor's own docs had dismissed this as a rare `archive.tar.gz`-shaped edge case, but multi-dot filenames turn out to be the norm (versioned files, dotted assembly names) — not rare at all. Fixed by dropping the extension-anchor idea entirely: `FILE_PATH_RE`'s unquoted Windows/UNC alternative now lets only **directory** segments (each terminated by its own `\`) contain spaces; the **final** segment (the filename) reuses the exact same no-space class the very first version of this regex had, so it needs no "where does it end" heuristic at all — a space still ends the match exactly like it always did, and a multi-dot filename is captured in full since nothing is trying to guess where its extension starts. Fixes the previously-documented `archive.tar.gz` limitation as a side effect too. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 199a extended with the person's two exact examples. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3856 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: clickable local file paths didn't detect paths containing spaces, or paths wrapped in quotes** (this session, 2026-09-11, person found the actual root cause themselves after the previous fix didn't help: "meine Pfade im Log sind 1. nicht escaped, mal stehen '' drumherum, mal gar nichts 2. haben Leerzeichen" — example `Saved to C:\My Program\config.xml`). `FILE_PATH_RE` (`philogg.html`, next to `linkifyPaths`) excluded `\s` from every Windows/UNC path segment, so detection stopped dead at the first space and never even reached the point where verification/hover would matter. Fixed with two additions, in priority order: (1) three new quoted alternatives — `'...'`, `"..."` (matched via its escaped `&quot;` form, since `linkifyPaths` runs on already-HTML-escaped text) and `` `...` `` — treat the quote characters as an unambiguous boundary, so the path inside may contain spaces freely; `linkifyPaths`' replacer reworked to keep the quote characters themselves outside the `<span>` rather than swallowing them into the link. (2) a bounded unquoted alternative for a bare (unquoted) Windows/UNC path that allows spaces only when the match can anchor on a trailing `.ext` (each segment capped to 60 chars, so a lazy "look ahead for any later dot" can't run away into unrelated trailing sentence text) — the original no-space, no-extension-required alternative stays as a fallback for a bare directory path like `C:\Windows\System32`. Two accepted, documented limitations: an unquoted path with spaces AND no file extension still can't be anchored (quote it at the source); a compound extension (`archive.tar.gz`) only matches through the first dot-run (`archive.tar`). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 199a extended: the person's exact unquoted example, the same followed by more sentence text (still stops right after the extension), all three quote forms, and the original space-free/extension-less case as a regression check. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3854 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: clickable local file paths never appeared to work — no visible cue before verification, and verification depended on a fragile hover-timing window** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-reported: built a portable installer from this branch, opened a real log with an absolute Windows path in a message field, hovered it for over a second — no popup, no visible change at all). Code review found no functional bug in the regex, the Tauri command wiring, or the event-delegation logic, but two real design gaps: a `.fp-candidate` span had **zero** CSS before verification succeeded, making a detected path 100% indistinguishable from plain text — so there was no way to tell "nothing was detected" apart from "the existence check silently failed"; and the entire feature depended on a single hover-triggered async round-trip (180ms debounce + IPC call) succeeding while the cursor happened to stay put. Fixed both: (1) `.fp-candidate` now gets a dim, muted dotted underline immediately on render, in every build, independent of verification — `.fp-verified` upgrades it to the full accent-colored link look once confirmed. This makes the feature self-diagnosing on a rebuild: no underline anywhere means detection itself isn't running; a dim underline that never upgrades means verification (the Tauri bridge, or the path just not existing) is the broken half. (2) Verification moved from hover-triggered to **eager, cached, once per unique path**: new `verifyVisibleFpCandidates()`, called at the end of `renderVisibleRows()`, resolves every newly-seen candidate path right after render (`fpPathCache`, deduped via `fpPathPending` so an in-flight check is never started twice) instead of waiting for a hover to survive its debounce window — hovering an already-resolved span now just opens the popup from the cache, with no IPC call and no timing dependency at all. Also fixes redundant IPC calls for a path that recurs across many rows in one file (the common case, e.g. the same config path logged on every save) — now checked exactly once instead of once per hover. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 199 extended: 199b now asserts the underline renders immediately (computed style) with no `window.philogg`; 199c asserts verification happens with zero `mouseover` dispatched and exactly one `pathExists()` call, including after a subsequent hover; new 199d pins the per-path dedupe (two rows, same path, one call); 199e (was 199d) keeps the nonexistent-path case. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3848 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Merged the two release workflows into one shared build with two entry points: "Build Tester Files" (artifacts only) and "Build Release" (same builds, also published as a GitHub Release)** (this session, 2026-09-11, person-requested). Previously `release.yml` published only `philogg.html` and `desktop-release.yml` only published desktop installers, each always creating its own GitHub Release. Replaced both with `.github/workflows/build-tester-files.yml` and `.github/workflows/build-release.yml`: both share the same five `workflow_dispatch` checkboxes (`build_html` default on, `build_windows`/`build_windows_portable`/`build_mac`/`build_linux` default off) and the same stamp/strip/build steps for every variant, so HTML is now just another selectable variant alongside the desktop ones instead of always being built via a separate workflow. `build-tester-files.yml` uploads each selected variant via `actions/upload-artifact` and creates no release. `build-release.yml` runs the identical builds but publishes them together under one GitHub Release tagged `build-<short-sha>` (a `prepare` job creates the release up front so `build_html`/`build_desktop` can each just upload into it). Which branch to build from is picked via the workflow's own built-in branch selector in the Actions UI (`workflow_dispatch` already supports this, no extra input needed). `README.md`, `PROJECT.md`, `docs/desktop.md`, `docs/ui-and-views.md`, `desktop/README.md`, `philogg.html`, `scripts/strip-comments.js` and `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` updated to reference the new workflow files/names. No `philogg.html` runtime behavior changed, so the regression suite was not run.

- **Added: clickable local file paths in log lines, desktop build only** (this session, 2026-09-10, person-requested). An absolute Windows/UNC/Unix path detected inside a rendered log line's message field is wrapped in a `.fp-candidate` span (`linkifyPaths`, `philogg.html`, applied to `markFieldHtml`'s output) — in every build, so the page renders identically in the browser and desktop wrappers. Only under the desktop (Tauri) wrapper, where `window.philogg` exists, does hovering one actually verify it: a debounced hover (~180ms) calls the new `window.philogg.pathExists(path)`, backed by a new `path_exists` Tauri command (`std::fs::metadata`, `desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`) — a single syscall, effectively instant even for many candidates on a page. A path that exists gets styled as a link (`.fp-verified`) and a hover popup (`#fpPathMenu`, same shape as the existing `#addToSelectionMenu`/`#treeCtxInfoMenu`) offering **"Open file"** (new `openPath`/`open_path` command, via `tauri_plugin_opener`'s `open_path`) and **"Open containing folder"** (the existing `revealPath`, already used for FEATURE_BACKLOG #52). A path that doesn't exist is never styled and never shows the popup — deliberately, since a path only means something when the log is being read on the machine that wrote it. The plain browser build (File System Access API) has no way to validate or open an arbitrary path string, so there the span renders but stays inert. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 199** — the path-detection regex (Windows/Unix positives, a fraction/date/URL negative, skipping existing `<mark>` tags rather than matching across them), no popup without `window.philogg` (browser build), a verified path opening the popup with both actions working, and an unverified (nonexistent) path never getting styled or opening anything. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3841 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Follow-up: the actual root cause of the previous entry's file-loading failure — `res.blob()` over `philogg://local/…` failed above some size, `res.arrayBuffer()` never did** (this session, 2026-09-10, person-reported back after the previous fix didn't help, with the detail that made this diagnosable: two files, same log format, same folder — a ~3KB one loaded fine via both drag&drop and the Open… dialog, an ~80KB one failed via BOTH, but loaded fine through a folder watch on that same folder via double-click). All three routes fetch a `philogg://local/…` response, but `loadDesktopLocalFiles`'s `openFile()` (drag&drop + the dialog — the previous entry's `nativeDragDropOwnsThis()` guard and toast-reason fix were real improvements but not the cause) was the only one reading it via `res.blob()`; `urlTailHandle.getFile()` (folder watch, tailing) and `loadUrlIntoTree` (file-association `?url=` deep link) both already used `res.arrayBuffer()` — and both already worked, including for large/growing files, which is exactly the tell. `.blob()` over that custom scheme reliably failed once the response crossed some size threshold between 3KB and 80KB under the Tauri webview; `.arrayBuffer()` of the identical URL never did. Fixed by switching `openFile()` to `res.arrayBuffer()`, matching the two routes that already worked. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **198d** — a wrapper-supplied load succeeds through a fetch mock implementing ONLY `arrayBuffer()` (no `blob()` at all), so a regression back to `res.blob()` fails loudly instead of silently. **3826 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: individual files could fail to load under the Tauri desktop wrapper (drag&drop and the Open… dialog alike) — flashed grey in the tree, then vanished with "Couldn't load"** (this session, 2026-09-10, person-reported: "In der Tauri Variante kann ich keine einzelnen Dateien mehr Laden. Weder per Drag&Drop noch über den Dialog... erscheint kurz grau im Tree, dann verschwindet sie und das 'Could not load' Popup erscheint."). Both routes converge on `loadFileDescriptors`, so the shared point of failure was the queued placeholder's own load attempt. Root cause: `philogg.html`'s own `window` `"drop"` listener had no guard against a desktop wrapper's *native* drag-drop handling (`windows.rs`) also owning the same drop — the wrapper's own comment states the native handler "suppresses the HTML drop events philogg.html would otherwise use," but that suppression is a webview/platform behavior this page never verified, not something it enforces. Whenever it doesn't hold, one OS drop reaches BOTH the wrapper's native handler (which loads the real path through `philogg://local/…`, see `loadDesktopLocalFiles`) AND this page's own listener a second time — racing the wrapper's real load with a redundant one for the same drop, which is exactly the flash-then-vanish symptom. Fix: new `nativeDragDropOwnsThis()` (true whenever `window.philogg` exists) now gates `dragenter`/`dragover`/`dragleave`/`drop` in `philogg.html`, so the wrapper's native handling is the only thing that ever processes an OS file drop there — a no-op change for a build where the HTML-suppression already worked (those listeners were unreachable dead code then), and a real fix everywhere it doesn't. Also: `loadFileDescriptors`' "Couldn't load" toast used to swallow the actual failure (an HTTP status from `openFile`'s fetch, a parse error, …) — it now appends the reason, so a genuine read/parse failure is distinguishable from this race (or anything else) without needing devtools. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 198** — the HTML drop listener no-ops when `window.philogg` exists but still works unmodified in the plain browser build, and a failed wrapper-supplied load's toast now names the reason (e.g. "HTTP 404"), not just the filename. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3824 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Plot settings: inherit at creation, and fixed a persistence gap for a filter that only inherits its Plot tab from an ancestor** (this session, 2026-09-10, person-requested). (1) A new filter created under a node with a configured Plot (e.g. a timerange via the plot's own "Select") now inherits a one-time deep copy of the parent's `plotConfig` at creation — `createFilterNode()`, gated only on `parent.plotConfig` existing, applies regardless of the new node's own `filterType`, so it covers every creation path (text popup, Level, timerange, Idset, …) through the one shared function. (2) Found while implementing (1): a node that only INHERITS its Table/Plot tab from an extraction-pattern ancestor (`findExtractionAncestor`/`nodeIsExtractionView`) rather than carrying the pattern itself — exactly a timerange created from a plot's "Select" — has its own real, independently-configurable `plotConfig`, but all four persistence carriers (`cloneSubtree`, `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters`) gated it on `node.filterType === "text"`, which such a node never is — so its plot silently reverted to blank after copy/paste, filter-file export/import, or a session-cache restore (only undo/redo was already correct). Fixed by gating on `nodeIsExtractionView(node)` at all four call sites instead — the same predicate that already decides whether the node shows a Plot tab at all. (3) Also fixed a related gap, separately person-reported: switching a plot's chart type to Scatter/3D (single-Y-series types) destructively truncated `plotConfig.yCols` in place down to one column; switching back to Line/Bar left the rest of the original multi-column selection permanently gone, even though every other plot field (X/Z/Color/axisEqual/limits) already correctly survived a type switch (same shared `plotConfig` object across types). New `plotConfig.yColsMulti` field stashes the full selection before truncating and restores it on switching back. `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` updated ("Plot config: inherited at creation, and persisted for a node that only INHERITS the Plot tab"). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 197** — inherited-copy independence (edits on either side stay isolated), the inheriting timerange node's `plotConfig` surviving all four persistence carriers (fails on three without the gate fix), a plain non-inheriting timerange node correctly getting/keeping no `plotConfig` (no regression), and a Line→Scatter→Line round trip preserving the full Y-column selection. **3818 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: a background-tailed file growing could reset unrelated in-progress UI state (e.g. a tree row rename)** (this session, 2026-09-09, person-reported: "Wir hatten jetzt schon öfter, das ein Render Dinge geändert/zurückgesetzt hat die gerade gar nicht angepasst werden sollten"). `onTailChange()` called a full `render()` on every poll where ANY tailed root grew — including one that wasn't behind the currently active view at all. `render()` always runs `renderTree()`, which wipes and rebuilds `#tree`'s entire `innerHTML`, discarding node DOM identity (an already-known gotcha, see "DOM identity across clicks" in `PROJECT.md`) — so any interaction mid-flight elsewhere in the tree got reset on the very next `TAIL_POLL_MS` tick of an unrelated background file, purely because it happened to still be growing. Fix: `onTailChange()` now only calls `render()` when a changed root is behind the active view (`getRootFileId(state.activeId)`); for a background-only change it instead calls `updateLoadRowProgress(rootId)` — the same cheap, targeted per-row count refresh load ticks already use — so the growing file's own tree row still reflects its new entry count without touching the rest of the DOM. Also did a broader review of every full-rebuild render path (`render()`/`renderTree()`/`renderVisibleRows()`/`renderHighlightVisibleRows()`) for other unnecessarily-broad call sites (duplicate/dead-code/performance review requested alongside); the render-scope rule this fix follows is now documented in `PROJECT.md` → "Known gotchas" for future call sites to check against. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 196** — two tailed files, one active and one backgrounded; asserts zero `render()` calls and DOM node identity preserved for the backgrounded file's tree row across a poll tick (fails without the fix), its `.tree-count` still updating, and that growth behind the active view still triggers exactly one real `render()`. **3801 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: "Show M newest files" caused an unbounded merge+render loop every poll, felt laggy, and could yank a scrolled-away selection back into view** (this session, 2026-09-09, person-reported: "das Scrolling fühlt sich laggy an, wenn ein Folder Watch aktiv ist" plus a selected row snapping back while scrolling, both traced to this one setting). `folderScanTick`'s "did anything change" check compared each poll's scan against `folder.files` — but `applyFolderAutoRules`'s "Show newest M" rule (rule 1) actively drops closed listing entries beyond the newest M straight out of `folder.files`, even though nothing disappeared from disk. The very next poll's scan still found those files; since they no longer matched anything in the (now-shrunk) `folder.files`, they looked brand new, triggering `mergeScannedFiles` (which puts them back) and then `applyFolderAutoRules` again (which immediately re-hides them) — forever, every `FOLDER_SCAN_MS` (3s), for any folder with more matching files than its configured "Show newest M". Each cycle's unwanted `render()` could also snap a deliberately-scrolled-away selection straight back into view (`captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor` prioritizing the still-selected row on every render — see `docs/ui-and-views.md` "Context view") — the exact symptom reported, even though that anchoring mechanism itself was working as designed and is unrelated to filter switches/tailing (which still need it and are unaffected). Root cause traced to `7fa9034` ("Add per-folder Folder watch settings dialog", 2026-09-03), which introduced "Show newest M" as the first auto-rule that removes entries from `folder.files` rather than just opening/closing them. Fix: `folder._lastScanKeys` (a `Set` of every scanned `relPath`) is now written inside `mergeScannedFiles` itself — the one shared merge funnel — and is what `folderScanTick` diffs against instead of `folder.files`, so a file "Show newest M" hid from the listing never looks new again on a later poll purely because of that hiding. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` updated ("Folder watch + lazy loading"). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 195** — 3 static files with "Show newest 1" configured: zero `render()` calls across 4 stable polls (fails without the fix), the 2 hidden files staying hidden instead of flickering, and a genuinely new file still being detected correctly. **3794 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Follow-up: native "include subfolders" was slow, and "keep N newest" picked the wrong files across subfolders** (this session, 2026-09-09, person-reported from manual testing of the entry right below, ~30 files across several subfolders). Two bugs, one root cause: `scanFolderHandle`'s recency sort needs each file's mtime and fell back to an extra `getFile()` round trip per file when a handle didn't already carry one — for a native (Tauri) listing that fetches the file's real *content* over `philogg://local/…` for nothing, since the resulting `Blob` has no `lastModified` at all, so it bought nothing and cost an extra IPC + disk read per file on every rescan (the "few seconds"/"20-30 seconds" delays). With mtime absent, `sortFolderRecsByRecency` fell back to name (`relPath`) order — which groups by subfolder path first, so once subfolders were involved, "keep N newest" could pick stale files from whichever subfolder sorted last alphabetically instead of the actually-newest ones (exactly what was reported: 3 older files from one subfolder instead of the true 3 newest). Fix: `commands.rs::LocalFile` gained an `mtime: Option<u64>` field (`fs::metadata().modified()`, same units as JS `Date.now()`), read once per file during the same `read_dir` pass `list_folder` already does; `nativeDirHandle.values()` (`philogg.html`) attaches it to each yielded handle; `scanFolderHandle` uses `entryHandle.mtime` directly when present instead of calling `getFile()`. Also parallelized what was serial: `nativeDirHandle.values()` now calls `list_folder`/`list_subfolders` together (`Promise.all`) instead of one after the other, and `scanFolderHandle`'s own recursion now walks sibling subdirectories together instead of one at a time. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: `nativeFolderBridge` gained an `mtimes` param mirroring the new field; new **145g** — a top-level file that sorts last alphabetically but is chronologically oldest, against two subfolder files that are the real newest — asserts "keep 2 newest" picks the mtime-correct files, not the relPath-correct ones. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3790 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: "Include subfolders" was a silent no-op for a watched folder under the desktop (Tauri) wrapper** (this session, 2026-09-09, person-reported). Root cause: `scanFolderHandle`'s recursion (`philogg.html`) only descends into an entry `handle.values()` yields with `kind === "directory"` — a real `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` yields those, but `nativeDirHandle`'s `values()` only ever yielded files from `philogg.listFolder` (backed by `commands.rs::list_folder`, a flat non-recursive `read_dir`), so under the wrapper there was never a directory entry to recurse into, regardless of the setting. Fix: new Tauri command `list_subfolders(path)` (`desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs`, registered in `main.rs`) returns a directory's immediate subdirectories, exposed as `philogg.listSubfolders` (`inject.js`); `nativeDirHandle.values()` (`philogg.html`) now also calls it and yields each result as a nested `nativeDirHandle` with `kind: "directory"`, so `scanFolderHandle`'s existing recursion (unchanged) works identically to the browser case. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: `nativeFolderBridge` (Group 145's mock wrapper) gained a matching `listSubfolders`; new **145f** watches a two-levels-deep native folder tree and asserts the setting actually recurses (and stops recursing when turned back off), including `relPath` for nested files. `docs/desktop.md` updated. **3787 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

(b) Fixed the header/column misalignment this exposed: `#tableRows`/`#highlightRows` reserve a dynamic left gutter for the highlight-color/bookmark/context-marker lane (`--rows-pad-left`, which grows with `--color-mark-w` and, for `#highlightRows.ctx-active`, with the wider context-marker lane), but `#tableHeader`/`#highlightHeader` had a fixed `14px` left padding — so every `.th` after the first walked out of alignment with its column whenever the gutter grew past that (which it always does, even at rest, since the base gutter is already `26px`). `#tableHeader`/`#highlightHeader`'s left padding is now the exact same `calc(26px + (var(--color-mark-w) - 3px))` formula (no text lives in that padding — it's the header-side counterpart of the rows' own reserved gutter, so the rest of the header stays pinned over the content as the gutter grows/shrinks), and `#highlightHeader` gets a `.ctx-active` class toggled in lockstep with `#highlightRows` (`updateContextToolbar()`) so its padding also widens to the `38px` context-marker variant. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 151's DOM-order assertion updated for the new `contextToolbar → highlightHeader → highlightBody` order. `docs/ui-and-views.md` updated. **3782 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **Fixed: breadcrumb chips still not vertically centered — asymmetric per-chip margin, not `#breadcrumbBar`'s padding, was the cause** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-reported follow-up to the entry right below, with a second annotated mockup measuring the actual top/bottom gap). `#breadcrumbBar{align-items:center}` only centers `#breadcrumb`'s WHOLE box — it can't see or correct an asymmetric margin on the `.crumb` chips inside it. `#breadcrumb .crumb` still carried its old `margin:0 4px 6px 0` (asymmetric 0px top / 6px bottom, a leftover from the pre-`#breadcrumbBar` era when that extra bottom margin only served as a gap between wrapped lines), which shifted the visible pill toward the TOP of `#breadcrumb`'s own box — so once that box got centered, the gap below the pill visibly exceeded the gap above it. Fix, scoped to `#breadcrumb` only (`.crumb`/`.crumb-sep` are shared with `.filter-target-chain`/`#extractFilterChain`, untouched): `#breadcrumb .crumb{margin:2px 4px 2px 0}` (symmetric, small enough to keep shrinking the row) and `#breadcrumb .crumb-sep{margin:10px 3px 10px}` (top re-derived from `8px`→`10px` to keep its glyph centered against the chip's now-`2px`-margin-top box). `#breadcrumbBar`'s `min-height` corrected `36px`→`40px` to match the real content height (28px chip + 2×2px margin + 2×4px padding) — it was already an underestimate; the actual rendered height was unaffected, only the documented/floor value was wrong. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 26's stale "chips align to `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar`'s float start" assertion (dead rationale since breadcrumb moved into its own row) replaced with a symmetric-margin assertion. `docs/ui-and-views.md` updated. **3782 passed, 0 failed** (`cd tests && npm test`).

- **"Notes" overview: a second auto-managed filter node, directly below "Bookmarks"** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested). It behaves like any other filter (jump into the Filtered tab, see the entry in its Context view surroundings) but its match set is exactly "this file's entries with a note" (`state.notes.has(e.id)`), same one-line pattern the `"bookmarks"` branch of `getEntries()` already uses. Selecting it force-enables Show Notes (`state.showNotes`) even if it was off — the whole point is reading every note in the file at a glance — and leaving it restores Show Notes to whatever it was before, via a small extension to `render()`'s existing "did `state.activeId` change" check (the same one driving `applyActivationView`). `setNoteAndRepaint` now calls `syncNotesFilterNode`+`renderTree()` after every note add/edit/delete, same as `toggleBookmark` already does for Bookmarks; every existing bookmark-restore call site (session cache restore, session import's `applySessionEntryByOrdinal`/`applySessionEntryByContent`) now also re-derives the Notes node. - **Follow-up: Link dialog's order-enforce gating now hides the row instead of disabling it, and both link options use the pill toggle** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested).
