The previous fix (below) made "Auto-close — keep only N open" a genuine sliding window, verified with alphabetically-increasing filenames — but the underlying "which file is newest" heuristic was still pure filename order, which silently breaks for any real naming scheme where a chronologically newer file doesn't happen to sort last as a plain string (unpadded day-of-month, a rotated-log scheme where the *active* file has the smallest name, etc.) — exactly the kind of case that would look, from the outside, like "the new file just doesn't open." `scanFolderHandle` now fetches each pattern-matching file's real `file.lastModified` via one extra `getFile()` call per file — gated behind a new `folderSettingsNeedRecency()` check so a folder with no auto rules configured pays nothing extra on its 3s poll — and a new `sortFolderRecsByRecency()` (mtime when both sides have one, name order as fallback) replaces the plain-array order everywhere `applyFolderAutoRules` picks "the newest" (show-newest-M, close-keep-N's window, auto-open-newest). mtime isn't available for a natively listed (desktop wrapper) folder's files — `urlTailHandle.getFile()` returns a plain fetched `Blob`, which has no `lastModified` — so the name-order fallback still matters there. Also re-verified, this time end-to-end through the real dialog checkboxes rather than direct `folder.settings` mutation: "Show relative path" now confirmed to update an **already-open** file's shown name live in both directions (on → off → on) with no re-open needed, not just right after opening it — this was already correct from the first follow-up below, just not previously tested through the actual UI controls. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 164** (extended in place) — a `Z-old.log`/`A-new.log` mtime-vs-name-order case; the keep-N sliding window re-tested through manually-opened files + the real settings-dialog number input + the real `folderScanTick` poll (not a manual `rescanFolder()` call); "show relative path" toggled through the dialog's real checkboxes, live, in both directions, on an already-open file. **3236 passed, 0 failed** (3226 + 10).

- **Folder watch settings follow-up: "Auto-close — keep only N open" now slides its window instead of stalling, and a relative-path name now survives opening the file** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-reported, follow-up to the settings dialog below). Two bugs: (1) `applyFolderAutoRules`'s auto-close rule only ever closed excess open files — it never opened the newer file that pushed the count over N, so with 2 already open and a 3rd, newer match appearing, the new one silently stayed unopened instead of the intended "oldest closes, newest opens, the N newest stay open." Rewritten as a genuine sliding window: for a pattern with `autoCloseKeep` set, `recs.slice(-N)` (the N newest) is computed first, whichever of those aren't open yet are opened (awaited), THEN whatever is open but fell out of that window gets closed — so a new arrival correctly evicts the previous oldest. "Auto-open newest file" (no keep-N configured) is unchanged, and is now explicitly skipped for a pattern that also has `autoCloseKeep` set, since that pattern's own window already covers it. (2) `nodeDisplayName()` (the one function every name display — tree row, breadcrumb, tab — already reads through) didn't know about a folder-watch file's `relPath`, so a file opened with "Show relative path" on reverted to its bare filename the instant it was loaded, even though the still-grayed listing kept showing the full path. `nodeDisplayName` now looks up a `folderId`-tagged file node's owning folder and, when `includeSubfolders`+`showRelativePath` are both on, returns its listing record's `relPath` instead of `node.name` — display-only, exactly like the pre-existing `node.label` override for filter nodes, so the underlying `node.name` (identity, matching in `folder.files`) is untouched. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 164** (extended in place) — the sliding-window scenario (2 open, keep-2, a newer 3rd file arrives → opens, the previous oldest closes, the still-in-window one is left untouched) plus a "Show newest M" follow-on with two open files; `nodeDisplayName()` and the actual tree row both keep showing the relative path after double-clicking to open the file. **3226 passed, 0 failed** (3224 + 2 net, after rewriting 3 pre-existing assertions in place for the new fixture).

- **Folder watch: per-folder settings dialog — filename patterns (with per-pattern auto-open-newest/auto-close-keep-N/show-newest-M) and include-subfolders/show-relative-path** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-requested). A gear icon (`.folder-watch-settings`, `ICON_GEAR`) on each watched folder's header opens `#folderWatchSettingsDialog` — styled with the same `settings-page-card`/`settings-row`/`settings-switch` classes as the app-wide Settings dialog, just scoped to one folder. Configurable per folder: **filename patterns** (`folder.settings.patterns`, default one `"*"` pattern — files matching none of the configured globs, reusing the filename-rule glob engine `getCompiledGlob`, are filtered out on top of the existing fixed `.log` extension filter), each with its own **auto-open newest file** (opens+selects the pattern's alphabetically-last match as soon as it appears, tailing/auto-scroll included — same effect as a manual open, via the existing `loadFolderFile`/`loadOneFileIntoTree` path), **auto-close — keep only N open** (closes the oldest open matches beyond N via the existing `closeFolderFile`, so they stay listed grayed out, not removed), and **show M newest files** (drops *closed* listing entries older than the M newest — an open file is never dropped); and folder-wide **include subfolders** (recursive scan — `scanFolderHandle` now walks subdirectories when a handle exposes `values()` on them; a no-op under the desktop wrapper's native folder listing, which is a flat, non-recursive `read_dir`) with **show relative path** (shows `sub/App.log` instead of just `App.log` in the grayed listing once subfolders are included). All three settings persist with the folder record (IndexedDB `folders` store, `persistFolder`/`restoreWatchedFolders`) and every change re-persists + re-scans immediately (no separate Save button, same immediacy as the rest of Settings). A file's merge identity switched from its bare name to `relPath` (`folderFileKey`) so same-named files in different subfolders don't collide; a closed file that stops matching after a pattern change is now dropped from the listing (an *open* one removed from disk is unaffected — still stays listed, as before). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 164** (new) — default `"*"` pattern behaves exactly as before; narrowing to `App*.log`/`Input*.log` drops non-matching files; the settings dialog opens/reflects/edits patterns live; recursive subfolder scan + relative-path display; and all three per-pattern auto rules (open-newest, close-keep-N, show-newest-M) end to end. **3224 passed, 0 failed** (3201 + 23).

- **Context view: hovering a run's fold line now highlights the whole run, not just the one row segment under the pointer** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-requested follow-up to the arrow-direction tweak below). Each revealed row contributes its own `.ctx-run-line` button (see `makeContextRunHandle`), so plain CSS `:hover` only ever lit up the segment actually under the cursor. Every slice of the same run now carries a shared `data-run-id` (`run.firstRow`), and `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` toggle a `.ctx-run-hover` class across all of them at once (queried within `#highlightRows`), including both end caps' carets — same visual as `:hover` (`.ctx-run-line:hover, .ctx-run-line.ctx-run-hover{...}`). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 163** (new) — hovering a middle slice, the top cap, and (after scrolling it into the virtualized window) the bottom cap each highlight every currently-rendered slice of the run, clearing again on mouseleave. **3201 passed, 0 failed** (3190 + 11).

- **Two small UI tweaks (this session, 2026-09-03, person-requested):**
  1. **Context view run-fold arrows point inward now, not outward.** `makeContextRunHandle`'s top/bottom caret caps (`ICON_CARET_UP`/`ICON_CARET_DOWN`, the fold buttons at the ends of a run's connecting line in the gutter) were swapped: the top cap now shows the down-chevron and the bottom cap the up-chevron, so both point toward the middle of the expanded run — the direction it collapses to — instead of away from it, which used to read like an "expand further" hint on a control that actually collapses.
  2. **Column statistics bar (above the extraction Table/Plot) is now collapsible, default collapsed.** `#extractStatsBar` gained a small `#extractStatsToggle` header button (`renderStatsBar` now writes the chips into a nested `#extractStatsContent` instead of the bar itself) and a persisted `extractStatsCollapsed` flag (`localStorage["philogg-extract-stats-collapsed"]`, same wiring shape as the sidebar/detail-panel collapse flags). Table and Plot share the one `#extractStatsBar` element (it lives in `#extractToolbar`, above both), so toggling it from either tab toggles it for both — no separate state needed. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 162** (new) — defaults to collapsed with nothing stored; the toggle expands/collapses and persists; content still renders while collapsed (CSS-hidden only); the shared state surviving a Table↔Plot tab switch either direction. **3190 passed, 0 failed** (3177 + 13).

- **A brand-new extraction-capable filter no longer auto-jumps to Table on its very first activation — it now opens to Filtered, with a new Settings → Behavior option controlling what a previously-visited node reopens to** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-reported follow-up to the two sessions below: the old unconditional auto-jump read as "the filter isn't matching anything" the very first time a wildcard filter was created, before the tab bar was noticed). `render()`'s `activeId`-changed block now calls the new `applyActivationView(activeNode)` instead of unconditionally revealing Filtered then jumping wildcard nodes to Table. A node that has **never been the active node before** (no entry yet in the new in-memory `nodeLastView` Map, keyed by node id) always lands on Filtered now, regardless of wildcards or the setting below. A node visited before goes by the new **"When switching to a filter"** row (`#settingsFilterActivationView`, backed by `filterActivationView`/`FILTER_ACTIVATION_VIEW_KEY` in `localStorage`, same wiring shape as the neighboring "Context/Filtered display" row): `"rememberLast"` (default) restores whichever tab — Context/Filtered/Table/Plot, or the merged Stacked pane — was last shown for *that specific node*; `"alwaysFiltered"` always lands on Filtered again. `nodeLastView` is recorded at the end of every `applyFhView()` call (the one place every tab switch funnels through, not just `applyActivationView` itself), so it stays live from ordinary tab clicks too; it's intentionally in-memory only, a per-session convenience like scroll position rather than part of the filter definition. Two sane fallbacks to Filtered instead of a dead/invalid tab: a node remembered on Table/Plot whose pattern was edited since to drop its wildcards (`nodeHasExtractableWildcards` now false), and a node remembered as `"stacked"` after the person has since switched the global "Context/Filtered display" setting back to "Separate". One behavioral widening worth flagging: this also changes *plain* (non-extraction) filter nodes — under `rememberLast`, reactivating a filter last left on the Context tab now restores Context instead of always forcing Filtered, superseding that part of Group 61's original "switching filters auto-reveals Filtered" coverage. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Which view a filter node opens to" and `docs/ui-implementation-plan.md`'s "Nachfolge-Session" for the full writeup. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 161** (new) — first-ever activation always Filtered regardless of setting; `rememberLast` restoring Table/Plot/Context per node and no-op for plain Filtered; `alwaysFiltered` overriding a remembered Table tab; the wildcard-removed fallback; the Settings row's localStorage round-trip and default; the stacked-layout-reverted-to-Separate fallback. Group 61c's "switching back to an already-Context-shown filter" assertion updated for the widened remember-last scope described above. Roughly a dozen other pre-existing groups (14, 24, 42, 45, 46, 53, 54, 55b/155b-e, 60c, 81, 125, 157a, 158a/d, 159a/b, 160a/b, and Group 27's pin-bookmarks extract-table check) added an explicit `w.applyFhView("table"/"plot")` call at the point their setup previously relied on the retired auto-jump — not a behavior change, just making each test's precondition explicit. **3177 passed, 0 failed** (3158 + 19).

- **Fix: clicking Context/Filtered while Table/Plot was on screen updated the tab pill but left the extraction table/plot visible underneath — person-reported, immediately after the filterType-merge session below** (this session, 2026-09-03). Root cause: `applyFhView()`'s final `else` branch (`view === "highlight"/"filter"`) only ever called `showFhTab()`, which toggles panel visibility *within* `#fhSplit` but never switches which content component is on screen (`#extractWrap` vs `#fhSplit`) — nothing reached that branch from Table/Plot before Table/Plot existed as tabs on the same group, so it was never taught to handle it. The double-click/plot-click reveal feature (`revealInFilteredView`, added the same day) had already hit and locally patched this exact root cause for its own call site; this fix moves that patch into `applyFhView()` itself so every caller (a plain tab click included) benefits, and removes `revealInFilteredView`'s now-redundant local copy. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 160** (new) — clicking Context from Table and Filtered from Plot, asserting the real `style.display` toggle on `#extractWrap`/`#fhSplit` (not a `.hidden` class check, which is exactly the assertion weakness that let the closely-related Group 159 bug through undetected the first time — strengthened there too). **3158 passed, 0 failed** (3142 + 16).

- **filterType merge: the separate "extract" filter type and its "Extract" popup button are retired — a wildcard "text" filter is simultaneously a normal filter and extraction-capable; double-click a Table row / click a Plot mark now reveals into the SAME node's Filtered view instead of jumping to the raw file** (this session, 2026-09-03, follow-up to `docs/ui-implementation-plan.md`'s unified-toolbar work below). Two related changes:
  1. **filterType merge.** `filterType === "extract"` and `#filterExtractBtn` ("Extract", the second popup button) are gone entirely — `commitFilter()` no longer takes an `asExtract` flag, and "Add filter" is the popup's only outcome. A `"text"` filter node whose pattern contains `[value:...]`/`[*]` wildcards now automatically both filters/highlights normally (unchanged — its matching already went through the same wildcard-regex code path a "text" node's wildcard pattern always used) AND unlocks Table/Plot (`nodeHasExtractableWildcards`, re-gated from `filterType === "extract"` onto `filterType === "text" && !node.isRegex && isExtractPattern(node.value) && compileExtractPattern(node.value)`). Inversion (NOT) is no longer excluded for such a node in the tree context menu — that exclusion existed only because the old "extract" filterType couldn't be inverted meaningfully; a wildcard "text" filter always could. `ignoredColumns`/`assertions`/`columnRenames`/`plotConfig` and their four persistence carriers (`createFilterNode`/`updateFilterNode`, `cloneSubtree`, save/load JSON, session cache) are all re-gated from `filterType === "extract"` onto `filterType === "text"` the same way. **No migration for old `"extract"`-type data** — the person explicitly said none is needed, since no version of the software has shipped yet: `FILTER_TYPES` no longer recognizes `"extract"`, so an old hand-edited/exported node of that type is simply rejected on import rather than kept loadable.
  2. **Double-click / plot-click now reveals in place.** The extraction table row's `dblclick` and the 2D/3D Plot tab's mark-click handlers, which used to call `jumpToFullLog` (jump to the node's raw root file, clearing the level filter — the only option back when an extraction node had no Filtered view of its own), now call the new `revealInFilteredView(entry, sourceRowEl, sourceScrollEl)` instead — modeled directly on `revealInHighlightView`'s Context-view counterpart. It stays on the SAME extraction-capable node (which already has an ordinary Filtered pane, see the unified-toolbar work below), switches `fhActiveTab` to `"filter"`, and selects/scrolls/flashes the row there, preserving its on-screen offset the same way `jumpToFullLog` did on the table-row trigger. `jumpToFullLog` itself is unchanged and still directly tested — it's simply no longer wired to any UI action (kept unwired, same precedent as `copyWholeExtractTable`).

  See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "filterType merge" for the full writeup, and `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Cross-view jumps preserve screen position" for the reveal mechanism. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Groups 1/8/39/40/41 updated in place for the merge (fixture creation now uses `"text"`, the tree context-menu Invert-exclusion test flipped to expect Invert *offered* for a wildcard node, the two-button popup redesign coverage in Group 41 rewritten around the single "Add filter" outcome, column-rename persistence assertions re-pointed at `filterType === "text"`); **158d** rewritten (the old-extract-node-still-imports coverage it held no longer applies and was dropped) to cover creating via the popup unlocking Table/Plot for the same node; **Group 159** (new) covers the double-click/plot-click reveal-in-place feature, including the plain-click-not-a-drag case. **3142 passed, 0 failed** (3143 + minor rewrites/drops, net −1 test).

- **Unified toolbar: Table/Plot join the main Context/Filtered/Stacked tab group instead of their own separate sub-toggle** (this session, 2026-09-03, `docs/ui-implementation-plan.md`). `#fhTabs` now renders `Context | Filtered | Table | Plot` ("Separate", default) or `Stacked | Table | Plot` ("Stacked") depending on a new Settings → Behavior → "Context/Filtered display" option — the only way `fhLayout` changes now; the old "Stacked" tab button that used to set it directly is gone. Table/Plot stay visible but `disabled` (not removed) when the active node has no extractable wildcards (`nodeHasExtractableWildcards`), so the rest of the tab group never shifts sideways. The `isExtract` special path in `renderMainView()` is gone entirely: an extraction node is now an ordinary node for Context/Filtered/Stacked purposes too (it already had real matched entries via `getEntries()`), and just additionally unlocks Table/Plot; activating one still auto-reveals its Table tab, same as before. `#extractViewTabs` (the old Table/Plot sub-toggle inside the extraction toolbar) is removed outright. One deliberate scope-cut versus the plan's original "physical slot row" idea (Schritt 4): `#contextToolbar`/`#extractToolbar`/`#plotToolbar`/the six log-view toggles stay in their pre-existing DOM locations rather than being reparented into a new shared container — reparenting them broke roughly ten existing regression tests asserting that exact DOM structure, more than could be responsibly migrated in the session; only each one's show/hide *condition* changed, from `isExtract`/always-on to `fhActiveTab`-driven. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Unified toolbar: Context/Filtered/Table/Plot as one tab group", `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`, and `docs/ui-implementation-plan.md`'s "Entscheidungen bei der Umsetzung" for the four originally-open decisions and this scope-cut in full. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 158** (new) — Table/Plot visible-but-disabled without wildcards / enabled with them / staying disabled for a wildcard-less extract pattern; the Settings option switching the tab group between its two shapes; the Context tab showing the match navigator while Filtered/Stacked don't; a hand-built pre-rework `extract`-node export still importing and rendering correctly. Groups 26 and 60c updated in place for the new "extract mode hides only while Table/Plot itself is on screen" behavior (their old "everything hides for an extraction node" / "`#extractViewTabs` still exists" assertions no longer hold). **3143 passed, 0 failed** (3117 + 26).

- **Desktop wrapper: Windows portable build — `PhiLogg-<sha>_portable.zip`, run straight off any drive with no install and nothing written to the host machine** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-requested). `settings::portable_dir()` detects a `philogg-portable` marker file next to the running exe; when present, `config_dir()` (`settings.json`) and `create_main`'s new `WebviewWindowBuilder::data_directory(...)` call (the webview's own storage, IndexedDB session cache included) both point at a `data\` folder beside the exe instead of the OS's per-user directories, and `html_path()` gains an exe-adjacent `philogg.html` fallback since the portable build has no installer/resource dir to read it from. The tray's "Open Config Folder"/"Clear Cache" needed no change — both already go through the same `config_dir()`/webview APIs. `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml` gets a fourth checkbox, `build_windows_portable` (default off), which — deduped against `build_windows` for the shared `windows-latest` runner — zips the raw unbundled `philogg-desktop.exe` from the same build together with the stamped `philogg.html` and an empty `philogg-portable` marker, uploaded alongside the installer. Windows-only: WebView2 ships with Windows itself, so there's no equivalent "no install" story on macOS/Linux to build a portable variant for. See `desktop/README.md` → "Portable build (Windows)" and `docs/desktop.md` for both the persistent-data and release-workflow detail.

- **Extraction table: renamable columns (right-click → "Rename column…", or F2 with one column selected); the new name shows in the table, the pattern chip tooltip, and the Plot view** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested). `node.columnRenames: { [colIndex]: string }` is a display-only override, keyed by the same stable `colIndex` `ignoredColumns`/`assertions` already use — the compiled pattern's own token name is untouched. `getColumnDisplayName`/`renameColumn` apply the overlay once, right after `extractColumns` is (re)built, so the table header, `#extractPatternView`'s chip tooltip, and (via `findExtractColumn`) the Plot view's axis titles/legend all pick it up with no per-consumer code. Threaded through every persistence carrier `node.assertions` already established (clone/copy-paste, undo/redo, save/load JSON, session cache), with an import sanitizer (`sanitizeColumnRenames`) dropping any dirty entry (non-integer key, non-string or blank value) individually rather than rejecting the whole map. See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Renamed columns". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 157** (new) — F2 with/without a column selected, the context-menu path, commit/cancel/blank-clears, the renamed name propagating to the header/pattern-chip/plot-axis-title, and all five persistence carriers including the dirty-import sanitizer. **3114 passed, 0 failed** (3087 + 27).

- **Plot view: 2D charts always show axis titles (and, with multiple Y series, the existing per-series legend) — visible in the normal view, fullscreen, and the exported/saved image; fix: the Fullscreen view's × button didn't close a 3D chart** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported/requested follow-up to the entry below). Two related fixes: (1) `renderPlotChart` now always draws the plotted column names as real axis titles (`.plot-axis-title`, `PLOT_MARGIN` widened to fit) — X always, Y only when there's exactly one Y series (with several, the existing top-of-chart per-series legend already names each by its own color) — and since both are part of `#plotSvg` itself, they show up in fullscreen and the saved PNG automatically; the separate DOM-only fullscreen legend added in the entry below was removed as redundant. (2) The fullscreen `×` button worked for a 2D chart but not a 3D one: it was `position:absolute` over `#plotChartArea`, a later sibling in the same stacking context that — per CSS's tree-order rule for same-`z-index:auto` positioned siblings — painted on top of it; harmless for `#plotSvg` (hit-tested only where something is drawn, and that corner was empty) but not for `#plot3dCanvas` (hit-tested everywhere in its box), which silently ate the click. Fixed by giving the button its own `#plotFullscreenHeader` row above the chart instead of overlaying it. See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Plot zoom / pan / hover tooltip". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 156** rewritten — 156a now also exercises the fullscreen X-button close for a 3D chart specifically (the actual reported bug), 156b (new) covers the axis titles/legend for one and two Y series; the old DOM-fullscreen-legend assertions were replaced since that mechanism is gone. Also fixed two Group 53/54 geometry assertions whose hardcoded pixel margins needed updating for the new `PLOT_MARGIN`. **3087 passed, 0 failed** (3079 + 8).

- **Plot view: its own toolbar (zoom/pan/filter moved out of the shared extraction toolbar), plus Fullscreen and Save-image buttons** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested, styled after the Context view's own toolbar). `#plotZoomBar` (previously a row of the shared `.extract-toolbar-row2`, visible on the Plot tab only) is now `#plotToolbar`, a real row in normal flow at the top of `#plotWrap` itself — the same in-flow-row-not-an-overlay pattern `#contextToolbar` already established for the Context view. Its zoom/pan controls and the two "Create filter from plot view" buttons live in a `#plot2dToolsGroup` sub-row, hidden the same way the old bar was (no 2D render yet, or chart type is 3D); a second group with the two new buttons is always shown. **Fullscreen** (`#plotFullscreenBtn`) shows just the chart plus a legend of the plotted Y series — no sidebar, no toolbar, no table — by physically moving the existing `#plotChartArea` node into a fullscreen overlay and back (reparenting keeps every zoom/pan/hover listener already bound to it working, no duplicated render path); exits via the `×` button or Escape. **Save** (`#plotSaveImageBtn`) exports the plot to a PNG exactly as currently zoomed/panned, by rasterizing the live `#plotSvg` (with computed styles inlined, since a standalone SVG has no access to the page's own stylesheet) or `#plot3dCanvas`. See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Plot zoom / pan / hover tooltip". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 156** (new) — the toolbar now lives inside `#plotWrap` rather than the shared extraction toolbar, fullscreen open/close (button and Escape) moves the actual chart-area node and shows/hides the legend, and the Save button doesn't throw when clicked. **3079 passed, 0 failed** (3066 + 13).

- **Fix: pasting into Excel still broke even with the confirmed-correct decimal separator — clipboard copy now writes an Excel-native `x:num` HTML flavor, sidestepping decimal-separator guessing entirely** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported follow-up: even explicitly picking "." — confirmed as what that Excel install both types and calculates with — still pasted with the point missing entirely; picking "," still shifted by a factor of 10). Root cause: Excel's plain-text-paste number recognition applies its own additional heuristics on top of whichever separator character arrives, unqueryable from a web page, and can misfire regardless of which character is objectively correct — so no choice of separator in the TSV text could ever be made fully reliable. Real fix: `copyTextToClipboard(text, html)` now also writes a `text/html` clipboard flavor (via the Chromium `ClipboardItem`/`clipboard.write` API, falling back cleanly to the old `writeText`-only path where that's unavailable) alongside the plain-text TSV. Excel — like every major spreadsheet app — prefers `text/html` over `text/plain` when both are present, and reads a numeric `<td>`'s `x:num="…"` attribute as the literal value with **no locale interpretation at all** (always period-decimal, per the MS Office HTML-clipboard spec) — the exact mechanism Excel's own Ctrl+C already emits for numeric cells. `buildExtractClipboardHtml` builds this; `extractClipboardCell` produces each cell's `{value, raw, numeric}`, where `raw` (the x:num attribute) is deliberately never localized, only `value` (the visible `<td>` text / the plain-text TSV fallback) is — so Excel now gets the exact value regardless of decimal-separator settings on either end, and the Settings override below remains relevant only for the plain-text fallback and other apps. See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Copying and exporting the extraction table". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 155e** (new) — the HTML flavor's x:num carries the raw invariant value while the visible text/TSV stay localized, the MSO namespace is present, `copyWholeExtractTable` goes through the same path with its header row, and the writeText-only fallback still works when `ClipboardItem` is absent (unchanged behavior for every pre-existing test, since jsdom has no `ClipboardItem`). **3066 passed, 0 failed** (3059 + 7).

- **Fix: auto-detected decimal separator could mismatch the actual spreadsheet app, mangling pasted numbers by a factor of 10** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported with a screenshot: `27.7120` pasted into Excel as `277,120` — `navigator.language`'s guess for the clipboard's decimal separator didn't match what that Excel install actually parsed, so a "," we inserted as a decimal point got read back as a thousands separator instead). `Intl.NumberFormat(navigator.language)` reflects the *browser's* language, not necessarily the OS/spreadsheet-app's number-format locale, and there is no web API that exposes the latter directly — so auto-detection alone can't be made reliable. Added an explicit override: Settings → Behavior → "Decimal separator for copy/export" (Auto / Point / Comma, `philogg-clipboard-decimal-sep` in localStorage, "Auto" = no stored override = same `systemDecimalSeparator()` guess as before). `resolveClipboardDecimalSeparator()` checks the override first; both the Ctrl+C clipboard path and the CSV export dialog's default now go through it instead of calling `systemDecimalSeparator()` directly. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 155d** (new) — no-override falls back to auto-detect, an explicit "." override wins over a deliberately mismatched auto-detect stub, "Auto" clears the stored override, and the CSV dialog's default picks up the override too. **3059 passed, 0 failed** (3053 + 6).

- **Extraction table: clipboard copies use the system decimal separator; new right-click "Export as CSV…" dialog** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported: pasting an extracted data table into Excel mangled floating-point values because the clipboard text always used "." for the decimal point regardless of the OS/browser locale). Two changes, on-screen display is untouched by both:
  1. **Ctrl+C / copyTableSelection and copyWholeExtractTable** now run every numeric cell through the new `localizeNumericCell`/`systemDecimalSeparator` (`Intl.NumberFormat(navigator.language).formatToParts(1.1)`) before writing TSV to the clipboard, swapping "." for whatever the system locale actually uses. Only cells matching a plain `-?\d+\.\d+` float are touched — text and integers pass through unchanged.
  2. **Right-click anywhere in the extraction table** now opens a small `#extractContextMenu` with "Export as CSV…", which opens `#csvExportDialog` (column delimiter, decimal separator, include-header-row checkbox — delimiter/decimal default to the system locale's convention, e.g. `;`+`,` for a comma-decimal locale). `buildExtractCsv` builds an RFC4180-quoted CSV string (`csvQuoteField`), saved via `saveCsvToFile` — `showSaveFilePicker` where available, else `downloadCsvFallback` (Blob + `<a download>`, mirroring the existing `downloadJsonFallback` pattern). See `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` → "Copying and exporting the extraction table". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 155** — 155a covers `localizeNumericCell`/`csvQuoteField` in isolation, 155b covers the Ctrl+C/copy-whole-table clipboard paths under a stubbed system separator, 155c covers the right-click menu, dialog defaults, CSV building/quoting, headers-off, and Cancel. **3053 passed, 0 failed** (3023 + 30).

- **Fix: column auto-fit (double-click a resize handle) was slow on large files** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported follow-up to the entry below). Root cause: `autofitColumnWidth` measured every single entry's text with a real `offsetWidth` read — each one a forced synchronous reflow, so a large file meant thousands of layout thrashes. Fixed the same way `computeMaxMessageWidth` already handles this for the message column: a cheap character-length scan (no layout involved) finds the single longest string across the file, then exactly ONE real measurement runs. No behavior change, same result, just far fewer reflows. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Log view column visibility / width". **3023 passed, 0 failed** (Group 154 unchanged, same assertions still hold).

- **Log view columns: middle-click a header to hide it, double-click a resize handle to auto-fit width to content** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested). Middle-clicking a hideable header title (Δt/Thread/Location/Method) now has the same effect as unchecking it in the existing `#columnsPanel` dropdown — wired on both the Filter and Highlight view headers, since both share the same `--row-grid`/`state.columnVisible`. Double-clicking a column's resize handle (`.col-resize-handle`) auto-fits its width to the widest value found across the **whole active file's entries**, not just the currently filtered view — the Excel "double-click the column border" behaviour. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Log view column visibility / width" for the implementation (`toggleColumnFromHeaderClick`, `autofitColumnWidth`/`measureColumnText`). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 154** (new) — 154a/b cover the middle-click hide on both headers plus popup-checkbox sync, 154c covers the auto-fit sizing to a value beyond the column's default. **3023 passed, 0 failed** (3007 + 16).

- **Fix: Log font no longer reformats unrelated UI chrome, and setting order no longer matters** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported follow-up to the entry below: *"Die Änderung des Fonts scheint sich nicht sofort auf alle Angezeigten Elemente zu wirken... Es wirkt zum Beispiel so, als wären die Level Filter Buttons formatiert wie die Log Einträge."*). Root cause: the new Log font picker (below) drove `--font-mono` directly, but a good number of unrelated UI-chrome elements — `.level-btn` (the level-quick-filter pills), badges, dialog inputs, plot tick labels, session-file rows, etc. — *also* style themselves off `--font-mono` purely for its own monospace look, unrelated to actual log content. So picking a Log font visibly reformatted those UI elements too, and since both the UI font's "Monospace" option and the Log font picker ultimately wrote into the same custom property, whichever was set last could appear to "win" — reading as order-dependent. Fixed by introducing a dedicated `--font-log` custom property, used *only* by the exact set of selectors `--log-text-scale` already scopes to (`.col-time`, `.col-delta`, the level badge, `.col-thread`, `.col-loc`, `.col-method`, `.col-msg`, `#entryDetail`'s message body, `#extractTable`, `.pair-row-time`, `.pair-row-msg` — i.e. table rows, extraction table, entry detail message, link/pair rows, matching `PROJECT.md`'s own definition of "log/text content"). `--font-mono` reverted to fixed/non-configurable, still covering the UI chrome's own monospace bits exactly as before this session. `applyLogFont()` now sets `--font-log` instead of `--font-mono`; `LOG_FONT_OPTIONS`' default stack is unchanged, so existing Log font selections keep behaving the same. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 153** updated throughout to assert on `--font-log`; two new sections — 153c confirms `--font-mono` (and thus `.level-btn`) is untouched by the Log font setting and that a log-row cell/level button resolve to genuinely different font stacks once Log font diverges from default, 153d confirms UI-font-then-Log-font and Log-font-then-UI-font land on the identical end state. **3007 passed, 0 failed** (3002 + 5).

- **Log font, selectable independently of the UI font** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested: *"Ich möchte die Schriftart für das UI und die der eigentlichen Log-Einträge getrennt auswählen können."*). Log/text content (table rows, extraction table, entry detail, link/pair rows) already keyed off the `--font-mono` CSS variable, but nothing in Settings let a person change it — the existing "UI font" picker (`#settingsUiFontSelect`) only touched `--font-ui`. Added a second "Log font" select (`#settingsLogFontSelect`) in Settings → Appearance, right below UI font, using the same curated-stack mechanism (`LOG_FONT_OPTIONS`, including a "Same as UI font" entry pointing at `var(--font-ui)`) plus the desktop `window.philogg.listSystemFonts` extension where available; stored under its own `philogg-log-font` localStorage key so the two pickers are fully independent. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 153** mirrors Group 111f/113 for the Log font select and confirms it doesn't disturb the UI font's own selection; Group 79's Appearance-card row count updated from 5 to 6. **3002 passed, 0 failed** (2983 + 19).

- **Desktop (Windows): Alt+Enter no longer pops the native system menu** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported: extracting a filter with Alt+Enter and then pressing Enter again opened a blank window menu top-left). Cause was WebView2's own handling of Alt as a browser "accelerator key" — the same mechanism Chrome/Edge use to focus a menu bar — firing even though `philogg.html`'s Alt+Enter handler already calls `preventDefault()` on the keydown; the frameless window (`decorations(false)`) has no menu bar of its own, so Windows falls back to the native system menu instead. `desktop/src-tauri/src/windows.rs`'s `create_main` now calls a new `disable_alt_accelerator_keys` (Windows-only) right after building the main window, reaching into the WebView2 controller via `webview2-com`/`windows` (added as `cfg(windows)`-only dependencies in `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml`) to set `ICoreWebView2Settings3::SetAreBrowserAcceleratorKeysEnabled(false)`. No test suite coverage — this is Rust/Tauri-side only, nothing the jsdom regression suite loads; verified by reading the WebView2 API, not by an actual Windows build in this session (no Windows toolchain available here).

- **Filter path pills no longer overflow their own 28px height, and truncate cleanly from the start of the name** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported: a long auto-generated filter name had no width limit of its own, so its text wrapped onto a second line inside the pill, spilling above/below it, with the following arrow-only separator then pushed onto its own line beneath it). `.crumb` (shared by `#breadcrumb` and the "add filter" popup's `#filterTargetChain`) now caps at `max-width:220px`. The chip's `title` tooltip already carried the full name for `#breadcrumb`; `#filterTargetChain`'s chips gained one too, since they had none before. **Two follow-ups in the same session, both person-reported after a real-browser check** (jsdom's computed-style assertions alone don't render layout, so neither surfaced there): (1) `.crumb`'s `justify-content:center` clipped an overflowing chip equally on both ends, showing an unmarked cut into the middle of the name instead of a left-aligned start — switched to `justify-content:flex-start`. (2) Even left-aligned, a real browser showed no clipping and no ellipsis at all: `overflow`/`text-overflow`/`white-space` had been set directly on `.crumb`, but once the chip became `inline-flex` its text turned into an anonymous flex item whose own overflow a flex container's `text-overflow` never elides — so nothing ever clipped and the pill just grew past its visual cap. Fixed by moving the text into an actual child element, `.crumb-label` (`flex:1 1 auto; min-width:0` plus the ellipsis trio), which `renderBreadcrumb()`/`renderFilterTargetChain()` now create instead of setting `chip.textContent` directly — the same `min-width:0`-on-a-flex-child mechanism `.tree-label` already relies on inside `.tree-row`. `docs/ui-and-views.md`: the same paragraph rewritten for the working mechanism. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 152** updated to assert on `.crumb-label` (the element the truncation properties actually live on now) rather than `.crumb` itself. **2983 passed, 0 failed** (2971 + 12).

- **Context view: a "… N lines" click keeps the selected row still** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested after weighing the options for it). Unlike a step row, this one has no revealed run of its own to belong to — it *is* the stretch between two blocks, with a run on both sides in the normal shape, and it reveals all of it — so there is no side that is obviously "its own". The alternative considered was leaving it as it was (always hold the row above, deterministic but backwards whenever you are reading *below* the stretch); this rule is the only one that gets both directions right. `contextSelectionAtOrBelow(filler.to)` decides: a selection at or below the stretch's end holds the row after the filler group (the block being read stays and the lines appear above it), anything else holds the row before it. A selection that is absent, belongs to another file, or sits *inside* the stretch — where it has no row to be held still yet — falls back to the row above, i.e. exactly the previous behaviour. Under the auto-expand default the selection is always the match whose context borders the stretch, so this reads simply as "the block I am reading stays put". `docs/ui-and-views.md`: the pin table's row rewritten plus a paragraph on why this one can't mirror the step rows. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 138 (r)** — the same click growing downwards with the selection above the stretch and upwards with it below, plus the no-selection fallback; each branch confirmed to fail when the pin is hardcoded to the other side. **2971 passed, 0 failed** (2965 + 6).

- **Context view: step rows unfold the right way round, and the view scrolls sideways** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported follow-up to the entry below, with a screenshot). Four things:
  1. **The step rows' direction was inverted.** The previous fix held the *far* side of the insertion, so a block unfolded away from the button that had just been clicked: the step at the bottom of a block pushed everything above it up, the one at its top pushed the block down. The right rule is the other one — a step row **belongs to the revealed run it sits against**, and that run stays put while the block unfolds away from it. So `side: "top"` (the step at the **bottom** of a block, growing that run downwards) now pins the row *before* the filler group, and `side: "bottom"` (the step at its **top**) pins the row *after* it. "… N lines" and the run-line collapse are unchanged.
  2. **The connecting line now meets its arrowheads.** Two separate errors: the line's inset on a capped end was 13px while the chevron's ink only reaches ~8.25px down its own box, leaving a visible gap; and the caret box was offset by −5px when it needed −7px, because an absolutely positioned child is placed against its parent's **padding** box — which here starts *inside* the 2px `border-left` that draws the line, so −5px put the arrows 2px to its right. Measured in a real browser afterwards: the caret's centre and the line's axis are both at x = 281.
  3. **The Context view scrolls horizontally.** `#highlightBody` was `overflow-x:hidden` with an ellipsis; it now mirrors the Filter view's whole mechanism — `.col-msg{min-width:max-content}` extended to `#highlightRows`, `syncHighlightRowsWidth()` giving the rows an explicit width from the widest message in the list (`cachedMaxHighlightMsgPixelWidth`, recomputed in `rebuildHighlightOffsets`), and `syncHighlightHeaderWidth()` plus a `scroll` listener keeping `#highlightHeader` the same width and at the same `scrollLeft`. Unlike `TABLE_ROWS_H_PADDING`, the padding is read off the element rather than hardcoded: this one varies with `ctx-active` (the marker lane) as well as with `--color-mark-w`.
  4. **The filler labels are centred on the visible area**, not on the row — which is now the point, since a row can be far wider than the panel. The label sits in its own `.ctx-filler-inner`: `position:sticky; left:0` against the scrolling body, a negative margin undoing `#highlightRows`' own `--rows-pad-left` (extracted into a custom property for exactly this) so "pinned at 0" means the panel's left edge, and a width taken from `highlightBody.clientWidth` per render. Verified in a real browser at two scroll offsets: the label's centre and the viewport's centre are both 739 at `scrollLeft` 0 *and* at 400.
  `docs/ui-and-views.md`: the pin table corrected and given the "a step belongs to its own run" rule, the filler-row paragraph extended with the centring, and a new "It scrolls horizontally" bullet under "Rendering". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 138 (q)** rewritten for the corrected direction (it had encoded the wrong one) — each case now asserts that the run the step belongs to does not move *and* that the far side moves by exactly the ten revealed rows; **Group 73** updated, since it asserted the Context view stays clipped, which is dead code now, and gained the mirror assertions for its header sync. **2965 passed, 0 failed** (2961 + 4).

- **Context view: a "Show more" click no longer jumps** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported follow-up to the entry below: *"Wenn ich auf ein Show more lines klicke, soll sich das Log nach oben oder unten erweitern … ohne dabei die Einträge der anderen Seite im View zu verschieben. Aktuell springt die Anzeige immer wieder auf die selektierte Zeile, wenn diese ausserhalb des Views liegt."*). `commitContextExpansionChange` routed every hand action through `captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor`, the file's general scroll-anchoring pair — which **prefers `state.selectedId`** and, when the selected row is off screen, reveals it *centred* rather than pinning a pixel spot. That is the right default for a whole-view change, but wrong for a step click: the selection at that moment is usually somewhere else entirely, so revealing ten lines threw the view across the file.
  The row-level actions now pin **one named row** instead (`captureContextRowPin`/`restoreContextRowPin`: an entry id plus its distance from the scroll container's top edge, re-found after the rebuild through `contextWouldBeIndex`'s binary search rather than a `findIndex` over what can be the whole file). Which row follows from where the new rows are inserted, so each action grows its own side and holds the other one still: a **top** step pins the row *after* its filler group (rows appear above it — the log grows upwards, nothing below moves), a **bottom** step pins the row *before* it (the log grows downwards, nothing above moves), "… N lines" pins the row above it and grows downwards (row 0 for a leading group, which has no row above), and collapsing a run pins the row above the run. An index outside the list means there is nothing on that side to hold, and `scrollTop` is simply left alone — the same effect. Expand-all/collapse-all keep the old anchor: there is no side that should stay put there. `"more"` fillers carry a `side` field for this, and `appendContextFillers` passes its own `contextStrips` key through so a click knows which row to name.
  Verified in a real browser as well as in jsdom (the row on the far side of the step holds its exact pixel: 712 → 712 for a top step, and a bottom step leaves `scrollTop` at 0). `docs/ui-and-views.md`: a new paragraph and table under "Revealing a step at a time". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 138 (q)** — both directions on a 200-line fixture, each with the selection deliberately off screen (the exact condition that used to trigger the re-centring); confirmed to fail on 2 assertions with the pin removed. **2961 passed, 0 failed** (2950 + 11).

- **Context view: partial expansion, a marker lane that reads, and a real toolbar** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested: *"Die Bedienung des Kontext Views ist noch nicht ganz rund. Die visual cues sind noch zu unscheinbar."*). Seven asks, all of them landing on the same view:
  1. **Auto-expand is the default.** `contextInitialExpansion` now defaults to `"aroundJump"`, relabelled "Auto expand around the selected row" and moved to the top of the select. The old default `"collapsed"` opened the view as the bare filter result, which made the point ("you are still on a filter") but left it inert until you deliberately unfolded something.
  2. **New setting "Lines revealed per step"** (`contextExpandStep`, `philogg-context-expand-step`, default **10**, clamped 1–1000): the width of one expansion step *per direction*, so the default reveals 10 lines above and 10 below the row you land on.
  3. **A hidden stretch is no longer binary.** `contextExpansions` went from a `Set` of open `gap.start` values to a `Map` of `gap.start` → the revealed root-index ranges `[{from,to}, …]` inside it (sorted, disjoint, merged; three writers: `revealContextRange`, `hideContextRange`, and the seeding in `buildContextView`). That is what makes "show me a bit more" expressible at all, and it is what an auto-expansion needs, since a window around a jump target sits in the *middle* of a gap. A partially revealed stretch renders `[run] → "Show more (+n)" → "… N lines" → "Show more (+n)" → [run]`: the step row exists only on a side that already has revealed rows against it, and only while more than one step is still hidden (below that it would do exactly what the "… N lines" row does). The two ends are separately operable — growing the run above never also grows the one below. With nothing revealed a stretch is just its "… N lines" row, because clicking the match itself is what opens the first step.
  4. **The bracket on match rows is gone**, replaced by the `.ctx-anchor-dot` the `context`/`countContext` filter already draws next to its reference entries. Reusing the bracket had been a deliberate borrowing ("this is what the filter produced") but it says *these rows belong together*, which is true of a time window and false of a filter result scattered across the file.
  5. **A revealed run is now one connected control.** Every revealed row carries a full-height `border-left` (`.ctx-run-line`) so the run draws one continuous line from its first row to its last, capped by the two carets that used to be the only fold affordance. The whole line is the button: a click anywhere along it collapses the run, so a block folds back from wherever you happen to be reading it.
  6. **The indent on revealed rows is gone** and the line/dots moved into the lane the bracket used to occupy (`--ctx-lane-x`, defined once). Consequence worth having: no log text moves sideways when a run opens, and the gutter reads as one column — a dot per result, a line per revealed run.
  7. **The nav chip became `#contextToolbar`**, a real row inside the table under its header, with a separate "collapse all" next to "expand all" and every button on the app's own `.toolbar-icon-btn` shape (22px, id-scoped override, the same pattern `#btnPinBookmarks` uses). It is in flow, not overlaid: it takes `CONTEXT_TOOLBAR_HEIGHT` out of `#highlightBody`'s viewport instead of covering log lines — and `setContextToolbarVisible` compensates the scroll position by exactly that number in the same step, so the rows themselves do not slide when it appears. That compensation is scoped to a bar appearing while the panel *stays* on screen; a tab switch hides the whole panel, where there is nothing visible to cancel out. With the floating chip went **Settings → "Match navigation position"** — there is no corner left to pick.
  Two model details worth keeping in mind. `contextAutoRanges` replaces `contextAutoExpansions` as "what the last jump may take back", and it records only what the jump *actually* revealed (`contextHiddenPartsOf`), so a range already open by hand is never adopted; `hideContextRange` removes precisely one range, splitting a run that merely straddles it, so a hand-revealed stretch overlapping the jump's window survives intact. And `contextRuns` lists each contiguous revealed run **once** (binary-searched per visible row by `contextRunAt`) rather than keying a map by row — a fully expanded file would otherwise cost a map the size of the file.
  **Also fixed, exposed rather than caused by the extra group**: seven more fixed short sleeps in the suite (`await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 20))` standing in for an async IndexedDB write/read in Groups 59a/59b, 70e, 70f and the two format-level groups) were latent flakes of exactly the kind the 2026-09-01 session already converted four of — under the heavier shard load they intermittently killed a whole shard, which shows up as a *lower pass count with nothing reported as failing*, the signal `tests/README.md` tells you to trust. They now `waitFor` the state they are about to assert on, and `waitFor` itself awaits its predicate so an async one (`listFilterLibrary()`) can be passed directly instead of being mistaken for a truthy promise.
  **Two of those conversions were wrong at first, and the same short-count signal caught them**: `resetDefaultFormat` mutates the format object *before* awaiting its IndexedDB write and only re-renders *after* it resolves, so a predicate on `state.logFormats` is already satisfied while the write is still in flight — the group finished, `withApp` tore the window down, and the pending continuation crashed in `renderLevelBar` against a dead `document`, killing a whole shard on every run. The 20ms sleep had been covering that by accident. Groups 70f and 116d now wait on a genuine POST-write effect instead (the format row's own re-render — its name text in 70f, its node identity in 116d, where a level-only reset leaves the text unchanged). The other five are fine as state predicates: `saveFormatEdit`/`saveFormatRuleEdit` update `state` *after* their await, so their state and panel-visibility predicates are post-write by construction. Worth remembering for the next conversion: the barrier has to be an effect the writer produces after its await, not one it produced before. Five consecutive full runs green afterwards, all at 2950.
  Verified in a real browser (Playwright screenshots) as well as in jsdom, since most of this is paint. `docs/ui-and-views.md`: the Context view section rewritten to the current state — gap model, the new "Revealing a step at a time", filler rows, row treatment, toolbar, initial state, jumping in, the offsets bullet. `README.md`: the Context/Filtered split bullet. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 138 rewritten** for the range model (dot instead of bracket, no indent, run lines, step rows, separately operable ends, the step row disappearing once one step covers the rest, expand/collapse-all, auto-window travel), **Group 147 rewritten** to assert revealed ranges instead of open gap starts, and new **Group 151** for the toolbar (where it sits, when it shows, button shape and disabled states, the scroll compensation, and the two settings). **2950 passed, 0 failed** (2915 + 35).

- **Bugfix: arrow-key navigation now always drives the pane that is actually on screen** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-reported: *"ab und zu würde beim Navigieren der Auswahl nach oben über die Pfeiltasten … die Selektion springt Zeile für Zeile nach oben über den Rand des sichtbaren Bereichs hinaus, dann würde manchmal die Tabelle nicht mitscrollen"* — not reliably reproducible). Diagnosed first: the scroll math itself is sound. `scrollToIndex`/`scrollToHighlightIndex`'s "scroll up" branch (`rowTop < scrollEl.scrollTop` → `target = rowTop`) holds exactly against `tableRowOffsets`/`highlightRowOffsets` in every row-height mode — a fuzz over plain / multiline / notes / multiline+notes walking 60 rows up from row 200 never left the selected row outside the viewport. The bug is one level up, in **which pane the keys were moving**.
  `state.entriesView` (`"filter" | "highlight"`) decides between `moveSelection` and `moveHighlightSelection`, and it was maintained *independently* of which pane tabs layout actually shows. Row clicks, `Ctrl+1/2/3` and `revealInHighlightView` set it — but the `#fhTabs` buttons and `revealFilteredView()` (a tree click on a filter node, and every filter-creation path) switched the visible pane **without touching it**. Left out of sync, `↑`/`↓` ran against the **hidden** pane, and because `updateSelectedRowClass` syncs the `.selected` class into *both* views' rows, the selection visibly walked row by row through the pane on screen while `scrollToIndex` moved the *other* pane's scroll container — which, being `display:none`, cannot scroll at all, so that scroll was lost outright. Intermittent exactly as described: clicking any row in the visible pane re-synced it, so the same sequence often worked. The two lists line up row-for-row on an unfiltered file node, which is why the selection appeared to step one row at a time while nothing followed it.
  **Fix**: `showFhTab` pins `state.entriesView` to the tab it is showing. It is the single funnel every tabs-layout pane switch goes through (`applyFhView`'s tabs branch, the `#fhTabs` buttons, `revealFilteredView()`, `revealInHighlightView`), and in that layout only one pane exists on screen, so this is an invariant rather than a preference. **Stacked layout is deliberately exempt** — both panes are visible there, so "the one last clicked in" stays the right answer, and `Ctrl+3`'s own explicit `entriesView = "filter"` is unchanged. One ordering consequence: `applyNavWaypoint` now restores `wp.entriesView` **after** its `render()`/`applyFhView` calls instead of before them, since both can route through `showFhTab`; assigned up front it would be overwritten in the stacked case, where nothing calls `showFhTab` afterwards to put it back.
  **Also fixed, found while verifying the above**: the suite's own shard runner could report a wrong total. Both `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` and `tests/run.js` ended on `process.exit()`, but under `run.js` a child's stdout is a **pipe**, where writes are asynchronous — `process.exit()` discards whatever is still buffered, and twice in this session that swallowed a child's `##SHARD` result line, giving `2273 passed, 0 failed` instead of 2915 with nothing listed as failing. That is exactly the "a group that silently stopped running shows up as a lower count" signal `tests/README.md` tells you to trust, so it had to go. Both now set `process.exitCode` and let the event loop drain; termination is unchanged (~43s) and a forced failure still exits 1 in both sharded and single-process mode. 13 consecutive full runs green afterwards.
  `docs/ui-and-views.md`: new "Which Log view the arrow keys move through" bullet under "Tree interactions", and the back/forward "Restoring" paragraph updated for the new ordering. `tests/README.md`: the expected count and a note on why neither file may go back to `process.exit()`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 150** — the `#fhTabs` route (Full tab clicked, 20× `ArrowUp`, the visible pane scrolls and the hidden one is left alone, the selected row genuinely inside the visible pane's virtualized window), the `revealFilteredView()` route (tree click on a filter node while standing in the Context view), Stacked layout staying exempt, and a nav-history round trip; verified to fail on 7 assertions without the fix. **2915 passed, 0 failed** (2897 + 18).

- **Back/forward navigation: a real "meaningful interaction" heuristic** (this session, 2026-09-02, person-requested: *"Ich möchte die Vor- und Zurück Navigation der App verbessern … den Nutzer in Ansichten und Interaktionen zurückführen … Ich kenne so einen Ansatz aus Visual Studio oder Jetbrains Rider … Oft verliert man sich im Code und kann so nochmal 'zurückfinden' auf was man zuvor angeschaut hat"*). The existing `#btnNavBack`/`#btnNavForward` (FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #53) recorded **only** changes of the active filter node, as `{nodeId, scrollTop}`. Three consequences, all fixed here:
  1. **Scrolling was invisible to the history** — worse, `trackNavScroll` overwrote the current entry's `scrollTop` on *every* scroll event, so the spot you were reading was already destroyed by the time you scrolled away from it. Scroll is now **B1**: `NAV_DWELL_MS` (700ms) after the last *user* scroll, a waypoint is pushed iff the view travelled at least one viewport height from the current waypoint's own remembered position; otherwise that waypoint is refreshed in place. Each further scroll restarts the timer, so a fast flick to the end of a file yields one waypoint at the destination, not fifty on the way; measuring against the waypoint rather than against the burst's own start is what lets several short bursts accumulate and what keeps the origin intact while you are away from it. `onNavScroll` is wired to all four scroll containers, `#highlightBody` included (only `#tableBody`/`#extractScroll`/`#linkScroll` were tracked before).
  2. **Entry jumps were invisible** — `revealInHighlightView` (double-click/Enter), `jumpToFullLog`, `jumpToEntry`, the minimap's click-to-jump and `jumpToTailedNewest` either don't change `activeId` or only change it as a side effect. They are **A2–A6** now: each freezes the current position before the jump and pushes the destination after it. A jump that also switches nodes still yields exactly one waypoint — `isSameNavPlace()` merges it into the one `render()`'s own A1 path already made.
  3. **The waypoint was too thin.** It now carries `{ nodeId, fhLayout, fhActiveTab, entriesView, selectedId, filterAnchor, contextAnchor, rawScrollTop, contextScrollTop }`, and Back/Forward restores the whole visible state: node, Context/Filtered/Stacked mode, selected row, and both panes' scroll positions. The two anchors are `{id, offset}` pairs from the existing `captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor` — **an anchor row instead of a raw pixel offset**, because a raw `scrollTop` means something different after a tail tick, a level-filter change, a multiline toggle or a re-sort. Accepted trade-off: with nothing selected the restore is row-granular (the fallback anchor reports offset 0), so a sub-row pixel offset is not reproduced. `rawScrollTop` still covers the extraction table and link pair view, which have no entry-keyed row list.
  Explicitly **not** waypoints (tier C): programmatic scrolls (tail-follow, a jump's own scroll, a restore — recognized through the existing `lastProgrammaticScrollTs` 150ms window); the Context/Filtered/Stacked toggle and the automatic `revealFilteredView()` — the view mode is stored *in* a waypoint and restored, but switching it is not itself a step, same as a tool window in an IDE; level quick-filter, sort, multiline/notes toggles, column changes, gap expand/collapse; and **selection**, which rides along on whatever waypoint is current instead of pushing one, so an arrow-key sweep through rows produces none and B1 puts exactly one at the destination once it has scrolled far enough. `contextExpansions` is deliberately not part of a waypoint either — the restore calls `applyContextJumpExpansion()` on the context anchor, exactly as `revealInHighlightView` does, which is all the correctness it needs. Undo/Redo stays orthogonal and untouched.
  Restoring a waypoint also disengages a live tail, for the same reason picking a row does — otherwise the next tail tick would immediately undo the Back. Also: `NAV_HISTORY_LIMIT = 50` (same cap and reasoning as `UNDO_STACK_LIMIT`); the buttons carry a dynamic tooltip naming their destination ("Back to: `<node>` · `<time>`"), the way `updateUndoRedoButtons` already does; an entry whose *anchor row* is gone is no longer treated as stale (it still names a live node, and the anchor restore falls back to the view's own default) while a deleted *node* still drops out as before. Per the person's answers when the plan was put to them: no keyboard shortcut was added (`Alt+←/→` is tree navigation, `Ctrl+-` is zoom-out; `SHORTCUT_ACTIONS` is unchanged) and no history dropdown — the two buttons plus the mouse's back/forward buttons stay the whole UI.
  `docs/ui-and-views.md`: the back/forward section rewritten to the current state (tier list, waypoint shape, why an anchor row, what the restore does). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 115** extended — 115d rewritten in place (the continuous `scrollTop` tracking it asserted is exactly what was replaced), plus 115g–115m for burst coalescing, the programmatic-scroll guard, the reveal jump's origin/destination pair, selection + layout restore, the same-place merge, arrow-key navigation staying silent, a stale anchor row, and the tooltips. **2897 passed, 0 failed** (2877 + 20).

- **Filter popup: "Match whole word", and every popup toggle now uses the Settings switch** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-requested: *"Im Filter Edit Fenster ergänze die Option 'Match whole word' … Bei der Gelegenheit: Vereinheitliche die Darstellung der Checkboxes. Nutze den Style der Toogles aus den Settings auch hier."*).
  1. **`node.wholeWord: boolean`** on an ordinary `"text"` filter node, **default off** as specified — no new `filterType`, the same additive shape `caseSensitive`/`columns`/`isRegex` already use, and it combines freely with all three. `"Test"` now matches `"Test is active"` and `"unit-Test done"` (a hyphen is a boundary) but not `"Testing activated"`/`"retest pending"`.
  2. **Matching is a boundary-checked `indexOf` scan** (`includesWholeWord`/`isWordChar`), not a `\b`-wrapped `RegExp`: `\b`'s word class is ASCII-only, so it would have called `"Gr"` a whole word inside `"Größe"` — wrong for German log text, which is most of what this option is for. `WORD_CHAR_RE` is `/[\p{L}\p{N}_]/u` instead, and the scan keeps the hot filter path allocation-free. `textFilterMatches` takes it as a trailing argument and honours it in its literal branch, column-restricted or whole-line.
  3. **Literal matches only, and visibly so.** A real regex (`\b`, lookarounds) and the wildcard-token language (`[value:word]`, `[*]`) express boundaries themselves, so the flag has no definition there. Rather than let a checked box silently do nothing, `updateWholeWordAvailability()` greys the toggle out (with a tooltip saying why) the moment regex mode is on or the input carries wildcard tokens, and `commitFilter` refuses to store the flag on such a node at all — the same kind of pure capability gate `#filterExtractBtn.disabled` already was, driven off the same `"input"`/regex-`change` listeners via a shared `updateFilterAvailability()`. Match highlighting follows the same rule (`textFilterMatchSpec` carries `wholeWord`, `findMatchRanges` skips a glued occurrence), so a marked span is always one the filter accepted.
  4. **Threaded through every persistence carrier** per CLAUDE.md's "Known gotchas": `cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`, the field-edit-undo `captureNodeFields`/`applyNodeFields`, `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters`, plus `bakeNodeCondition`/`getEntriesFromBaked`/`materializeBaked` so an AND/OR/LINK side keeps the flag it was baked with.
  5. **Checkbox presentation unified**: all four booleans in `#filterPopup` (regex mode, `Aa`, "Match whole word", `NOT`) now render as the same on/off switch the Settings rows use — `.settings-switch` in a new compact `.settings-switch-sm` size, inside a shared `.filter-toggle` row that replaces the three near-identical `.filter-regex-toggle`/`.filter-case-toggle`/`.filter-invert-toggle` rules; `.filter-toggle-danger` keeps `NOT`'s red accent. The `<input type="checkbox">` underneath is untouched, only its appearance is replaced, so every `.checked`/`change` path still works as before. Switch and caption are two separate `<label>`s (nested labels are invalid HTML) with the caption carrying `for=`. The other dialogs' checkboxes (link options, plot controls, column menu, session export) are deliberately left as they were — the ask was scoped to the filter edit window.
  `docs/filters.md`: new "Text filter: match whole word" section, plus the popup section's layout/availability paragraphs updated in place. `PROJECT.md`: the `text` row of the filter-type table and `createFilterNode`'s signature. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 149** (matching semantics incl. the `"Größe"` case and hyphen boundaries, an AND over a whole-word side, highlight ranges, the popup's default/live count/greying/submit/edit-prefill behaviour, every persistence carrier, and the switch markup of all four toggles).
  **Also fixed, unrelated to the feature but exposed by it**: four fixed short sleeps in the suite (`50ms` for a folder-lazy-load and a file drop in Groups 37/38b, `20ms` for the Format Manager's IndexedDB write in 116d) were latent flakes — the extra shard load from Group 149 made them fail in a different place on each run. They now `waitFor` the state they are about to assert on, per `tests/README.md`'s own rule. Three consecutive full runs green afterwards. **2877 passed, 0 failed** (2833 + 44), and the same count against a `scripts/strip-comments.js` copy.

- **Test suite performance: 176s → ~30s, same 2833 assertions** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-requested performance review: *"Das Durchlaufen der Tests benötigt aktuell einen Großteil der Bearbeitungszeit"*). Profiled first: the suite builds ~290 jsdom windows and **78% of its runtime was window construction**, not assertions — the section-duration distribution was flat (median 0.52s, p90 1.22s), so there was no slow group to optimize. Splitting one window's ~470ms further: ~75ms HTML parse, ~48ms CSS parse, and **~350ms compiling the same 871 KB inline `<script>`** — V8 re-compiling identical source 290 times.
  1. **The page script is compiled once** (`vm.Script`) and run into each new window's VM context via `dom.getInternalVMContext()`, instead of being left inline for jsdom to re-compile per window. Per-window construction dropped ~470ms → ~135ms; the suite went **176s → 78s** with a byte-identical `2833 passed, 0 failed`. Safe here because `philogg.html` has exactly one `<script>`, it is the last element in `<body>`, and the app hooks neither `DOMContentLoaded`/`load` nor `readyState`/`document.currentScript` — so running it after the parse is equivalent to running it inline. `runScripts` deliberately stays `"dangerously"`: the ~30 test sites that inject their own bridge `<script>` elements (including `withApp`'s `window.__t`) depend on it.
  2. **The reload groups no longer poll a proxy condition** — a latent flake the speed-up exposed rather than caused. They spun `for (let i = 0; i < 40 && state.rootIds.length === 0; i++) await sleep(50)` and then asserted on `localPath`, `columnVisible`, the filter tree, bookmarks — but `restoreSessionFromCache`'s **first** pass fills `rootIds` with grayed queued placeholders, long before any of that lands. They were green only for as long as window construction stayed slow; at a faster boot two trial runs went red in two *different* places. `philogg.html` now **names its boot promise** (`const bootRestore = restoreSessionFromCache().then(... restoreWatchedFolders())`) — previously a floating promise — which the bridge exposes as `T.bootRestore`, giving all 13 reload groups one exact "restore has settled" barrier to await instead of a guess. GROUP 127 deliberately keeps polling: it asserts on the *intermediate* restore state, so it uses the new `waitFor(pred)` helper on the structural moment it actually needs. The 10 duplicated local `sleep` helpers are hoisted to one module-level definition.
  3. **`npm test` now shards across cores** via a new `tests/run.js` (~30s on 4 cores). Every `GROUP` banner carries a `group(N);` marker; `withApp` returns without building a window when that group belongs to another shard, and `run.js` sums the shards back into the single `N passed, M failed` line — so a group that silently stopped running shows up as a lower count. The shard unit is the **group**, never the individual `withApp`: multi-window reload groups hand one `IDBFactory` and closure state from window to window, and sub-lettered banners (30a-e, 55a-d, 73b/c) share their number so they stay together. `SHARDS=1 npm test` (or `npm run test:single`) runs it in one process; **`GROUP=58 npm test` runs a single group in ~2s**, which is the loop to use while iterating on a feature.
  Verified: `2833 passed, 0 failed` at every step, the shard partition is disjoint and complete (140 groups over 35/36/35/34), forced failures still aggregate across shards with exit code 1, and three consecutive sharded runs were green.
  **Rejected after measuring**: pre-stripping the HTML/CSS comments out of the shell with `scripts/strip-comments.js`. It does cut construction to ~113ms, but only ~8s end-to-end, and the suite went red in both trial runs. Not a stripper bug — the full `stripDocument(html)` that `release.yml` ships passes 2833/0 — just not worth the fidelity loss.
  `CLAUDE.md`'s "when to run the suite" rule was tightened in the same pass: it demanded a full run for **anything** under `desktop/`, but the suite never builds Tauri and never reads a file there (the wrapper groups drive `philogg.html` against faked `window.philogg`/`nativeDirHandle` bridges), so a change confined to `desktop/src-tauri/**` gets zero signal from it and needs `cd desktop && npm run build` instead.

- **Bugfix: a newly created filter is now the only selected node** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-reported: *"wenn ich einen neuen Filter anlege, ist dieser selektiert, der zuvor selektierte Filter/File aber auch noch"*). Same root cause the file-load half of this hit before (see "loading a second file left a stale multi-selected highlight", `tests/` Group 112c): a plain, non-Ctrl click on a tree row puts that row's id into `state.multiSelect` — it is not a Ctrl-only gesture — and node **creation** set `state.activeId` to the new node without dropping it. Since `.multi-selected` paints the same `--accent-soft` background `.active` does, the row the filter was built from kept looking selected right next to the new node's genuinely active row. Every creator now ends in one small `activateNewNode(node)` helper that sets `state.activeId` and clears `state.multiSelect` together: `createFilterNode`, `createAndOrNode`, `createLinkNode`, `createContextNode`, `createCountContextNode`, `createSelectionFilterNode`, `mergeFiles`' merged file node, and `pasteClipboard`'s clone. `createFileNode`/`activateQueuedFileNode` keep doing the same pairing inline — they need their `sessionRestoreInProgress` guard wrapped around it. A real Ctrl+click multi-selection is untouched; only creating a node collapses it. `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Tree interactions" gained the rule next to the single-click one it follows from. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 148** (the real UI route through the filter popup, a filter built on another filter, the context-node/selection-filter/paste creators, and a Ctrl+click multi-selection still showing two highlighted rows), verified to fail on all six assertions without the fix. 2833 passed, 0 failed.

- **Context view: "Collapsed, opened around a jump" now follows the match navigation and mouse selection** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-requested). The setting only ever reacted to a jump coming *from* the Filtered view (double-click / `Enter` → `revealInHighlightView`); the Context view's own `‹`/`›` arrows and `Ctrl+↑/↓`, added in the same rework, moved the selection without touching a single gap — and neither did clicking another match — so the "peek at this spot" reading of the setting stopped applying the moment you started working *inside* the view it was built for. Asked for: the surrounding context should travel *with* the selection — open around the match you land on, shut again behind you — unless a gap was opened or collapsed by hand, which wins.
  1. **`moveContextMatchSelection` routes through `applyContextJumpExpansion`** like every other jump does, then rebuilds (`buildContextView` + `rebuildHighlightOffsets`) and lets `selectHighlightEntry` find the row in the **new** list — the index it computed before the expansion is stale the moment the row list changes. No `captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor` pair here, unlike `setGapOpen`: centering the target is what positions this view, so there is no reading position to preserve.
  2. **`applyContextJumpExpansion` gained the "shut the previous jump's gaps" half.** It now computes the gap set this jump *wants* first, then — under `"aroundJump"` only — shuts whatever the last jump had opened and is not wanted again, before opening the rest. One open window travels along the file instead of every visited spot staying revealed.
  3. **Hand-set gaps are exempt, via a second set.** `contextAutoExpansions` holds the subset of `contextExpansions` that the jump machinery opened *itself* and nobody has touched since — the only gaps a later jump is allowed to shut. `setGapOpen` drops its gap out of it (open or collapse, either way the person now owns that gap) and `setAllGapsExpanded` clears it wholesale; a gap that is already open by hand when a jump happens to want it stays hand-owned rather than being adopted. Created, reset and dropped in lockstep with `contextExpansions`, so a node switch clears both.
  4. **Nothing changes for the other two setting values.** Under `"collapsed"`/`"expanded"` no set is tracked and nothing is ever shut: a match always has a row of its own, so the correctness half (open the gap a target is buried in) doesn't fire for the arrows at all, and they leave every gap exactly as it is.
  5. **A plain click on a match row is a jump too** (`selectContextRowByClick`) — picking another result with the mouse reads exactly like walking there with the arrows, chip readout included. With one requirement the arrows don't have: **the clicked row may not move on screen**. It is not centred; what shuts above it is a different height from what opens there, so `scrollTop` stops meaning the same thing across the rebuild — its on-screen offset is captured beforehand (`captureRowScreenOffset`) and restored against the *new* offsets array through `scrollToHighlightIndex`'s `opts.offset`, which `selectHighlightEntry` now forwards. Only real matches count (a click on a revealed context row is not a move to another result), and Shift/Ctrl clicks are routed straight to plain selection — rebuilding the list mid-gesture would move the rows out from under a multi-selection.
