Every requested interaction, for all three chart types (Line/Bar/Scatter): mouse-wheel zoom anchored at the cursor's data position (unlimited zoom-in, zoom-out clamped so it can never go wider than the current default/"home" view); middle-click-drag panning (rAF-batched, naturally a no-op at 100% zoom since there's nowhere to pan yet, clamped so it can't push past the home view's own edges once zoomed in); an editable zoom-level readout with +/- buttons and a Reset; left-click-drag rectangle zoom, multi-step (a second drag inside an already-zoomed view zooms in further), its trailing click suppressed via the same one-shot-flag pattern the timeline minimap's own drag-select already used (so it doesn't also trigger the existing mark-click "jump to log entry"); and a hover tooltip showing a mark's exact underlying value (looked up live from `extractRowsData`, not a rounded/formatted copy) — nearest-point hit-testing for line/scatter's small circle marks, rect-containment for bar. Bar charts, whose X axis is categorical (one slot per row), were generalized onto the same `xPix`-domain-mapping pipeline line/scatter already used (domain `[0, rowCount]` in row-index units) specifically so zoom/pan works there too, not just for the two continuous-axis chart types. All marks now render inside a `<g clip-path>` since a zoomed/panned view can legitimately place content outside the plot rectangle, which plain SVG doesn't clip on its own. `plotZoom` is ephemeral view state (like `plotConfig.normalize`/`xMin`), reset whenever an axis-shape-changing control fires. See "Plot zoom / pan / hover tooltip" above for the full mechanism. Regression-tested: new **Group 54** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` — zoom-out clamped exactly to the home domain, an extreme zoom-in factor collapsing the span with no floor, wheel-zoom anchoring (the data value under the cursor stays under the cursor), wheel ignored outside the plot rectangle, the zoom-level readout/buttons/typed-%/reset, panning's no-op-at-home/works-once-zoomed/edge-clamp behavior, drag-to-zoom's exact dragged-rectangle-to-data-domain mapping and multi-step re-zoom, the trailing-click suppression (and a plain click on a mark still working, unaffected), `plotZoom` resetting on an X-column change, and the tooltip's exact-value content via both hit-testing paths. 946 passed, 0 failed total.
- Plot tab: point/bar → log entry, plus an "Equal axis scale" option (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested: *"implementiere Plot-Punkt -> Log-Eintrag; zusätzlich: Plot Option für Axis-Equal"* — picking up two items from the suggestions batch added to `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` earlier the same session). (1) Every plotted mark now carries `class="plot-mark" data-row="<extractRowsData index>"`; a single delegated click listener on `#plotSvg` resolves a click back to the underlying entry and calls `jumpToFullLog` — same destructive jump the extraction table's own row double-click already uses, since the Plot tab still has no Highlight-view companion of its own. (2) `plotConfig.axisEqual` (checkbox, default off, hidden for Bar — categorical X has no meaningful pixel-per-unit ratio) keeps X and Y at the same pixel-per-data-unit ratio by symmetrically padding whichever axis' *pixel-mapping domain* is looser than the other, leaving the original "nice" tick positions untouched. See "Plotting" above for both. Regression-tested: new **Group 53** — marks carry the right `data-row`; clicking one navigates to the right file/entry with the level filter reset, clicking chart background is a no-op; axis-equal verified geometrically (before: same 0..20 data range on both axes renders at different pixels-per-unit on a 722×346 chart area; after: they match, with only the looser axis padded) and hidden for Bar. 907 passed, 0 failed total.
- `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` extended with a suggestions section (this session, 2026-08-18, person-asked: *"Hast du weitere Feature- oder Workflow-Vorschläge für das Feature Backlog?"*) — 21 unvetted proposals grouped by area (navigation & reading, filter-tree workflow, aggregation & analysis, multi-file correlation, sharing & output), each naming the gap it closes and the first open question. Doc-only change, no code touched. It also flags that the backlog's old "Sortable columns" item looks already shipped (`state.sortColumn`/`sortDir` on the Time/Level/Thread/Location headers) and should be confirmed and struck.
- Log-level filter and filter creation now work reliably while a file is still loading — feature parity with tailing (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested follow-up to the entry directly below: *"Jetzt würde ich aber gerne auch schon während des Ladevorgangs in der Lage sein, Log-Level Filter zu bedienen, neue Filter anlegen, etc. Also alles was ich auch tun könnte, wenn es sich um ein Tailing und nicht um einen Ladevorgang handeln würde."*). The entry below fixed `renderTree()` being rebuilt every load tick; `renderLoadTickMainView` still called the full `renderLevelBar()` every tick, the exact same class of bug (all four level buttons — and their click listeners — recreated from scratch, up to once per animation frame), so toggling a level filter while a file loaded suffered the identical click-loss symptom. Fixed with new `updateLevelBarCounts()` (a targeted `.cnt` text write, via a new `btn.dataset.level`), used during ticks instead. Separately, `updateLoadRowProgress` was generalized into `updateLoadRowLiveData`: it now recursively walks the whole subtree under the loading root (via `row.dataset.nodeId`, now stamped on every tree row, not just file rows), so a filter created while the file is still loading keeps showing a live, growing count (and percentage-of-parent bar) on its own row too, instead of freezing at its creation-time value — tailing's own full `render()` already gave filter children this for free, just at a cadence slow enough (1.5s) to never trigger the click-loss bug in the first place. Filter creation itself (the popup) was never actually blocked by anything load-related. See "File loading: usable while it loads, progress on the real row" above (Fix 3, new). Regression-tested: new **Group 52** — `renderLevelBar()` mocked to count calls, proving zero calls across several real parse ticks while a level button's `.cnt` still updates live and a click on the SAME held button reference still correctly toggles the filter; a filter created mid-load shown growing its own row's count (matching `getEntries()` exactly) across further ticks instead of freezing, with its percentage-of-parent bar kept live too. 893 passed, 0 failed total.
- Bugfix: a click on another row while a file loads (and stays the active view) now reliably registers — the ACTIVE-view half of the fix below (this session, 2026-08-18, person-reported follow-up: *"Während eine Datei lädt, muss ich noch immer mehrfach auf eine andere Klicken bis der Wechel erfolgt, es scheint nicht jeder Klick registriert zu werden. Nach dem Wechsel auf die bereits vorhandene Datei funktioniert aber alles flüssig..."*). The entry directly below fixed the BACKGROUND case only; `scheduleLoadRender` still called the full `render()` — `renderTree()` included — on every parse tick whenever the loading file WAS the active view, which it always is at first (a fresh load auto-activates). `renderTree()` tears down and rebuilds every row in `#tree` from scratch; doing that up to once per animation frame is exactly what made a click on some other, already-loaded row need several attempts — the same class of DOM-identity bug CLAUDE.md's "Known gotchas" already documents for `renderVisibleRows()`/native `dblclick`, just for `renderTree()`/native `click`. Fixed by splitting what a load tick touches: new `updateLoadRowProgress` (extended to also update `.tree-count`, not just the progress fill) handles the loading file's own row directly, on every tick, without ever calling `renderTree()`; new `renderLoadTickMainView` handles the rest of what the active view needs (table/minimap/level bar/status) the same way. Only genuinely structural moments — a row appearing (`createFileNode`) or disappearing (`flushLoadRender`'s cleanup) — still go through a real, full `render()`. See "File loading: usable while it loads, progress on the real row" above (rewritten). Regression-tested: new **Group 51** — `renderTree()` mocked to count calls, proving zero calls across several real parse ticks while the loading file stays the active view (its own content still genuinely live via `getVisibleEntries()`); a different row's DOM element, captured right after the one legitimate structural rebuild, staying the exact same object through every subsequent tick; and a real click dispatched on that held reference still correctly switching to it. 879 passed, 0 failed total.
- A file loading in the background no longer costs a full render — switching to a different, already-loaded file stays fully responsive (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested same-session follow-up to the entry directly below: *"Aktuell blockiert das Laden und die Animation teilweise das UI. Ich möchte eine Datei Laden und während die Animation läuft auf eine andere, bereits geladenen Datei wechseln können. Das UI soll dann sofort bedienbar sein und das andere Log in Minimap und Views anzeigen. Von dem gerade ladenden Log möchte ich dann nur noch den Fortschrittsbalken am Dateinamen sehen. Wichtig ist, dass das UI in der Zeit komplett responsive bleibt."*). The entry below made a loading file's own view live, but `scheduleLoadRender` ran a full `render()` (whole tree + table + minimap rebuild) on every parse-chunk tick regardless of whether that file was even what was on screen — so a load happening in the background, while a different already-open file was the actual active view, competed for full re-renders on every animation frame and made the UI feel sluggish exactly as reported. Fixed with `loadRenderRootIsActive(rootId)`: `scheduleLoadRender` now only does the rAF-throttled full render while its root **is** the active view; otherwise it calls new `updateLoadRowProgress(rootId)`, a direct DOM write to just that row's `.tree-load-fill` width (targeted via a new `row.dataset.nodeId` on every file row) — no `render()`, no tree/table/minimap rebuild at all. The active check is re-verified a second time inside the rAF callback itself (not just at schedule time), so a switch-away between scheduling and the next actual frame doesn't still cost one stray full render. `flushLoadRender` (start/end of a load, not the hot path) is unaffected. See "File loading: usable while it loads, progress on the real row" above (new paragraph). Regression-tested: new **Group 50** — `render()` and `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimationFrame` mocked (deterministic manual flush, since a real animation frame was found to fire well before even a 9000-line/3-chunk file finishes parsing, making real-timing races impractical to test against without slowing the suite down) to prove zero `render()` calls across several background parse chunks on a 60,000-line file while a different file stays active and fully undisturbed, the fire-time re-check correctly falling back to the cheap path, the background row's progress fill still updating directly, and exactly one full render at the load's natural completion. 871 passed, 0 failed total.
- A file is now directly usable while it's still loading, instead of only once fully parsed (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested: *"Wäre es möglich, die Datei direkt beim Ladevorgang bedienbar zu machen. Also das Log, soweit geladen direkt zu visualisieren und die Minimap fortlaufend zu befüllen... Technisch müsste das doch ähnlich sein wie eine Datei zu tailen. Nur ohne dabei runter zu scrollen, Die Filter Ansicht würde also einfach oben stehen bleiben."*). Same-session follow-up to the `.tree-loading-row` entry directly below, going further: the real file node (`createFileNode`) now exists in the tree — selectable, filterable, fully interactive — from the instant a load starts, before a byte has been read, and `parseLogTextAsync` streams completed entries onto `node.entries` chunk by chunk instead of handing them all over at the end, so the tree row's count, the Filtered/Full views, the level bar, and the timeline minimap all fill in live as a large file parses, exactly like the person described tailing. The `.tree-loading-row` placeholder is gone entirely — the progress fill (`node.loadFraction`) now rides directly on the real row via `renderNode`. No auto-scroll: `renderTable()`'s existing "start at the top" default is untouched by the load path, so the Filtered view stays put while entries stream in, unlike tail auto-follow. New `scheduleLoadRender`/`flushLoadRender` throttle the resulting re-renders onto `requestAnimationFrame` (same shape as `scrollTicking`) and — this was a real bug caught while testing, not hypothetical — explicitly call `invalidateCachesForRoots([rootId])` before each one, since a file's `entries` growing mid-parse is exactly the kind of change `node._cache`/`node._levelCounts` don't otherwise know to distrust (without it, the level bar cached `{}` forever after the very first still-empty render). See "File loading: usable while it loads, progress on the real row" above (rewritten in place, superseding the entry below's description of the mechanism). Regression-tested: Group 48b/48c rewritten in place for the new row shape (no `.tree-loading-row` left to assert against), new **Group 49** covering the actual streaming behavior — mid-parse entries/tree-count/level-counts on a 9000-line file forced across multiple `PARSE_CHUNK_LINES` chunks, correct final level-counts (the cache-staleness bugfix), and no auto-scroll. 854 passed, 0 failed total.
- Import/Export moved to a toolbar menu; pop-up loading animation replaced by an inline tree progress row (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested: *"Verschiebe die Import / Export Funktionalität hinter ein Button Menü (analog zum Open Button) in die obere rechte Ecke, nicht mehr als Kontext Menü auf einer Datei."* + *"Entferne die Pop-up Ladeanimation für Dateien, stattdessen zeige einen mini-Ladebalken direkt im Datei/Filter-Tree... Die Anwendung bleibt so lange bedienbar."*). Two independent changes. (1) Export/Import session moved from the tree's per-node context menu (plus a dedicated empty-tree-background context menu that existed only to keep "Import session…" reachable with zero files loaded) to a new **"Session…"** toolbar button+dropdown, built as a structural copy of the existing "Open…" button/`#openMenu` pattern — see "Header / toolbar layout" → `#btnSession`/`#sessionMenu` and "Session export / import" (both rewritten in place for the new placement). The empty-background context menu is gone outright, not just its two items — the toolbar button covers that case (zero files loaded) unconditionally, so the special case it existed for no longer exists. (2) The old full-screen, blocking `#progressOverlay` pop-up shown while a file loaded is gone entirely, replaced by a `.tree-loading-row` mini progress bar rendered directly in the tree (or, for a watched-folder file, swapped in for that file's grayed `.folder-watch-file` placeholder) — see new section "File loading rows (mini progress bar, no pop-up)". The app stays fully interactive while a file loads now, since nothing overlays it anymore. Regression-tested: Group 21 updated in place for the toolbar placement (negative check: node context menu no longer offers either item; toolbar button+dropdown behavior asserted instead), new **Group 48** (a: empty-tree-background menu removal + toolbar button reachable with zero files; b: loading row for a plain "Open…"/drag-drop load, including the app staying responsive mid-load; c: loading row swapping in for a folder-watch file's placeholder) — 839 passed, 0 failed total.
- Performance/sync code review (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested): audited the rendering/scroll/filter-switch pipeline end to end for jank and cross-view staleness. Most of the app was already in good shape from prior sessions — `getEntries`/`getLevelCounts` memoization, the minimap's memoized background pass, every scroll listener (table/highlight/extract/link) already rAF-batching, `computeHighlightMap` computed once per `renderMainView()` and shared, selection/undo/redo/filter-commit all correctly triggering a full re-render so no stale-view bugs were found there. Found and fixed one real gap: the three panel-resize drag handlers weren't rAF-batched like the scroll listeners were — see "Virtualized rendering: extraction table & Link pair view" → "Panel-resizer drag handlers now rAF-batch their re-render too" for the fix. Regression-tested: **Group 47** (819 passed, 0 failed total).

- Extend undo/redo: file delete, value/pattern edits, invert toggle, assertion changes (this session, 2026-08-17, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`). Group 16's original scope (filter node delete/move only) grows to cover the four items the backlog explicitly named. Two additions to the existing snapshot-based stack: (1) a new `"deleteFile"` action kind — `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo`'s old file branch (`if (node.type !== "filter") { deleteNode(nodeId); return; }`) is replaced with a real snapshot+push, reusing `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` extended with a `node.type === "file"` branch that captures/restores `entries`/`cacheKey`/`merged`/`tail` **by reference** (not cloned — same entry objects and live tail handle just get unplugged from `entryIndex`/`state.rootIds` on delete and plugged back in on undo, same as a real close/reopen); the folder-file-close branch (`node.folderId`) is checked first and unchanged, so that path is still not undoable. (2) a new generic `"edit"` action kind — `captureNodeFields`/`applyNodeFields` snapshot a node's whole set of mutable fields wholesale (no per-field inverse), with `withFieldEditUndo(nodeId, mutateFn)` capturing before/after around an arbitrary mutation; three new wrapper call sites (`updateFilterNodeWithUndo`, `updateTimeRangeFilterNodeWithUndo`, `toggleInvertWithUndo`) plus the value-assertion dialog's Save/Clear handlers calling `withFieldEditUndo` directly now replace their un-undoable predecessors at every trigger (F2/context-menu "Edit filter…", the time-range dialog including its legacy `after`/`before`→`timerange` migration, the context menu's "Invert (NOT)", and the assertion dialog). `UNDO_KIND_LABELS` centralizes the toast/tooltip label per kind (delete/file delete/move/edit) instead of the old raw `action.kind` string. See "Undo / redo" above (rewritten in place for the full current scope) and "Known limitations" below (old blanket "not undoable" line replaced with the narrower remaining gaps). Regression-tested: **Group 46** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` (812 passed, 0 failed total) — file delete/undo/redo with entryIndex and rootIds-position restoration and the filter subtree coming back with it, folder-file close confirmed to still NOT push an undo action (regression guard on branch ordering), and undo/redo round-trips for each of the four edit-kind call sites including the legacy time-filter migration; Group 16's own stale "files are out of undo scope" assertion updated in place to match.
- Theme-specific level colors for light mode (this session, 2026-08-17, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`). `--level-error/warn/info/debug/trace` and their `-soft` alpha variants used to reuse the dark-mode hex values verbatim in light mode (documented as a known limitation, now resolved) — pale colors picked for readability against the dark panel background read poorly against light mode's near-white one, `--level-info`'s pastel blue and `--level-debug`'s pale gray especially. Added a light-mode override for all ten variables inside the existing `:root[data-theme="light"]` block, darkened/saturated to roughly AA contrast (~4.5:1+) against `--bg-panel`, following the same pattern `--accent`/`--warn` already used for their own light variants (`--level-warn` reuses `--warn`'s existing light value for consistency; `--level-debug`/`--level-trace` reuse `--text-secondary`/`--text-tertiary`'s tones). Also fixed a knock-on issue this surfaced: `.level-btn.active.lvl-*`'s hardcoded dark text color assumed a light/pastel swatch behind it (true in dark mode, no longer true once light mode's swatches are themselves dark) — added a light-theme-scoped override switching that text to white. See "Theming" above. Not regression-tested — pure CSS color values with no state signature, the documented jsdom blind spot (`tests/README.md`'s "Known gaps"); full suite re-run clean (776 passed, 0 failed) to confirm no unrelated regression.
- Virtualize extraction table / Link pair view (this session, 2026-08-17, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`). Both views only ever put the scrolled-into-view window (plus a small buffer) into the DOM now, same idea as the main log table's pre-existing `renderVisibleRows` scheme: the extraction table (`renderExtractVisibleRows`) uses a fixed 28px row height and two spacer `<tr>`s (top/bottom) since it's a real `<table>` that can't be windowed via absolute positioning; the Link view (`renderLinkVisibleBlocks`) analytically precomputes each pair-block's height from its tuple size (`computeLinkBlockOffsets`/`linkBlockHeight`, since block height varies with `getTupleEntries(pair).length`) and reuses the main table's `#tableSpacer`/`#tableRows`-style absolute-positioning split directly, since it's plain `<div>`s. `extractRowsData`/`extractColumns`/`linkPairsData` always hold the FULL result regardless of what's rendered — sort, cell selection, copy/export, stats, plotting, and the minimap's full-range rectangle are all completely unaffected. The one real design problem this surfaced: Link-view pair selection used to be tracked purely as a DOM class (`.pair-selected`), which only worked because every pair always had a live DOM node before virtualization — a virtualized block can now be torn down and rebuilt between a click and any later read (scrolled away, then back), so selection moved to an index (`linkSelectedPairIndex`) with `applyLinkSelectionClasses()` re-applying the visual class to whatever's currently rendered, and `minimapMarkedEntries()` reading the index directly instead of querying `linkBody`. Both views now also reset their scroll position (and, for the Link view, the selected pair) when switching to a genuinely different node — a pre-existing latent gap invisible before virtualization, since every row/block always had a DOM node no matter the scroll position. See "Virtualized rendering: extraction table & Link pair view" above for the full mechanism. Regression-tested: new **Group 45** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` (776 passed, 0 failed total) — bounded/windowed DOM row and block counts against 300-row/50-pair results, correct spacer heights and window movement on scroll for both views, select-all still covering every logical cell rather than just the rendered subset, a pair selection surviving being scrolled fully out of view (with `minimapMarkedEntries()` still resolving it correctly and no stale `.pair-selected` left behind) and correctly reappearing when scrolled back, and scroll/selection both resetting on a genuine node switch.
- Bugfix: a transient tail-poll failure no longer permanently kills tailing (person-reported, this session, 2026-08-17, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below: *"Jetzt sehe ich tatsächlich, dass die Anzahl der Logeinträge steig, sowie eine markierung links vom Dateinamen im Tree, nach ein paar Sekunden stoppt die Aktualisierung und Visualisierung aber."*). With the reload-persistence fix below in place, tailing DID start updating and showing the live dot — but stopped again a few seconds later. Root cause: `tailTick`'s catch block treated ANY `getFile()`/read failure as permanent on the very first occurrence (`t.failed = true`, live dot gone for good) and logged nothing at all — there was no way to distinguish a transient hiccup from a genuinely permanent one, or even to see that a failure had happened, since the app has zero `console.warn`/`console.error` calls anywhere else either. The person's own suspicion that it correlated with moving the mouse was investigated and ruled out as the actual trigger — no code path connects mouse events to `tailTick` (log rows are pure-CSS `:hover`, the app's few `mousemove` listeners all early-return unless a drag is actively in progress) — but a transient read failure fits regardless of what specifically surfaces it: the most plausible cause is the log-writing process briefly holding the file locked without shared-read access while it appends the next line, an entirely ordinary condition for a log actively being written to by another program, not a bug in the writer. Fixed with a consecutive-failure counter: `t.errorCount` increments on each failure (logged via a new `console.warn`) and resets to 0 on the next clean poll; only a **streak** of `TAIL_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_ERRORS` (5, ~7.5s) failures in a row now sets `failed`, so one bad poll during a writer's brief lock window no longer kills tailing for the rest of the session, while a genuinely permanent failure (moved/deleted/permission revoked) — which fails every single poll — still reaches the threshold and stops tailing, just as before. `errorCount: 0` threaded through every site that constructs a `node.tail` object (`loadOneFileIntoTree`, `mergeScannedFiles`, `tryReattachFileTail`) for consistency, though the increment itself tolerates a missing field via `(t.errorCount || 0) + 1`. See "Tailing (live file updates)" → "Read failures are retried, not fatal on the first one." Regression-tested: new **Group 44** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` (752 passed, 0 failed total) — a sub-threshold failure streak leaving tailing/the live dot untouched with each failure logged, a clean poll resetting the streak and growth resuming normally, and a genuinely persistent failure still reaching the threshold and stopping tailing with an already-failed node correctly skipped on further ticks.
- Bugfix: tail handles now persist across a reload (person-reported, this session, 2026-08-17: *"Ich habe gerade eine Logdatei geöffnet die noch beschrieben wurde... Der Inhalt hat sich nicht fortlaufend aktualisiert... auch hat mir die Datei keinen visuellen Hinweis gegeben, dass es sich um eine 'aktive' Datei handelt."*, verified by reopening the file and seeing more entries). Root cause: FEATURE_BACKLOG.md's known gap "Persist tail handles across reload" — a file the session cache silently auto-restored on relaunch (which reads to the person as simply "opening" the file, not as a reload) never got a `node.tail` at all, so an actively-growing log neither updated live nor showed the pulsing `.tree-live` dot, and only manually re-opening the file (which the person did, and correctly noticed had more entries) re-established tailing. Fixed by persisting the file's `FileSystemFileHandle` alongside its text in the existing `files` IndexedDB store (`persistFileNode`, no `CACHE_DB_VERSION` bump — same "add a field to an existing store" precedent `folderId` set) and a new `tryReattachFileTail(node, handle)` helper called from `restoreSessionFromCache` for every restored non-folder file: `handle.queryPermission({mode:"read"})` needs no user gesture, so it either silently resumes tailing (fresh `offset` recomputed from the restored text's own length) or leaves the file a static snapshot — the exact same graceful-degradation shape `restoreWatchedFolders` already established for directory handles. Deliberately skipped for folder-owned files (`node.folderId`) — they already reattach via `restoreWatchedFolders`/`mergeScannedFiles`, and duplicating the handle there isn't needed. See "Tailing (live file updates)" (the "Survives a reload" paragraph, new this session) and "Known limitations" (the now-resolved note). Regression-tested: new **Group 43** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` (740 passed, 0 failed total) — `handle:null` persisted for an untailed file, a tailed file's handle round-tripping through real IndexedDB, the expected degrade-to-static-snapshot outcome once the round-tripped handle can't survive fake-indexeddb's strict structured clone (same documented jsdom gap as Group 38c's folder-handle test), the successful-reattach path exercised directly with a fresh working fixture handle (correct offset, live dot reappearing, tailing genuinely resuming on the next poll), and the not-silently-granted-permission path leaving a file untailed rather than throwing; Group 38c's own folder-handle fixture (a plain object with an own `getFile` function property, not structured-clone-safe) caught the initial version of this fix writing the handle unconditionally, which is what led to scoping `persistFileNode`'s new field to non-folder files.
- Extraction table: synthetic Index + t(ms) columns (person-requested, this session, 2026-08-17; described here as its current, corrected shape rather than layered deltas — see below for the correction itself). Every extraction table now leads with two always-present columns, ahead of the pattern's own: a 0-based row-order **Index** and **t (ms)** (cumulative elapsed time since the extraction's first entry, 0 on row 0) — Index doubling as the default X axis on every extraction's Plot tab, as requested. Implemented as two synthetic descriptors (`colIndex` `-2`/`-1`, so they never collide with `spec.columns`' `0..n-1`) prepended to `extractColumns`, with values stored as negative-index expandos on each row's `values` array — every existing `colIndex`-keyed mechanism (sort, assertions, stats, plot, copy/export) already worked generically off `extractColumns`/`row.values[ci]` and needed no new code; only `renderExtractTable` (prepending the descriptors, stamping the two values) and `columnColor` (fixed a negative-`i` modulo bug the new negative `colIndex`s exposed — `-1 % 6 === -1` in JS, indexing before the palette array) actually changed. Plot Y-default auto-pick was adjusted to prefer a real pattern column over the synthetic ones. **Same-session correction**: the elapsed-time column originally shipped as "ΔT," a row-to-row delta (blank on the first row) — the person immediately flagged this as wrong for plotting: *"deltaT macht so keinen Sinn für einen zeitlichen Plot. Der erste Eintrag müsste 0 haben, der Rest dann die bis dahin aufsummierten deltaT."* Correct: a per-step delta can't serve as a plot X value at all, since row N's axis position would depend on every prior row's spacing rather than its own column value. Fixed to a cumulative running total (`e.ts - entries[0].ts`, naturally `0` on row 0) and renamed "t (ms)" to match, since it's no longer a delta. See "Extraction table: synthetic Index + t(ms) columns" above (rewritten in place to describe the current, corrected shape end-to-end). Regression-tested: **Group 42** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, rewritten in place for the correction (728 passed total) — column descriptors/values (explicitly checked against the wrong per-step value)/header DOM/sorting/export/plot defaults, plus the `columnColor` fix; Groups 5, 14, and 24 updated in place where their assertions assumed the extraction table had no leading columns (sort/assert buttons now targeted by `data-sort-col`/`data-assert-col` rather than "first in the DOM"; column/button counts and copy-export text bumped by the 2 new columns).
- Filter popup: "Extract values" checkbox removed — Extract and Add filter are now two plain buttons (person-requested, this session, 2026-08-17, same day as the two entries below): *"Is there still a Checkbox for extraction? there should not be one, since the logic of extract or not is now purely based on Which button is pressed (extract or add)... it should even be possible to edit an extraction but end on add filter, which would replace the extraction with a filter. and also the other way around."* The just-shipped default-flip bugfix below patched the checkbox's *value*, but a checkbox still means a checked/unchecked *state* sits in the popup between typing and submitting — exactly the "versteckte Logik im Hintergrund" the person's original design principle ruled out. Removed `#filterExtractCheckbox` entirely; `#filterExtractBtn` ("Extract", `type="button"`) and the pre-existing `#filterSubmitBtn` ("Add filter"/"Save", `type="submit"`) both call a new shared `commitFilter(asExtract)` — the button clicked is the only thing that decides `"text"` vs. `"extract"`, evaluated fresh at the moment of the click, nothing decided or carried ahead of time. `filterExtractBtn.disabled` is the one thing still computed ahead of click (`updateExtractAvailability()`, renamed from `updateInvertAvailability()`), and it's a pure capability gate (is there a wildcard to extract at all), not an outcome decision — NOT/case-sensitivity/column-restriction are no longer conditionally disabled or hidden by pattern content either, since they're "Add filter"-only parameters that Extract simply never reads. Because `commitFilter` is genuinely shared between create and edit mode with no "preserve the old type" step, editing an *existing* extraction and clicking "Save" now replaces it with a plain filter, and editing an *existing* plain filter and clicking "Extract" now turns it into a real extraction — both directions explicitly requested, and structurally impossible to express cleanly with a single checked/unchecked toggle. `evaluateLiveMatch()` was simplified to a single branch (always previewing the "Add filter" outcome specifically), collapsing what used to be three separate extract/wildcard/plain-text branches. See "Filter popup: Extract vs. Add filter as two buttons, target chain, sectioned layout" above (rewritten in place to describe all four passes together). Regression-tested: **Group 41** rewritten in place as the current canonical coverage (704 passed total) — the checkbox's absence from the DOM, the original person-reported repro still holding under the new mechanism, extraction still reachable, and both edit-mode type-conversion directions; Groups 8, 24, 35, 39, and 40 updated in place wherever they'd relied on the checkbox.
- Bugfix: "Extract values" no longer defaults to checked (person-reported, this session, 2026-08-17, same day as the two entries below: *"wenn ich einen Filter mit wildcards erstelle z. B. über den create from Message dialog, entsteht eine extraction auch wenn ich auf Add Filter und nicht auf Extract klicke"*). `openFilterPopup()` (create mode) used to default the Extract-values checkbox to checked for every new filter — so "Filter for this ___" (which prefills a wildcard pattern but never touches the checkbox itself) let that hidden default silently decide that a plain "Add filter" click would build an extraction table. Person's explicit design principle behind the fix, not just a flipped default: *"Das Filter Edit Dialog sollte keine grundlegend unterschiedlichen Zustände kennen, sondern nur unterschied initialisiert werden. alle Zustände sind dabei im Dialog selbst visualisiert. keine versteckte Logik im Hintergrund"* — the popup has exactly ONE behavior per submit, entirely determined by what's visibly checked at that moment, never by which entry point opened it. Fix: the checkbox now always defaults to UNCHECKED in create mode, with no per-entry-point branching — both "Filter for this ___" and plain Ctrl+F go through the same `openFilterPopup()` and share the one default automatically. `openEditFilterPopup()` was deliberately left alone: initializing the checkbox from an EDITED node's actual `filterType` isn't a hidden default, it's displaying that node's real current state — exactly the "nur unterschiedlich initialisiert" the person asked for. See "Filter popup: Extract vs. Add filter as two buttons, target chain, sectioned layout" above (rewritten in place to describe all three passes together). Regression-tested (new **Group 41** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 717 passed total — the exact reported repro, the same default via hand-typed Ctrl+F, extraction still reachable by deliberately checking the box, and edit-mode's node-driven initialization in both directions; Groups 8, 24, 39, and 40 updated in place wherever they'd relied on the old checked-by-default behavior).
- Filter popup: sectioned layout + "Filter for this ___" context menu (person-requested follow-up, this session, 2026-08-17, same day as the entry directly below). Two changes. (1) The popup's layout was restructured into explicit top-to-bottom sections, each divided by a thin top border: input + wildcard chips together (`.filter-input-section`), a settings row (case-sensitive/NOT only — `.filter-settings-row`), the live pattern preview, the column-restriction ("Applies to") chips as their own section, and a footer row with the match count left-aligned and Extract values + Add filter right-aligned (Extract values immediately left of Add filter) — superseding the single-row `.filter-input-row`/`.filter-options-row` split from the entry below. Extract values itself is now styled as a real toggle button (bordered, fills with the accent color when checked) rather than a plain checkbox+label, since it moved from "options" to "actions." (2) The row context menu's "Extract numbers from this message" was renamed to **"Filter for this ___"** (`#ctxFilterForColumn`), where the blank is whichever column was actually right-clicked (`resolveContextFilterColumn`, walking up from `ev.target` to the row's `.col-time`/`.col-level`/`.col-thread`/`.col-loc`/`.col-method`/`.col-msg` span, falling back to "message"). It prefills the input from that column's text AND pre-selects that column in the "Applies to" chips; numeric content still becomes a `[value:...]` wildcard pattern (unchanged `buildNumericExtractPattern`), but a column with no numbers now falls back to its exact literal text instead of a no-op toast — and the action no longer decides text-vs-extraction itself, leaving that entirely to the popup's own Extract-values button (so the same right-click can now produce either a wildcard filter or an extraction, chosen after the fact). Also fixed in the same pass: `insertTokenAtCursor` (a token-chip click) and this new context-menu action both set `#filterInput.value` directly without ever calling `updateInvertAvailability()`, only `evaluateLiveMatch()` — meaning the Extract-values checkbox could get stuck disabled after a wildcard was inserted any way other than typing it, a real bug for the "uncheck it to get a plain wildcard filter" workflow this session's feature depends on. See "Filter popup: Extract vs. Add filter as two buttons, target chain, sectioned layout" above (rewritten in place to describe both this pass and the one below together). Regression-tested (new **Group 40** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 707 passed total; Group 39's two structural layout assertions updated in place for the superseded class names, Group 25 updated for the renamed menu item id).
- Filter popup: "Extract values" checkbox, target-chain visualization, sizing/layout fix (person-requested, this session, 2026-08-17: *"the filter edit window is too small for its content"* + three specific asks). `#filterPopup` gained `max-height:calc(100vh - 76px); overflow-y:auto` (it had no size cap before — content could render past the viewport with no way to scroll to it). New `#filterExtractCheckbox` ("Extract values") decides whether a `[value:...]`/`[*]` wildcard pattern builds an extraction table (checked, the default — old always-extract behavior, unchanged) or stays a plain `text` filter matched via the same regex shape but only tested, not captured (unchecked) — no new node field, derived purely from `filterType`+`value` so every persistence carrier already handles it. The old plain-text `"Filter on “X”:"` label was replaced with `#filterTargetChain`, reusing `#breadcrumb`'s own `.crumb`/`.crumb-sep` pill visualization. `#filterInput` now gets its own row (`.filter-input-row`), with the toggles/submit button below it in `.filter-options-row`; the `#filterHint` "Werte extrahieren: ..." row was removed (the token chips already insert those wildcards directly). See "Filter popup: Extract values checkbox, target chain, layout" above. Regression-tested (new **Group 39** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 680 passed total; Group 35 updated in place for the checkbox-gated extract transition).
- Folder watch follow-up: unified Open menu, files rendered in-folder, capability gate, session persistence (this session, 2026-08-16, person-reported feedback on the feature below, same day). Four changes: (1) **"Open files…"/"Open folder…"** collapsed into one **"Open…"** button with a dropdown (`#openMenu`, `openFilesPicker()`/`openFolderPickerFlow()`) — the old two-button layout looked inconsistent and read as more prominent than folder watch's actual (Chromium-only) browser support warranted. (2) **Opened files now render INSIDE their folder's own section, in folder order** — `renderFolderSection()` walks `folder.files` in order rendering each as either a real `.tree-row` (`renderNode`) or a grayed placeholder, instead of moving an opened file out to the plain `#tree` list the way the initial build did; `renderTree()` correspondingly skips any `node.folderId`-tagged root when building that plain list. A file's position in the folder is now visible at a glance regardless of open/closed state, and survives close (back to grayed, same spot) and reopen (real row, same spot) without ever moving. (3) **`FOLDER_WATCH_SUPPORTED = !!window.showDirectoryPicker`**, checked once upfront — replaces the initial build's silent `<input webkitdirectory>` fallback (which the person found didn't actually work as a real watch in a non-Chromium browser) with a `showCopyToast` popup notice from both entry points: the "Folder…" menu item, and a dropped folder detected via the broadly-supported `webkitGetAsEntry()` even when the newer `getAsFileSystemHandle` API is absent. No degraded folder-watch experience is offered anymore — either it's fully live, or the person is told plainly why not. (4) **Watched folders now survive a reload** — new `folders` IndexedDB store (`persistFolder`/`cacheDeleteFolder`, `CACHE_DB_VERSION` 2→3) holds each folder's directory handle as-is, relying on `FileSystemHandle`'s spec guarantee to survive structured clone; each file's own cache record gained a `folderId` field so an already-open file re-links to its restored folder by that shared, stable id (`folder.id`, now generated via `newCacheKey()` instead of a session-only `uid()`). Boot-time `restoreWatchedFolders()` runs right after the existing `restoreSessionFromCache()`, calls the no-gesture-required `queryPermission()` first (silent resume if still granted — reattaching tail handles to already-open files along the way), and falls back to a lock icon + **Reconnect** button (`reconnectFolder()`, the only place `requestPermission()` is called, since that needs an actual click) otherwise. See "Folder watch + lazy loading" above (rewritten in place to describe the current, post-follow-up shape end-to-end rather than layering deltas) for the full mechanism. Regression-tested: **Group 37** rewritten in place for the merged-view layout (622 assertions passed); new **Group 38** (this session) covers the menu's open/close/dispatch behavior (38a), the capability-gate popup from both the menu and a detected folder drop while plain file drops stay unaffected (38b), and a real two-window shared-IndexedDB round trip proving `folderId` and the folder record survive a reload plus the reconnect flow (38c, with the one part a real IndexedDB round trip can't exercise in jsdom — a working handle surviving structured clone — explicitly noted and tested at the function level instead). 652 passed, 0 failed total after this session.
- Folder watch + lazy loading (earlier the same session, 2026-08-16, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`; superseded in three of its four design choices by the follow-up entry directly above — kept here for the historical initial-build record). Compatible `*.log` files in a picked/dropped folder list grayed-out and only get read/parsed on double-click or a "Load file" context-menu entry; an inactive listing can't be filtered or copied onto since it's never a real tree node; closing a folder-loaded file returns it to the grayed listing instead of removing it — all three of these still hold exactly as built. New `state.folders` list, rendered via `renderFolderWatchList`/`renderFolderSection`/`renderInactiveFileRow` into a new `#folderWatchList` directly above `#tree`. Opening a listed file (`loadFolderFile`) reuses a new shared `loadOneFileIntoTree()` helper (factored out of `loadFileDescriptors`, which now just loops over it) so a folder-opened file goes through the exact same read/parse/persist/file-history/tailing wiring as any manually opened one, tagged `node.folderId` so `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo` can special-case its close (`closeFolderFile`) into "back to grayed" instead of "gone." New files appearing later in a live-watched folder are picked up by `folderScanTick`, polling every `FOLDER_SCAN_MS` (3s) — same handle-based shape as `tailTick`.
- Arrow-key navigation in the filter tree (this session, 2026-08-16, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`). New `state.focusRegion` ("entries" | "tree", default "entries" — the pre-existing default, so plain ArrowUp/Down-selects-a-row behavior in the log table is completely unaffected until the tree is clicked at least once) tracks which pane the plain arrow keys act on: set to `"tree"` by clicking a tree row, a breadcrumb chip, or dropping a tree drag onto a new parent; set back to `"entries"` by `applySelection`, the single choke point every entry-table selection already shares (`selectEntry`/`selectHighlightEntry`/`revealInHighlightView`). With `"tree"` focus, a new `moveTreeSelection(key)` moves `state.activeId` instead of `state.selectedId`: Up/Down step through `flattenTreeIds()` (the same depth-first, root-then-children order `renderNode` already renders in, so it matches the tree's visual top-to-bottom order exactly), Left jumps to the current node's parent, Right to its first child — there's no collapse state to toggle (every node is always expanded), so Right is simply "descend one level." Deliberately reuses the plain arrow keys rather than a modifier combo, since a click already disambiguates which pane is being interacted with, the same way clicking a table row vs. a tree row already meant something different before this feature. See "Tree interactions" above. Regression-tested (new **Group 36** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 583 passed total): the focus-region default, a real tree-row click switching focus, all four directions including the parent/first-child edges and the last/first-node clamp (no wraparound), the moved-to node also becoming the sole `multiSelect` entry (matching a plain click), and a subsequent log-row click handing focus back to `"entries"` with the pre-existing row-navigation behavior intact.
- Text filter: case-sensitive option + per-filter target column (this session, 2026-08-16, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`, bundled together as requested). New `node.caseSensitive`/`node.columns` fields on `text` filter nodes, and a matching case-sensitive checkbox (default unchecked) + one toggle chip per column (Time/Level/Thread/Location/Method/Message) in the filter popup — deliberately reusing the level filter bar's own selection logic (`state.levelFilter`/`applyLevelFilter`): none of the chips selected searches every column same as before this feature, one or more restricts to those, so "all selected" and "none selected" have the identical effect. Shared `textFilterMatches()`/`textColumnValue()` helpers back both `getEntries`'s `"text"` branch and the popup's live-match count, so they can't drift apart. Both fields are `text`-only — hidden/disabled in the popup, and stripped from the node, the moment the input is detected as an extraction pattern (extract patterns match via their own regex against `entry.message`). Threaded through all four filter-node persistence carriers (`cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`, `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters`), same pattern `node.ignoredColumns` already established. See "Text filter: case-sensitive + target column" above. Regression-tested (new **Group 35** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 567 passed total): column-key matching semantics, the all-vs-none-columns equivalence, case sensitivity, popup UI defaults/live-match/edit-mode round trip, the extract-pattern field-clearing edge case, and all four persistence carriers.
- Renamed the application from LogTrail to PhiLogg, this session, 2026-08-16 (explicit person request). `logtrail.html` → `philogg.html`, `tests/logtrail.regression.test.js` → `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`. All user-visible strings (`<title>`, `.brand-name`, file-picker labels, toast messages) and internal identifiers updated to match: `THEME_STORAGE_KEY`/`FH_LAYOUT_STORAGE_KEY` (`philogg-theme`, `philogg-fh-layout`), `CACHE_DB_NAME`/`CACHE_ENABLED_KEY` (`philogg-session-cache`, `philogg-cache-enabled`), `FILTER_FILE_FORMAT`/`SESSION_FILE_FORMAT` (`philogg-filters`, `philogg-session-export`), and the exported session filename prefix (`philogg-session-YYYY-MM-DD.json`). This was a deliberate choice over keeping the old identifiers for backward compatibility: previously saved `localStorage`/IndexedDB state (theme, layout, session cache) resets on first load after upgrading, and filter/session files exported before this change no longer import (rejected as "Not a PhiLogg filter/session file") — accepted trade-off for a consistent rename, not a bug. All references across `PROJECT.md`, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `tests/README.md` updated to match; `Examples/general.log`'s `LogTrailDemo` namespace left untouched (sample log fixture data, unrelated to the tool's own name). Regression suite passes unchanged (536 passed, 0 failed) — no behavior changed, only names.

- Minimap: full-range indicator, rendered-subset recolor, and selected-entry markers (person-reported follow-up to the viewport-indicator bugfix below, this session, 2026-08-16: *"the range indicator doesn't show the whole Filtered view's time span, only what's currently scrolled/rendered"*). The old single `#minimapViewportRect` (whatever's scrolled into view) is now two rectangles: `#minimapFullRangeRect` covers the WHOLE time span the Filtered view's result set spans, in the same look the old rect always had; `#minimapRenderedRangeRect` nests inside it, covering only the entries that actually have DOM rows right now (a real subset for the virtualized table view — `updateMinimapFullRange`/`updateMinimapRenderedRange`), one step more saturated so it reads as "specifically this part" without competing with the overlay bars. `updateMinimapFullRange` scans for the real min/max across `minimapLastViewEntries` rather than assuming the first/last array elements are the extremes — true for the table view's chronological list, NOT guaranteed for the Link view's flattened pair entries (a "before" hop's match can land earlier than its own reference — see "Link filter" below), so the same function stays correct for both callers. Also new: a position marker (`updateMinimapSelectionMarkers` — a thin line + small flag per marked entry) for the current selection, wired into `applySelection`, the single choke point `selectEntry`/`selectHighlightEntry`/`revealInHighlightView` already shared — every existing selection path gets a marker for free, no new call sites needed at each one. For the Link view specifically, clicking a pair's brace (`.pair-brace`, "Klammer") now marks BOTH real entries the pair/tuple is made of, not just one point (`minimapMarkedEntries`, reading a `data-entry-ids` attribute `renderLinkView` stashes on the brace) — which needed the minimap to actually be visible during the Link view at all; it was hidden outright before, now shown since pair entries duck-type as normal entries (ts/level) and need no special-casing to feed the density bars/full-range calc, just the flattened tuple entries instead of the pairs themselves. `minimapMarkedEntries` gates the brace-selection branch on the Link view actually being the CURRENT active view (checks `state.activeId`'s own filterType), so a `.pair-selected` left over from navigating away can't keep marking stale entries once a different node is active — `renderLinkView` only clears it by rebuilding from scratch the next time IT runs, which doesn't happen just from switching away. Bugfix caught while building this (pre-existing, unrelated to the change itself): `updateMinimapRenderedRange`'s `startIdx` was only ever clamped at 0, never at `currentViewEntries`' upper bound — switching to a much smaller filtered result while previously scrolled deep into a larger one indexed straight past the array's end and threw; both `startIdx`/`endIdx` are now clamped on both sides. See the new "Timeline minimap" section below for the full mechanism. Regression-tested (new **Group 34** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`; **Group 31** extended for the rect split): full range vs. rendered subset both visible and differing correctly for a 100-entry file scrolled to its middle, a single selection's marker appearing/moving/clearing, the minimap rendering during the Link view with the rendered-subset rect correctly staying hidden there, a brace selection marking exactly its pair's two real entries at their own timestamps, deselection clearing the markers, and the stale-brace-after-navigating-away guard.

- Bugfix: minimap "scrolled/filtered into view" indicator now covers the last shown entry's whole density bar (person-reported via screenshot, this session, 2026-08-16). `updateMinimapViewportIndicator` used to set the highlighted rectangle's edges to `minimapTsToX(entry.ts)` — the raw, continuous x position of an entry's exact timestamp — while the density bars themselves are drawn per bucket (`bucketOf()` in `renderTimelineMinimap`). Since an entry's exact moment can land anywhere within its bucket's time range, not necessarily at the bucket's right edge, the rectangle regularly stopped short of the last shown entry's own bar — visually making a row that *was* in the current view look like it sat outside the highlighted range (exactly the reported symptom: "the range indicator only runs to the start of the last included bin, it should include that bin too"). Fixed with a new `minimapBarSpan(ts)` helper mirroring `bucketOf()`'s exact bucketing formula, returning the full `[left, right)` pixel span of the bucket a timestamp falls into; `updateMinimapViewportIndicator` now uses `.left` for the first shown entry and `.right` for the last, so the rectangle always covers whole bars, matching what's actually drawn. Regression-tested (new **Group 33** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`): hand-crafted timestamps placing the last shown entry exactly 500ms into a 10,000ms-wide bucket (deterministic given the suite's stubbed 800px container width) confirm the rendered rectangle's right edge lands at the bucket's true right edge, measurably past where the old raw-position code stopped.

- Unified time filter — new `"timerange"` filterType (this session, 2026-08-16, person-reported follow-up to the drag-select entry below: *"I'd like the result as a single filter, without the two sub-filters. Alternatively, time filters could always be from/to, with the two existing variants being from/to infinity"*). Replaces the old single-bound `"after"`/`"before"` filter types with one type carrying both bounds directly: `value: { from, to }`, either side `null` meaning unbounded — so drag-select on the minimap (below) now produces exactly **one** tree node instead of three (an "after" filter, a "before" filter, and an AND node combining them). `"after"`/`"before"` stay in `FILTER_TYPES` purely so already-saved sessions/exports keep reading correctly (`getEntries` still has their branches) — nothing creates them anymore. Every creation path was switched to the unified type: drag-select, the row context-menu's "Filter after/before this row" (same UX, unified node underneath), and a new **right-click on the minimap**, which opens the filter in create mode prefilled at the clicked point. The old after/before types had **no edit UI at all**; time filters are now editable too, via a new small dialog (`#timeRangeDialog`, `openTimeRangeDialog`/`closeTimeRangeDialog`) reachable through F2 or the tree's right-click "Edit filter…" (both gate on the new `isTimeFilterType()` helper) — editing a legacy after/before node through it migrates the node to `"timerange"` in place (one-way; `updateTimeRangeFilterNode`). The dialog uses two `<input type="datetime-local" step="0.001">` fields with **empty = unbounded** on submit (`localInputValueToTs`/`tsToLocalInputValue` round-trip local time, matching `formatTime`/`parseTimestamp`'s own local-time interpretation) rather than a separate infinity checkbox — same convention the pre-existing value-assertion dialog's optional min/max range already uses — plus a small × button per field to clear it, silent swap-if-reversed on submit (matching the minimap drag's own behavior), and a rejection (visible inline error, dialog stays open) if both fields are left empty. Because the new value is still carried under each filter node's existing generic `value` field (not a new top-level field), **no persistence-carrier code needed touching** beyond adding `"timerange"` to `FILTER_TYPES` — `cloneSubtree`/`snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`/`serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`/`serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters` already copy `value` generically for every filter type (see CLAUDE.md's "Known gotchas" note on filter-node fields — this is the case where the gotcha turned out not to apply). Regression-tested (new **Group 32** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, and **Group 31** updated in place for the new single-node drag-select shape rather than left testing the superseded three-node one): legacy after/before nodes still evaluate/tag correctly, F2 migration in place, unified context-menu creation, tree right-click Edit routing for all three time-filter types, dialog validation/swap/Clear buttons, minimap right-click creation, and a `"timerange"` node's value round-tripping through the real Save/Load filter JSON path unchanged.
- Drag-select time range in minimap (this session, 2026-08-16, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`; **see the entry above — later the same session, its output was redesigned from a 3-node after+before+AND shape to a single `"timerange"` node**). Click-dragging across the timeline minimap creates a from/to time filter instead of only click-to-jump. A pixel-distance threshold (`MINIMAP_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX = 3`) tells a real drag apart from a plain click; a one-shot `minimapDragSuppressClick` flag stops the browser's trailing `click` event (fired after every mouseup, including one that ends a drag) from also being interpreted as the pre-existing click-to-jump. The drag's live overlay (a highlighted range + a floating "from → to · duration" label) is built from two plain `<div>`s (`#timelineMinimapDragRect`/`#timelineMinimapDragLabel`) positioned *outside* the minimap's `<svg>`, specifically so `renderTimelineMinimap()`'s full `innerHTML` rebuild — which can fire mid-drag on a tail tick or level toggle — doesn't wipe the in-progress selection; this also keeps the drag mechanism structurally independent of `#minimapViewportRect` (the pre-existing "scrolled into view" indicator), satisfying the backlog item's explicit caution that the two must not interfere. Regression-tested (**Group 31** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`): overlay show/hide across the full gesture, the scrolled-viewport indicator continuing to render correctly after a drag-created filter, and a plain (non-dragged) click still falling through to click-to-jump.
- Bugfix: File filter history — emptying a filter tree back to zero children now overwrites the old restore memory instead of resurrecting it (person-reported via screenshot, this session, 2026-08-14). Previously only "tree has filters" ever wrote a history record; deleting the last filter left the stale record (and the never-cleared `node.filterHistoryMatch`) sitting untouched, so the ghost preview reappeared showing the *original pre-session* filters the instant `children.length` returned to 0 — not what the person had just done, and not even nothing, which is what an intentionally-emptied tree should mean. Fixed with a per-file, session-only `node.filterHistoryTouched` flag (set the first time the tree gains a real child, at which point `filterHistoryMatch` is also cleared for good) so `buildFileHistoryRecords()` can tell "never touched this session" (leave the old record alone) apart from "touched, and now empty again" (delete the stored record, not just skip writing one). Both directions — gaining a filter, losing the last one — now overwrite whatever came before; reloading a file after all its filters were removed finds nothing to restore, exactly as requested. See "File filter history" for the full write-up. Regression-tested: Group 30c extended (in-memory match clears once the tree is populated) plus new **Group 30e** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, reproducing the exact reported scenario end-to-end across three simulated sessions sharing one `IDBFactory` — create-then-delete leaves zero stored records and no ghost preview, and a third session's reload of the same content confirms no match at all.
- Bugfix: `#viewBar` row height — round 4 (person screenshot, this session, 2026-08-14), reversing the direction of rounds 1–3. `#fhTabs` (the Full/Filtered/Stacked toggle) is the row's actual fixed height reference, not the level-filter pills/breadcrumb chips it was previously matched *to* — three earlier rounds chased padding/line-height/vertical-align parity between `.crumb` and `.level-btn` without ever comparing either against `#fhTabs` itself, missing that `#fhTabs` carries an inherent ~4px more chrome from its nested double-box structure (`.view-tabs` padding+border, wrapping `.view-tab`'s own padding). Also corrects a wrong assumption from round 2 (a flex container "ignores line-height for its own sizing" — verified false this session for a flex item that's inline text, e.g. `.level-btn`'s label). Fixed by pinning explicit, deterministic `line-height` values (replacing every remaining `line-height:normal`, which is font-metric/platform-dependent given the system-font stacks in `var(--font-ui)`/`var(--font-mono)`) on `.view-tab`/`.level-btn`/`.crumb`, plus an explicit `height:26px` on `#btnPinBookmarks` — the arithmetic now guarantees all four elements land on the same 26px total regardless of platform, verified against a real Chromium render. See "Header / toolbar layout" → "Round 4" for the full arithmetic. Regression-tested: Group 26 and Group 29b in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` updated to assert the new pinned `line-height`/`height` values (jsdom can't assert the resulting rendered pixels directly, same documented blind spot as rounds 1–3).
- Bugfix follow-up to the `#btnPinBookmarks` move below (person-reported, this session, 2026-08-14): the button still carried `.toolbar-icon-btn`'s fixed 29x29px header size after moving into `#viewBar`, visibly taller than `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar`/`#breadcrumb` next to it — all three get their height from `padding:4px` + `border:1px` + content, not a fixed box. Fixed with `#btnPinBookmarks{width:auto; height:auto; padding:4px 8px}`, scoped to the id so every header button sharing `.toolbar-icon-btn` (`#btnUndo`/`#btnRedo`/`#btnBookmarks`/`#btnShortcuts`) keeps its original 29x29px size untouched. Regression-tested (Group 29b in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`): asserts the cascaded `width`/`height`/`padding` values on `#btnPinBookmarks` and confirms all four header buttons are unaffected — jsdom has no layout engine, so this can't assert the actual rendered pixel height (see the group's own comment); real visual parity was reasoned from the box-model match to `.level-btn`/`.crumb`, not verified against a screenshot.
- File filter history (this session, 2026-08-14, person-requested follow-up to the session cache; scoped in a short design discussion first — see "File filter history" for the full writeup of the resolved open questions: no size cap, filter-tree only, tier-1/tier-2 ghost preview, tier-3 gets nothing). New third IndexedDB store `fileHistory` (`CACHE_DB_VERSION` 1→2) remembers the latest filter tree per real (non-merged) file, keyed by content fingerprint — independent of any session, so it's found again even in a brand new tab that never saw the old one. `loadFileDescriptors` looks up a match (`matchFileHistory`, reusing Session export/import's tiered fingerprint technique in the opposite direction) right after a fresh load; a hit renders as a dismissable grayed "ghost preview" under the file's still-childless tree row (`renderFilterHistoryPreview`) with a Restore button, never auto-applied. Disappears the instant the file gets any real filter child, restored or not — purely a `node.children.length` condition, no dismiss flag. No cap on stored records (reasoning: storage tracks filter-tree complexity, not log-file size, so a size-driven cap doesn't apply — see the section for the deferred time-based-expiry alternative if it's ever needed). Regression-tested (new Group 30 in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 30a/30b/30c): tier-1 persistence/restore across two windows sharing one `IDBFactory`, tier-2 matching against a grown file plus the unrelated-file negative case, and the merged-file/empty-tree exclusions plus the ghost preview's auto-hide behavior. Follow-up in the same session (closing a deliberately-noted gap): 30d re-runs the tier-1 round trip through the REAL `loadFileDescriptors` entry point — an actual `File` read via `FileReader` (jsdom supports both natively) — proving the `matchFileHistory` hook wired inside `loadFileDescriptors` itself fires correctly, not just the lower-level function called directly the way 30a-c (and every other group) do.
- Moved `#btnPinBookmarks` from the header into `#viewBar` (person-requested UI adjustment, this session, 2026-08-14; no filtering/matching logic touched). The pin-bookmarks toggle used to sit in the header next to `#btnBookmarks`; it now floats between `#fhTabs` and `#levelBar` in `#viewBar` — the row that already controls what the active views show/hide, which is what this toggle does, rather than sitting among the header's mostly popup-trigger buttons. Purely a markup move plus one new `#btnPinBookmarks{float:left; margin:0 14px 6px 0}` CSS rule (same float/margin pattern already used for `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar`) — the button keeps its existing `.toolbar-icon-btn`/`.active` styling rather than being restyled as a `.level-btn` pill, per the person's explicit request, since the two toggle in different directions (additive OR vs. the level pills' subtractive AND — see "Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View"). No JS changes needed: `updatePinBookmarksButton()`/the click handler both resolve the element by id, unaffected by its new DOM location. See "Header / toolbar layout" and "Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View". Regression-tested (new Group 29 in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`): button is now a direct child of `#viewBar` (not `.toolbar-group`), sits between `#fhTabs` and `#levelBar` in DOM order, keeps `.toolbar-icon-btn` and does *not* get `.level-btn`, floats left, stays visible independent of `#fhTabs`, and the toggle-click behavior (state flip + `.active` class) still works from its new location.
- Bugfix follow-up to "Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View" below (person-reported, this session): pinned rows weren't updating when a bookmark was added or removed — only toggling the `#btnPinBookmarks` button itself refreshed the Filtered view. Root cause: `toggleBookmark()` repainted via `renderVisibleRows()` using the *stale* `currentViewEntries`/`pinnedOnlyIds` left over from the last full render — correct before this feature existed (a bookmark change never used to affect *which* rows are shown, only a dot on an existing row), but wrong now that pin mode can add/remove rows on a bookmark change. Fixed by having `toggleBookmark()` recompute `currentViewEntries`/`pinnedOnlyIds` itself (call `getVisibleEntries()` again) whenever `state.pinBookmarksInFilteredView` is on, updating the spacer height and status strip alongside it — deliberately without routing through `renderTable()`, which would reset scroll to the top on every single bookmark toggle. See "Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View" → new "Gotcha" paragraph. Regression-tested (Group 28 in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`): row appears/disappears immediately with no intervening `render()` call, status-strip count updates, scroll position is preserved, and the pin-off case is confirmed untouched (no recompute at all).
- Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View (this session, 2026-08-14, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item 1 — person-requested, modeled on glogg/klogg's "marks"): new toolbar toggle `#btnPinBookmarks` (`state.pinBookmarksInFilteredView`); when on, `getVisibleEntries()` unions the active filter's result with the active root's bookmarked entries, re-sorted into chronological order, bypassing both the filter tree and the level filter — a pin is absolute. Deliberately built as an extension of Bookmarks (not a third marker type) and deliberately kept out of `#levelBar` (additive OR vs. the level pills' subtractive AND — see rationale in "Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View"). Rows present only via the pin get `.pinned-row` (inset left accent, not a background tint, so per-level row coloring stays visible); a bookmark that already matched the filter doesn't get this class. Status strip gains a `"· N pinned"` suffix when applicable. Scoped to the active root only (no cross-file pinning); has no effect on extract/link views since `getVisibleEntries()` is only reachable from the plain-table render path. Persisted alongside `levelFilter`/`sortColumn` in all three existing settings carriers (session cache, session export/import). Regression-tested (Group 27 in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`): merge/union correctness, level-filter bypass, chronological ordering, per-root scoping, row-class distinction between a pinned-only vs. a genuinely-matching bookmarked row, extract-mode non-interference, and persistence round-trips through both the cache and session-export paths.
- Bugfix (person-reported via a third screenshot, same day): even with matching height (previous entry), breadcrumb chips still weren't reliably top-aligned with `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar`'s floated row — `.crumb` used `vertical-align:middle`, which aligns an inline-block relative to its *parent's* font baseline/x-height, not to wherever the floats happen to start; the two only lined up by accident, dependent on inherited font metrics, so it wouldn't necessarily "stay" aligned as requested. Fixed by switching `.crumb`/`.crumb-sep` to `vertical-align:top` with `margin-top:0` — since the floats are the first content in `#viewBar` and don't push the following normal-flow content's *starting* y-position down (only narrow the available width), a float's top and `#breadcrumb`'s first line both begin flush at `#viewBar`'s content-box top structurally, not coincidentally; pinning `.crumb` there makes the alignment hold regardless of font/content. Wrapped-line spacing moved from `.crumb`'s old top+bottom margin to `margin-bottom` alone, matching the floats' own `margin-bottom`. See "Header / toolbar layout". Regression-tested: `verticalAlign === "top"` and `marginTop === "0px"` asserted for `.crumb`, plus a sanity check that `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar` also have `marginTop: 0px` (Group 26, extended) — the third round of assertions on this same row, each one catching what the previous round's numeric-parity check didn't (padding parity ⇒ still needed line-height parity ⇒ still needed alignment-reference parity).
- Bugfix (person-reported via a second screenshot, same day): the breadcrumb-vs-level-pill height mismatch from the entry below wasn't actually fixed by matching `font-size`/`padding` alone — `#breadcrumb` still carried a leftover `line-height:26px` (from before the chips were resized), silently inherited by the inline-block `.crumb`/`.crumb-sep` and inflating their own line box on top of the now-matching padding, since an inline-block's single line of text IS sized by line-height. `.level-btn` never showed the equivalent problem because it's a flex container, which ignores line-height for its own box sizing — so the mismatch was invisible on one side of the comparison and only visible on the other. Fixed by dropping the line-height from `#breadcrumb` and setting `line-height:normal` explicitly on `.crumb`/`.crumb-sep`; wrapped-line spacing now comes purely from their own margin. See "Header / toolbar layout". Regression-tested: `getComputedStyle(...).lineHeight === "normal"` asserted for both a `.crumb` and a `.level-btn` (Group 26, extended) — this is the assertion the previous fix should have had but didn't, since matching font-size/padding numerically doesn't guarantee matching rendered height across a flex vs. inline-block layout.
- Follow-up to the header/toolbar reshuffle (person-requested, same day, from a screenshot): breadcrumb chips (`.crumb`) resized to match the level filter pills (`.level-btn`) exactly — same `font-size` (10.5px) and `padding` (4px 9px) — since they'd drifted to a subtly different height/text size once the breadcrumb stopped being a flex item. Chips are also now clickable: `renderBreadcrumb()` attaches a click handler per chip that selects that ancestor node (`state.activeId`/`state.multiSelect`, then `render()`) — identical single-select behavior to a plain tree-row click, so the breadcrumb doubles as a "jump to an ancestor filter" shortcut. See "Header / toolbar layout". Regression-tested (Group 26, extended): computed-style parity between a `.crumb` and a `.level-btn`, and a click on a non-active chip updating `state.activeId`/`state.multiSelect` and the tree's active-row class.
- Bugfix (person-reported via screenshot, same day): the float-based `#viewBar` wrap-around from the entry below shipped without its own `flex:0 0 auto` — a `flex:0 1 auto` (the CSS default) flex item CAN shrink below its content's natural height, and #content's flex-shrink algorithm did exactly that whenever the window was short on vertical space, since floated children don't reliably contribute to a shrinking flex item's automatic minimum size the way normal flex children do. Result: the Full/Filtered/Stacked toggle and level-filter pills got compressed/clipped, visually overlapping the table header below instead of rendering at full height. `#statusStrip`/`#timelineMinimap` — #viewBar's siblings in the same `#content` flex column — already carried `flex:0 0 auto` for this exact reason; #viewBar just needed the same declaration back (it's a separate concern from its own `display:flow-root`, which controls how #viewBar lays out *its own* children, not how #viewBar behaves as a flex item of its parent). Regression-tested: `getComputedStyle(viewBar).flexShrink === "0"` asserted directly (Group 26, extended) — jsdom can verify the CSS property even though it can't render the actual clipping/overlap.
- Follow-up to the header/toolbar reshuffle below (person-requested, same day): `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar` no longer flex items of `#viewBar` — they're `float:left` now, so they stay pinned to the top-left line even when the breadcrumb wraps to multiple lines, instead of the whole row growing taller and centering everything within it. `#breadcrumb` changed from a flex container to a plain block with `display:inline-block` chips (`.crumb`/`.crumb-sep`), which is what lets it flow text-style around the two floats on the first line(s) and then use the full row width on any wrapped line below them — flexbox has no way to do "first line shares space, later lines full width," since a flex item's box is always one rectangle. `#viewBar` switched to `display:flow-root` so its height still reaches the floats' bottom edge even when the breadcrumb is short/empty. Keeps the panel as compact as possible when the active filter chain is long. See "Header / toolbar layout". Regression-tested via jsdom computed-style assertions (Group 26, extended) — real line-wrapping itself is outside jsdom's reach (no layout engine, same caveat as the flex-direction regression in "Testing approach"), but the float/display mechanism the wrapping depends on is asserted directly, guarding against an accidental revert to flex.
- Header/toolbar reshuffle (person-requested UI adjustments, 2026-08-13; no filtering/matching logic touched). (1) Removed the "local, single-file log viewer" subtitle from the header (`.brand-tag` gone from markup and CSS). (2) Merged the breadcrumb, level quick-filter, and Full/Filtered/Stacked toggle — previously three separate toolbar rows (`#breadcrumb`, `#levelBar`, `#fhTabBar`) — into one always-visible row, `#viewBar`: tabs, then level filter, then breadcrumb (which takes the remaining width). Only `#fhTabs` itself is hidden/shown per view state now (no files / extraction node → hidden); `#levelBar`/`#breadcrumb` stay visible and populated in every state, including extract mode, since extraction still applies the level filter and the filter chain is still useful there — see "Header / toolbar layout". (3) Moved the keyboard-shortcuts reference list out of a permanent bottom-left sidebar strip (`#shortcuts` was a direct child of `#sidebar`) into a popup (`#shortcutsPanel`) behind a new header button (`#btnShortcuts`, new `ICON_HELP`), using the exact same open/close/outside-click/Escape pattern as the pre-existing `#bookmarksPanel`. Regression-tested via jsdom (Group 26 in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`, 21 assertions incl. the extract-mode regression guard for point 2 above).
- Bugfix follow-up to the extraction preview/ignored-columns session above (person-reported): (1) "Extract numbers from this message" set `filterInput.value` directly without firing an `input` event, so the live pattern preview (and the live match count) never appeared until the person typed something themselves — fixed by calling `evaluateLiveMatch()` explicitly right after, same as `insertTokenAtCursor`/`openEditFilterPopup` already did after their own direct `.value` writes. (2) Right-click → "Edit filter…" opened `#filterPopup` but it immediately closed itself again in the same click — `treeContextMenu`'s click handler didn't `stopPropagation()`, so the click kept bubbling to `document`'s global "click outside a popup closes it" listener, which saw `ev.target` (the menu item — `closeTreeContextMenu()` only toggles a CSS class, doesn't remove it) sitting outside `#filterPopup` and closed it right back; F2 was unaffected since it's not a click event. Exactly the same class of bug as the preview-span/pattern-chip fix above, in a different spot — worth remembering for any future click handler that opens something else: it must not let the triggering click keep bubbling. Regression-tested (Group 25).
- Extraction: live pattern preview + ignored columns (from person-supplied `REQUIREMENTS-extract-preview-and-ignore.md`).
